# Changelog

All notable changes to Construct are documented here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and the project follows [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- The OpenCode orchestrator micro-prompt (native-subagent hosts) now carries a compact **few-shot tool-use example** — small local models call tools far more reliably with one worked `orchestration_policy` call shown, and it stays within the prompt word cap (bead construct-c16l).
- `construct doctor` now reports **docling document-extraction runtime** readiness, and `construct doctor --probe-local` runs an opt-in agentic-coherence probe over each registered Ollama model (COHERENT/COLLAPSED with repeat ratio) plus quantization guidance (prefer Q4_K_M+) and verified-capable model hints (bead construct-hjdk).
- `construct install --with-docling` eagerly provisions the pinned docling Python venv (uv) at install time, alongside the embedding-model warmup; otherwise docling stays lazy and provisions on first document ingest. The same lazy/eager split as other heavy resources.
- Sterile-host test harness (`tests/helpers/sterile-host-env.mjs`, bead construct-o4fd): host-config tests now isolate real user state (HOME + XDG base-dirs + `OLLAMA_MODELS`) behind a temp root with a stubbed `ollama`, and `scripts/run-tests.mjs` fingerprints the real `opencode.json`/`.claude` settings/`.codex`/Ollama model set before and after the whole suite, failing on any drift. Documented in `tests/helpers/README.md`.
- When the default OpenCode model is local (`model: "ollama/…"`), `construct sync` now disables the heavy external MCP servers (context7/github/memory/sequential-thinking) in the global `opencode.json` and keeps `construct-mcp` (bead construct-5b6u). This is the only lever that drops their ~130 tool schemas from every agent's request — including the built-in Build/Plan agents the per-agent `permission` prune does not reach. A cloud-model default restores the full surface on the next sync.
- Local-model support for OpenCode + Ollama, with an empirical investigation behind it (ADR-0032, `tests/e2e/reports/local-model-validation.md`, bead construct-k6fu). A proxy that records the exact payload reaching Ollama, plus direct `/v1` bisection, established that the reported "word salad" (`"client client … ver ver"`) had three independent causes, not one: (1) OpenCode reaches Ollama over the OpenAI-compatible `/v1` endpoint, which **does not honor `num_ctx`** — so the runtime context stays at Ollama's 4096 default no matter what `opencode.json` says; (2) the full MCP surface serializes ~133 tool schemas (~36k input tokens), and only disabling a whole MCP server reduces it (per-agent `permission`/`tools:false` do not drop the schema); (3) the collapse is **model-specific** — `qwen2.5-coder:7b` degenerates on OpenCode's agentic prompt regardless of config, while `qwen3-coder:32k` and `devstral:24b` run the identical Construct payload coherently with correct tool calls. Construct now: auto-provisions context-extended Modelfile variants (`<model>-cx<N>k` via `ollama create`, the only mechanism that sets a real window) for small tool-capable models and registers the variant in place of the raw tag (`lib/ollama/provision-context.mjs`, wired through `platforms/opencode/sync-config.mjs`, override via `CONSTRUCT_LOCAL_NUM_CTX`); ships an empirical agentic-coherence probe (`provision-context.mjs --probe`) and surfaces it at sync rather than silently registering a model that will collapse; and documents the whole setup in `docs/cookbook/run-local-models.mdx`. A reusable measurement harness lives in `tests/helpers/ollama-record-proxy.mjs` and `tests/e2e/local-model-ab.mjs`.

### Fixed
- `construct sync` no longer loads the 90MB local embedding model during config generation (bead construct-w1am). `buildLearnedPatternsBlock` (sync-only, via `resolvePromptContract`) called the async `searchObservations` from a synchronous function — the Promise was mishandled so the block always returned empty, while the un-awaited call kicked off a model load that hung process exit ~80s on a cold cache (the real cause of the `sync-contract` timeout). Replaced with the synchronous, embedding-free `listObservations`: `construct sync --dry-run` now finishes in ~0.1s with zero network calls, and learned-patterns injection actually produces output instead of silently failing.

### Changed
- On hosts with native subagent routing (OpenCode, VS Code, Cursor), the `construct` orchestrator prompt no longer injects the static 29-agent roster — it gets a tool-bound micro-prompt that resolves the chain at runtime via the `orchestration_policy` MCP tool (ADR-0032). On a small-context local model the roster alone is ~3–4k tokens and compounds the context overrun. Hosts without native routing (Claude Code, Codex) still receive the roster. The OpenCode local-model config writer (`lib/opencode-config.mjs`) now emits only boundary-surviving sampler params (`temperature`, ChatML `stop`) and drops the silently-ignored `num_ctx`/`repeat_penalty` and the never-appropriate `frequency_penalty`/`presence_penalty`. The `construct` agent's OpenCode `permission` map prunes heavy MCP tool groups (github, context7, sequential-thinking, memory) from the orchestrator.

## [1.0.24] - 2026-06-09

### Changed
- Browser automation (Playwright MCP) is now opt-in instead of a default-managed MCP (ADR-0031). `@playwright/mcp` was written into every project's Claude config but no agent prompt ever directed its use, it is redundant with `WebFetch`/`WebSearch`/`context7` for the common web-research case, and it downloads hundreds of MB of browser binaries on first use — the same footprint ADR-0024 rejected Puppeteer for. Removed `playwright` from `specialists/registry.json` and `platforms/claude/settings.template.json` `mcpServers` (the two sources project sync merges), so `construct sync`/`init` no longer install it. It remains in `lib/mcp-catalog.json` (`category: optional`, version-pinned to `@playwright/mcp@0.0.75`) and installs on demand via `construct mcp add playwright` — discoverable under `construct mcp list` "Enhancements". Because it is no longer in the registry, the global-scope prune leaves a user-added entry alone, so opt-in persists across sync. `specialists/prompts/cx-qa.md` documents the opt-in for live web-UI testing. Coverage in `tests/sync-contract.test.mjs` (project settings exclude playwright; global prune preserves an opt-in entry).

### Fixed
- The release binary (SEA) build is fixed. The esbuild bundle failed on all five platforms with three errors left by the Postgres-removal refactor: `bin/construct` still `await import()`-ed the deleted `lib/storage/migrations.mjs` and `lib/storage/backup.mjs` (lazy imports, so `npm test` never resolved them — only esbuild's static bundling did), and `@lancedb/lancedb` (a native module) was missing from the esbuild `--external` list so the bundler choked on its `.node` binary. The `construct storage migrations`/`repair-migrations` actions and the `construct backup` command — both Postgres-only and already broken at runtime — now print a clear "no longer supported" message; `release.yml` externalizes `@lancedb/lancedb` (lazy-loaded, so the binary degrades gracefully for vector features like it already does for onnxruntime/sharp). Verified by reproducing the exact release esbuild command locally (3 errors → 0).
- `InboxWatcher.poll()` now awaits the observation write it records per ingested file. `recordInboxObservation` fired `addObservation` fire-and-forget (`lib/embed/inbox.mjs`), so the LanceDB write outlived `poll()` and kept writing into `.cx/` after the caller continued — racing test teardown as `ENOTEMPTY: directory not empty, rmdir '…/.cx'` and failing `InboxWatcher`/`maxDepth` intermittently on `ubuntu/node20` and `macos/node22` (cells the squash-merge to `main` exposed; the PR's single `ubuntu/node22` cell passed). `recordInboxObservation` is now `async` and awaited, so `poll()` returns only once ingestion (including the vector write) is durable. The `InboxWatcher` tests also pin `CONSTRUCT_EMBEDDING_MODEL=hashing` (was `env: {}`, which stripped CI's global setting and fell back to the slower `local` ONNX model).
- The `session-reflect` hook's 500ms hard budget (`HARD_BUDGET_MS`, ADR-0029) is now tunable via `CONSTRUCT_REFLECT_BUDGET_MS`. The budget force-exits the hook so reflection never blocks session-end longer than 500ms, leaving an overrunning LanceDB write to the embed daemon's reconcile to backfill. Under parallel-test load a cold `createTable + mergeInsert` overruns 500ms, so the deadline killed the write mid-commit — the table was created but empty, and the `A1 end-to-end` functional test (which asserts the written observation is immediately searchable) failed in the CI `test` job. The hook keeps the 500ms production default; `tests/functional/a1-session-reflect.functional.test.mjs` sets `CONSTRUCT_REFLECT_BUDGET_MS=15000` so the cross-process write completes deterministically. Reproduced and verified in an `--platform linux/amd64` node:22 container against the full suite.
- LanceDB writes are now serialized per database directory and retried on transient conflict, fixing data loss under concurrent writes. The runtime builds a fresh `VectorClient` per write and the inbox/daemon path fans `addObservation` out across files without awaiting, so concurrent `mergeInsert`/`createTable` calls collided with LanceDB's optimistic concurrency (`Incompatible transaction … Overwrite at version N`, `Table already exists`, and orphaned-temp-file `failed to persist … No such file or directory`), silently dropping most observations. Reproduced deterministically: 12 concurrent writes left only 1–4 searchable; the CI `test` job failed on `vector-client`/`InboxWatcher` on `ubuntu/node22`. `lib/storage/vector-client.mjs` adds a module-scope per-path write queue (`serializeWrite`) and `withWriteRetry`, makes `createTable` race-safe (falls back to `openTable` on "already exists"), and routes `storeObservation`/`storeDocument` through both. Regression coverage: `tests/vector-client.test.mjs` asserts all 12 concurrent writes persist and are searchable.
- `tests/vector-client.test.mjs` now sizes its embedding fixtures to the active engine's dimension instead of hardcoding 384. The CI `test` job sets `CONSTRUCT_EMBEDDING_MODEL=hashing` (256-dim), so a 384-dim fixture stored a 256-dim table column and the query failed with `No vector column found to match the query vector dimension: 384` — failing `vector-client` deterministically on `ubuntu/node22`. The fixture now reads `getEngineDimensions()` in `beforeEach`, so it passes under both `local` (384) and `hashing` (256). (This was first misdiagnosed as an empty-`createTable` issue and a create-with-data refactor was reverted once the dimension mismatch was reproduced locally via the CI env var.)
- `construct mcp remove <id>` now also clears the entry from VS Code (`<User>/mcp.json`, top-level `servers`) and Cursor (`~/.cursor/mcp.json`, top-level `mcpServers`). Previously it cleaned only Claude/OpenCode/Codex, so an MCP that `construct sync` had written to all surfaces lingered in the two editor configs after removal (surfaced while removing the now-opt-in `playwright`). `lib/mcp-manager.mjs` gains `getVSCodeUserMcpPaths`/`getCursorMcpPath` plus a JSON-config remover; coverage in `tests/mcp-manager.test.mjs`.
- Global Claude Code sync now installs only machine-wide safety hooks (`block-no-verify`, dangerous Bash guard, config protection, edit guard, JSON validation, and secret scan) instead of the full Construct workflow hook suite. Project-local `.claude/settings.json` still receives the full hook set. This keeps `construct install --scope=user` useful for baseline protection without making SessionStart, Stop learning loops, orchestration tracking, and per-read auditing ambient in every Claude session.
- Global Claude Code sync now keeps only the low-risk `context7` documentation MCP and prunes Construct-managed project/workflow MCPs (`construct-mcp`, `memory`, `playwright`, `sequential-thinking`, `github`) from `~/.claude/settings.json`. Project-local Claude settings still receive the full MCP set. This removes the global GitHub bearer-token footgun and keeps browser automation, memory, and Construct orchestration scoped to repos that opted into Construct.
- Green CI after the LanceDB/Postgres-removal refactor (`672e171`), which left five CI jobs red on `main`. (1) `lib/strategy-store.mjs` referenced `fs.promises.stat` with no `fs` import — a `no-undef` eslint error that failed the lint job; replaced with `statSync` (the file is otherwise sync-fs throughout). (2) `lib/observation-store.mjs` instantiated `new VectorClient()` with no env, so the LanceDB store resolved from `process.cwd()` instead of the `rootDir` argument — a writer (e.g. the `session-reflect` hook subprocess) and a reader in different cwds hit different databases and search returned nothing. Added `vectorClientFor(rootDir)`, which pins `CONSTRUCT_LANCEDB_PATH` to `rootDir/.cx/lancedb` only when unset (managed installs with a configured home-global path are untouched). (3) `searchObservations` dropped the `project` field when mapping vector results, so `filters by project` assertions saw `undefined`; restored it. (4) `tests/functional/a1-session-reflect.functional.test.mjs` pinned the `hashing` embedding model only in the hook subprocess, not the searching test process (default `local`, different vector dimension → empty search); it now pins `hashing` for both and polls the cross-process read to absorb LanceDB commit-visibility latency under parallel-test load.
- Dashboard `next build` (Turbopack) failed on Linux CI with `Cannot find module '../lightningcss.linux-x64-gnu.node'`. The `672e171` lockfile carried only a metadata-less `darwin-arm64` stub for `lightningcss` and `@tailwindcss/oxide`, so `npm ci` on Linux had no native binary to install. `package-lock.json` now carries fully-resolved (URL + integrity + os/cpu) entries for every platform of both packages; no dependency versions changed.

### Removed
- Obsolete `postgres + pgvector integration` CI job and its four Postgres-era functional tests (`postgres-roundtrip`, `vector-roundtrip`, `observation-reconcile`, `hybrid-search-consolidated`) plus the `tests/functional/_lib/postgres-docker.mjs` helper. The job imported the deleted `lib/storage/migrations.mjs` and tested a storage layer removed in `672e171`; LanceDB equivalents are covered by `tests/vector-client.test.mjs`, `tests/embed/reconcile.test.mjs`, and `tests/retrieval-fusion.test.mjs`. Removed from the `ci-required` aggregator's `needs` list.

## [1.0.23] - 2026-06-08

### Changed
- `construct sync` now defaults to detected hosts instead of writing all six adapter trees, matching `construct init`'s selection model (ADR-0027 §1). With no `--hosts=`/`CONSTRUCT_SYNC_HOSTS`, sync writes adapters only for hosts found on the machine (Claude as the baseline when none are detected); hosts outside the selection are skipped and their previously Construct-managed adapter config is pruned. Set `CONSTRUCT_SYNC_HOSTS=<csv>` or `--hosts=<csv>` to force a specific set. Backward-impact: a machine that previously received all six adapter trees from a bare `construct sync` now receives only what is installed. Functional coverage pins the full host set explicitly in `tests/functional/sync-respects-scope`, `host-config-parity`, and `sync-no-double-frontmatter`.
- `construct install` now offers an opt-out for the macOS background pressure-release service (`construct-2fm8.1`). Added `--no-launch-agent` flag and an interactive consent prompt; users can now choose whether to register the login LaunchAgent.
- `lib/hooks/ci-status-check.mjs` now serves the on-disk CI status cache on the `UserPromptSubmit` synchronous path and detaches a background refresher (`scripts/refresh-ci-status.mjs`) when the cache is stale (stale-while-revalidate).
 The previous shape ran `gh run list` synchronously inside the hook on every cache miss (declared budget 4000ms, blocking `UserPromptSubmit` for the duration of a `gh` call); the new shape declares `@p95ms 200` and the critical path is bounded by a 50ms `git branch --show-current` probe. First-prompt edge case: a never-cached branch sees no status on the first prompt; the next prompt (after the detached refresher writes) sees the real conclusion. ADR-0029.
- `construct install` is now scope-explicit (ADR-0029). New flag `--scope=project|user|both`, default `project`. With no scope the install prints scope guidance and writes nothing — silent machine-state writes (`~/.construct/config.env`, `~/.construct/lib` symlink, Postgres compose, MCP merges) require `--scope=user` (or `--scope=both`). The `npm i -g @geraldmaron/construct` postinstall path likewise prints scope guidance instead of silently running `construct sync --global`; the front-door agent and global hook injection land only on explicit `construct install --scope=user`. `bin/construct install --help` reflects the new flag. Backward-impact: users who relied on the silent postinstall global sync after `npm i -g` must run `construct install --scope=user` once after upgrading; `construct doctor` surfaces the drift. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/install-scope.functional.test.mjs` (5 cases: default project no-op, explicit project, invalid scope, missing scope value, `--help` flag) and `tests/functional/postinstall-global-scope.functional.test.mjs` (2 cases: global postinstall prints guidance, skip env still works). bd `construct-7w69`.
- `construct init` no longer presents Construct-the-tool as host project content (ADR-0027 §2 conformance). The conflation — flagged when an agent surveying a freshly-init'd repo cited Construct's scaffolding as the project's own documentation — is fixed at four source sites in `lib/init-unified.mjs`: (1) AGENTS.md is no longer pre-written with a ~120-line generic Construct doctrine body (which leaked the internal `construct-6uo` bead and stale `construct init:update`/`--docs-preset` commands outside any marker block); `bd init` creates the project skeleton and the `injectIntoAgentFile` pass adds Construct's guidance as a fenced `<!-- BEGIN CONSTRUCT INTEGRATION -->` block only — symmetric with how CLAUDE.md was already produced. (2) `construct_guide.md` ("Welcome to Construct") is written to the ignored `.cx/` tree instead of the repo root, so it is never committed or surveyed as project content (its template's stale `54329-54339` ports / `54330` dashboard URL / `init`-as-per-machine framing are corrected). (3) `docs/README.md` indexes only the lanes actually scaffolded (was a hardcoded 12-lane list pointing at directories that do not exist) and drops `construct docs:verify`/`init:update` prose presented as the project's own policy. (4) a greenfield `README.md` is a neutral project skeleton, not a Construct CI-policy advertisement. `inbox/` is centrally ignored via `host-disposition.mjs` rather than carrying a committed keep-file. `lib/docs-verify.mjs` and `lib/init-update.mjs` are reconciled to the new AGENTS.md shape (marker-block presence, not five prescribed doctrine headings). Backward-repair for repos already polluted by prior versions ships as two `ask`-safety reconcile tasks: `legacy-doctrine-strip` (collapses an un-fenced AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md doctrine body to the header + marker blocks, preserving user content) and `legacy-guide-decommit` (relocates a root `construct_guide.md` into `.cx/`), surfaced by `construct doctor` and applied on explicit `construct sync --reconcile=<id>`.
- Per-turn Stop-hook tax reduced: `stop:typecheck` (120s timeout) and `stop:snapshot` (15s) are now `async: true` in `platforms/claude/settings.template.json`. Both write status for the NEXT session to read (typecheck → `~/.cx/pending-typecheck.txt`, snapshot → `.cx/context.md`), so they never needed to gate the current turn. `stop:policy-engine` correctly stays synchronous (legitimately gates on red CI / in-progress beads / drive-mode). Worst-case blocking-timeout sum on Stop dropped from 150s to 15s; observed per-turn tax goes from ~3s (policy-engine + typecheck + snapshot) to ~2s (policy-engine only).
- MCP server template versions pinned to remove per-session cold-start: `@upstash/context7-mcp@3.1.0`, `@playwright/mcp@0.0.75`, `@modelcontextprotocol/server-sequential-thinking@2025.12.18` (previously `@latest` / unspecified, which made `npx -y` resolve the registry on every cold start). Pins land in `specialists/registry.json` and propagate to every host adapter on `construct sync`.

### Fixed
- `construct init` no longer pollutes the Construct repository with host-project scaffolding (`construct-83ik`). Added `isConstructPackageRepo` guard to bypass `.gitignore` mutation, `.construct/` staging, and `beads init` when running inside the tool's own repository.
- `npm postinstall` (and the underlying `stageProjectAdapters`) now correctly updates the project's `.gitignore` to cover the newly staged adapters (`construct-f6l6`). Previously, `postinstall` staged directories like `.claude/` but left them untracked; it now appends missing Construct ignore patterns automatically.
- `construct sync` now treats an existing `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json` as evidence that OpenCode is installed, even when the `opencode` binary is not on `PATH`. The detected-only default (ADR-0027 §1) previously keyed host detection solely on the binary, so a non-PATH OpenCode install (or a CI runner without the binary) was excluded from the sync set — and the `!wants` cleanup branch could prune a config file that genuinely belonged to the user. `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` now adds `opencode` to the detected set when the canonical config file exists, so the sync writes into the existing config instead of skipping or deleting it.
- Host-detection contract tests are pinned to an explicit `CONSTRUCT_SYNC_HOSTS` set so they exercise the per-host writer logic deterministically regardless of which host binaries the runner has on `PATH` (`tests/sync-contract.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/opencode-config-path.functional.test.mjs`). Without the pin, the detected-only default wrote only the Claude baseline on a host-less CI runner, so the codex/opencode/project-agent assertions failed.

### Added
- `orchestration.chainOfThought` is now a working disclosure control for provider-executed specialist reasoning (ADR-0030), wired from the previously inert config field. `hidden` (default) requests and shows nothing; `surface` requests reasoning (Anthropic adaptive thinking with `display: summarized` on Opus 4.6+/Sonnet 4.6, the legacy `enabled`+`budget_tokens` form only on older models; OpenRouter `reasoning: {enabled:true}`, with `{exclude:true}` in hidden mode so default-reasoning models do not leak) and attaches it to each task so `construct orchestrate run`/`status`, the `orchestration_run` MCP tool, the daemon SSE snapshot, and `hostAdapterMetadata` render it; `telemetry_only` writes reasoning to the `worker.completed` run trace (`.cx/traces/*.jsonl`) but never displays it. Capture lives in `lib/orchestration/worker.mjs` (returns `{ output, reasoning, … }`); mode resolution and routing in `lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs` (`resolveChainOfThought`, same precedence as `workerBackend`). The inline backend calls no model and discloses nothing. Modes and provider mapping follow Anthropic/OpenRouter/OpenAI disclosure conventions and the OpenTelemetry GenAI telemetry pattern. Coverage in `tests/orchestration-chain-of-thought.test.mjs` (worker capture per mode + runtime gating for all three modes).
- `construct.config.json` gains an optional `$schema` field and a committed JSON Schema (`schemas/project-config.schema.json`, draft-07) so editors autocomplete, document, and validate the project config; `lib/config/schema.mjs` accepts `$schema` and the new `orchestration.chainOfThought` enum.
- Self-driving host-emulation test suite (`construct-2fm8`) proving Construct works as each host actually invokes it, fully sterile and auditable. `tests/e2e/host-suite.mjs` runs four deterministic, CI-safe layers and writes a committed audit report (`tests/e2e/reports/host-emulation-suite.md`): **Layer 1** (`tests/functional/host-mcp-emulation.functional.test.mjs`) connects to the real construct MCP server (`lib/mcp/server.mjs`) over stdio as a host does and proves skills load (matched to disk + audited in `~/.cx/skill-calls.jsonl`), templates resolve, and `orchestration_policy` routes a substantial request to a specialist chain while not over-orchestrating a typo; **Layer 2** (extends `tests/functional/host-config-parity.functional.test.mjs`) asserts `construct-mcp` is wired for every selected host + no config inlines a secret; **Layer 3** (`tests/functional/construct-not-invoked-is-inert.functional.test.mjs`) proves `CONSTRUCT_ROLES=off` makes the role machinery inert and a non-cx subagent is untouched (non-interference); **Layer 4** (`tests/e2e/lib/artifact-quality.mjs` + test) is the artifact-quality gate (template sections via `lintDocStructure` + prose-paragraph + sourced-research heuristics) that passes a good PRD and fails a flat one. The opt-in real-LLM layer drives the specialist chain to produce a PRD/evidence brief and scores it with the same gate. Config conventions validated against current (2025-2026) norms for Claude Code/Cursor/VS Code/OpenCode/Codex; adapter configs confirmed gitignored (per-machine), matching the community norm.
- End-to-end owner-review harness scaffolding under `tests/e2e/`: `runner.mjs` orchestrates three sterile scenarios (greenfield Next.js, established noisy project, research corpus) across seven tiers and writes a committed, inspectable Markdown report per scenario plus a cross-scenario summary; `lib/owner-verdict.mjs` is the shared verdict-grid renderer/serializer (Functions/Documented/Discoverable/Noise/Recommendation, with unmeasured cells rendered `?`); `lib/command-sweeper.mjs` reads `lib/cli-commands.mjs` and applies an explicit safe-invocation policy (blocking/mutating commands are help-probed, never run live) with file-diff capture; `lib/peer-comparison.mjs` holds the Tier-6 peer rows and enforces a primary-source citation (URL + access date) per claim; `lib/embed-probes.mjs` carries the Tier-7 envelope/secret assertions and per-surface probe contracts (CLI-JSON, SDK, MCP, HTTP+SSE, npx). `--skeleton` mode emits report scaffolding from source-of-truth data (verified 92 public + 15 internal commands, contract 1.1.0) with no fixtures or model calls; `tests/e2e/lib.test.mjs` pins the helpers (10 cases). Fixture execution and live tiers land per the plan's per-scenario sequencing.
- Per-hook performance budget harness + nightly gate (ADR-0029). `scripts/bench-hooks.mjs` measures wall-time p95 over N=20 runs per `lib/hooks/*.mjs`, comparing against the declared `@p95ms` header; emits a JSON report at `.cx/bench/hooks-<ISO>.json`. `tests/perf/hook-budgets.test.mjs` enforces presence of `@p95ms` on every wired hook at unit-test speed and gates the benchmark behind `CONSTRUCT_BENCH_HOOKS=1` so per-PR runs stay fast. `.github/workflows/hook-budgets.yml` runs the gate nightly (07:17 UTC) and on `workflow_dispatch`. `construct doctor bench:hooks` exposes the same lane for local probing. `scripts/refresh-ci-status.mjs` is the detached refresher for `ci-status-check`. bd `construct-7w69`.
- Footprint contract section in `README.md` and `docs/concepts/architecture.mdx`: per-scope table itemizing every path Construct writes at project (`construct init`) and machine (`construct install --scope=user`) scope, the "never touched" list (shell rcs, npm global, `git config --global`), and the per-hook `@p95ms` performance-budget contract. Mirrors ADR-0029's decision in user-facing docs so the install/init footprint is discoverable without reading the ADR. `README.md` "Getting started" example updated from `construct install --yes` to `construct install --scope=user --yes`. bd `construct-7w69`.
- ADR-0029 install scopes & per-hook performance budgets (`docs/adr/0029-install-scopes-and-hook-budgets.md`, proposed): codifies `construct install --scope=project|user|both` (default `project`) so the install verb stops silently mutating `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` / `~/.claude/settings.json` / `~/.construct/*`, and introduces the `@p95ms <N>` per-hook budget header convention plus a nightly benchmark gate (`scripts/bench-hooks.mjs`, `tests/perf/hook-budgets.test.mjs`). Defaults derive from Anthropic's primary-source budgets (UserPromptSubmit 30s ceiling) and the community ~500ms PostToolUse perceptual threshold; peer evidence cited from claude-task-master #1180 (global-config ambiguity treated as a bug) and ruflo #1530 (11 node-spawning hooks → 4× per-prompt slowdown). Extends ADR-0025's "aware, never ambient" doctrine and ADR-0027's disposition contract to machine scope. Tracking bd epic `construct-7w69`; this ADR `construct-hvns`.
- ADR-0027 host project footprint & non-destructive scaffolding (`docs/adr/0027-...`): the disposition contract (tracked / ignored / asked-before-modify), the marker-block injection pattern for files Construct does not own, the install/init/sync boundary, the uninstall reversal guarantee, and the forward-fix-AND-backward-repair principle. Tracking epic `construct-jsut` with 13 work items.
- State-reconciliation framework (`lib/reconcile/`): registry of versioned tasks with `safety: auto|ask`, an idempotent runner, and a stamp file at `~/.construct/reconcile.json`. Auto-safety tasks run silently from `construct sync`; ask-safety tasks surface via `construct doctor`. Distinct from `lib/migrations/` (artifact-JSON schema versioning) and `lib/storage/migrations.mjs` (Postgres schema). Tracking `construct-w9pp`.
- First reconciliation task `legacy-skills-cleanup` (`construct-r9u3`): removes SKILL.md files at `~/.agents/skills/` whose frontmatter lacks the `name` and `description` fields required by the Anthropic Agent Skills loader, preserving any file whose mtime drifted past its declared `updated_at` (a user-edit signal). Verified against a local machine carrying 141 stale files left by an older sync: one `construct sync` removed all 141; the second sync is a no-op. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/reconcile-legacy-skills.functional.test.mjs` (5 cases: empty machine, stale-only, mixed with foreign and current files, user-edited stale preserved, stamp file landing under overridden `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE`).
- Five ADR-0027 reconciliation tasks backfill state written before each forward-fix landed. Auto-safety (run silently from `construct sync`): `gitignore-coverage` appends missing Construct ignore patterns to a project `.gitignore` that predates the full set, under the same header the init writer uses; `agent-instructions-rewrap` refreshes a stale CONSTRUCT INTEGRATION block in an existing `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` (never creating one and never touching a file without a block); `mcp-entry-reconcile` strips Codex `[mcp_servers.<id>]` tables whose `bearer_token_env_var` names an unset env var from already-written global and project configs, preserving every other table. Ask-safety (run only on explicit `construct sync --reconcile=<id>`): `adapter-prune` removes adapter directories for hosts not installed on the machine (never `.claude` or `.github`); `postgres-namespace` rewrites a legacy singular `construct-postgres`:54329 compose to the per-home container/port form — data-safe, invoking no docker and deleting no container or volume, returning a summary that instructs the operator to run `construct down && construct up`. Each task's `detect()` is pure-read and each `apply()` is idempotent. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/reconcile-tasks.functional.test.mjs` (10 cases including idempotency for every task, ask-task exclusion from the auto runner, and a fake-docker assertion that `postgres-namespace` spawns no docker process).
- `construct sync --reconcile=<id>` runs a single reconciliation task on explicit consent (the path for ask-safety tasks), printing whether it ran and its summary, or the error plus the available-id list for an unknown id. `construct doctor` gains a reconciliation-drift section that lists every task reporting `needsRepair` with its summary and the exact `construct sync --reconcile=<id>` remediation command — ask tasks and any auto task left undone (e.g. a sync run with `--no-reconcile`) both surface there.
- OpenCode config ownership boundary (`docs/concepts/opencode-config-ownership.md`): documents which keys in the global `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json` Construct manages versus which belong to the user, and the non-destructive merge rules that keep the two from colliding. Extends ADR-0027's good-citizen doctrine from the project footprint to the user's daily-driver config. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/opencode-config-ownership.functional.test.mjs`.
- Host-disposition single source of truth (`lib/host-disposition.mjs`): the enumerated set of ignored host artifacts plus the adapter-directory list, consumed by the init `.gitignore` writer and (next) the reconciliation framework so forward-fix and backward-repair never diverge.
- `construct init --commit-bootstrap`: opt in to keeping the beads bootstrap commit; the default leaves bootstrap files uncommitted so a fresh init never mutates the host repo's commit history.
- Marker-block injector (`lib/agent-instructions/inject.mjs`): non-destructive injection of Construct's integration guidance into `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` via a versioned, hash-stamped `<!-- BEGIN CONSTRUCT INTEGRATION -->` block — content outside the markers is preserved byte-for-byte, injection is idempotent on the same hash, a version/hash bump replaces (never duplicates) the block, and the block dedups against a sibling Beads Integration block. `construct init` applies it to both files after `bd init`. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/agent-instructions-injection.functional.test.mjs`.
- `construct init` adapter selection by detected host: writes adapter sets only for hosts found on the machine; `--with-<host>` force-includes one and `--all-hosts` writes them all. Copilot (`.github/`) is opt-in only — never written by detection — so init never touches a repo's CI directory without `--with-copilot`; selecting VS Code pulls in the `.claude/` baseline (VS Code reads `.claude/agents` natively). `construct sync --project --hosts=<csv>` (or `CONSTRUCT_SYNC_HOSTS`) overrides the selection explicitly.

### Fixed
- Project Claude Code now receives the `construct-mcp` MCP server (and the full registry MCP set) like VS Code, Codex, and OpenCode (`construct-2fm8`). `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` `writeProjectClaudeSettings()` sourced project MCP servers only from the curated `platforms/claude/settings.template.json` (4 servers, no `construct-mcp`), so Claude Code was the only selected host missing the construct server that exposes `project_context`/`get_skill`/`get_template`/`orchestration_policy`/`agent_contract` — without it, the project's `construct` agent could not load skills/templates or run the specialist loop. The writer now merges the `specialists/registry.json` MCP set on top of the template seed (resolved via `buildClaudeMcpEntry`), existing/template entries winning so user edits stick. Regression guard in `tests/functional/host-config-parity.functional.test.mjs` asserts the construct MCP server (`lib/mcp/server.mjs`) is wired for every selected host, plus a secret-scan asserting no host config inlines a credential.
- `construct install` no longer prints the "Synced … front-door agent to global scope" + "Completions updated" summary twice (`construct-h8tx.7`). Install runs the global sync twice on purpose (`sync` then `sync --global`) to cover the project-detected and forced-global paths; in a non-project install cwd both resolve to the same global branch and emitted the identical summary. `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` gains a `--quiet` flag that suppresses only the closing summary line (not the work or any warning), and `lib/setup.mjs` passes it to the first call so the canonical summary prints exactly once from `sync --global`. No change to what either call writes; the misleading prior comment in `setup.mjs` (claiming plain `sync` does not write the front-door agent) is corrected. Regression guard in `tests/cli-surface.test.mjs`. Found by E2E owner-review Tier 1.
- `construct ingest` of a PDF no longer reads as an indefinite hang on first run (`construct-h8tx.11`). The intake/ingest PDF path provisions a docling Python sidecar on first use (`lib/runtime/uv-bootstrap.mjs`): install uv, create a venv, then `uv pip install docling` — a large ML-dependency download bounded by a 600 s timeout but emitting no output, so it looked frozen for minutes. The provisioner now writes staged progress to stderr ("Provisioning the docling document extractor (first run)… / Installing docling … the slow step / docling extractor ready") and distinguishes a timeout from a real failure with an actionable message (check network, or set `ingest.fallback` to `"adapter"`). stderr-only, so JSON consumers on stdout are unaffected. Correction to the original triage: the hang is docling provisioning, not the resolved Ollama model (which was planned but never called). Found by E2E owner-review Scenario C.
- `construct intake classify --json` returns a typed contract envelope on empty input instead of a bare `exit 1` with no stdout (`construct-h8tx.8`). The embedded-contract verb printed a usage string to stderr and exited when no `--text`/`--file`/stdin artifact was supplied, leaving an embedder with nothing to parse. It now emits a versioned envelope carrying `data.error.code: "missing_input"` and an actionable warning on stdout (still exit 1 so shells see failure). Regression guard in `tests/intake-cli.test.mjs` (empty input → parseable error envelope; real input → plan envelope). Found by E2E owner-review Tier 7.
- Internal commands (`internal: true`) no longer leak into bash/zsh tab completion (`construct-h8tx.9`). `lib/completions.mjs` built its candidate list from the full `CLI_COMMANDS`, so all 15 internal commands (`hook`, `seed-traces`, `migrate`, `dashboard:sync`, `init:update`, the five `lint:*`, `evaluator:rubrics`, `activation:status`, `prune`, `overrides`, `resources`) were tab-suggested despite being hidden from `--help` — a violation of the cli-commands.mjs visibility contract. The generator now shadows the imported list with a public-only filter so every completion path is public-only; a `construct sandbox prune` subcommand or a description word like "hook" is unaffected. Regression guard in `tests/cli-surface.test.mjs` (internal absent + public present, both shells). Found by E2E owner-review Tier 5.
- Postgres `NOTICE`/`INFO`/`DEBUG` chatter no longer dumps raw message objects to install/init stdout (`construct-h8tx.6`). The `postgres` (porsager) client's default `onnotice` printed the full notice object (e.g. `{ severity_local: 'NOTICE', code: '42710', message: 'extension "vector" already exists, skipping', … }`) on idempotent DDL during migrations. `lib/storage/backend.mjs` now passes an `onnotice` handler that drops informational chatter and surfaces only WARNING-and-worse to stderr. Found by E2E owner-review Tier 1.
- Markdown table cells in generated CLI docs now escape `|` (`construct-h8tx.10`). `lib/auto-docs.mjs` interpolated `cmd.description` raw, so `construct install`'s `--scope=project|user|both` broke the README/reference command-table row into phantom columns. A `tableCell()` helper escapes pipes (and flattens newlines) at both emission sites; README.md and `docs/reference/cli/*.md` regenerated. Found by E2E owner-review Tier 5.
- Generated CLI reference docs (`docs/reference/cli/*.md`) regenerated to match `lib/cli-commands.mjs` after AUTO-docs drift surfaced during E2E Scenario A validation: `construct dashboard` was missing from the core reference table entirely, and `construct install` carried the pre-ADR-0029 description ("Docker, cm/cass, config, embeddings") instead of the current `--scope=project|user|both` form. Reference tables now match the catalog source of truth.
- The project-local launcher (`templates/distribution/run.mjs`, staged as `.construct/run.mjs`) now resolves the Construct source repo's own `bin/construct` via a new `trySelfRepo()` resolver — keyed on the project `package.json` name being `@geraldmaron/construct` — inserted after `CONSTRUCT_DEV_PATH` and before the `node_modules`/npx/global chain. Without it the Construct repo's own hooks dead-ended: with `CONSTRUCT_DEV_PATH` unset, no `node_modules/@geraldmaron/construct`, and the global `construct` symlink dangling (its homebrew target removed), every hook fell through to `npx -p @geraldmaron/construct@<pin> -- construct …`, which could not expose the bin and dropped to PATH — surfacing `sh: construct: command not found` on every `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`/`UserPromptSubmit`/`Stop` hook. Two anti-proliferation guards added to `tests/distribution-bootstrap.test.mjs`: a drift guard asserting the staged `.construct/run.mjs` is byte-identical to `templates/distribution/run.mjs` (the staged copy had silently gone stale, missing the npx-resolve probe the template already carried — the exact mechanism by which template fixes fail to reach vendored launchers), and a self-repo resolution test that runs the launcher with no dev-path/global/`node_modules` and asserts `bin/construct` still answers.
- `stop-notify` no longer fires a desktop banner on turns that did no file work. The banner is now gated on `fileCount > 0 || hasWarnings`; all ledger/session bookkeeping still runs unconditionally. `stop-typecheck` now writes `unchecked` (not `pass`) when no files were queued — a stale `pass` in `~/.cx/ts-result.txt` was the root cause of bare "TS OK" banners on every no-op turn. `ts-result.txt` is now consume-cleared by `stop-notify` each turn so a result is surfaced once, not re-fired indefinitely.
- MCP `memory_add_observations` now persists to the shared Postgres `construct_observations` store, not only local JSON (`construct-5o2p`). The handler was synchronous and called the async `addObservation()` without `await`, and `lib/mcp/server.mjs` dispatched it without `await` (unlike the awaited `memory_search`/`rovo_search`), so the local-JSON write landed but the SQL INSERT was abandoned before the tool returned — the response even reported `created: [{}]` (an unresolved Promise's `.id`). The handler is now `async` and awaited end to end. Related: the SQL-store path in `lib/observation-store.mjs` no longer swallows errors silently — a reachable-but-failing backend writes a one-line warning before degrading to the local index, so it cannot masquerade as "no backend configured". Found by sterile end-to-end validation (documents and `cx_score` reached Postgres; observations did not).
- `.construct/run.mjs` launcher no longer dead-ends when the pinned version cannot be resolved (`construct-e9ur`). `tryNpx()` returned true merely because `npx` was on PATH and exited with npx's status, so a project pinned to an unpublished/yanked version (or any offline run) failed every launcher invocation — and therefore every Claude Code hook — even with a working global install present. The launcher now probes that the pinned spec resolves before handing the process to npx; on a miss it falls through to the global install, cached binary, and Docker resolvers as documented.
- `construct init` no longer writes dead `.cx/.gitkeep` / `.cx/inbox/.gitkeep` files (`construct-sevd`). Because `.cx/` is gitignored in full (ADR-0027), those keep files could never be tracked; init now creates the `.cx/inbox/` directory the intake watcher polls without the useless keep file. Applies across `lib/init.mjs`, `lib/init-unified.mjs`, and `lib/init-docs.mjs`.
- The Construct MCP server advertised `rovo_search` twice in `tools/list` (byte-identical declarations), so every client received a duplicate tool; the duplicate declaration is removed (`construct-atf7`). Tool count drops from 69 to 68.
- `construct init` generated `.gitignore` now covers every machine-specific generated artifact — the six adapter dirs (`.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, `.vscode/`, `.opencode/`), the `.construct/` launcher, `.cx/`, `.mcp.json`, `.github/prompts/`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, `config.env`, `embed.yaml`, `plan.md` — mirroring Construct's own repo `.gitignore` and idempotent per pattern. A fresh init no longer leaves adapter directories untracked.
- `construct init` produces zero unsolicited commits. `bd init` is git-native and unconditionally commits its bootstrap (`.beads/*`, `.gitignore`, agent files); init now restores the host repo's commit pointer afterward — every file preserved in the working tree, uncommitted — unless `--commit-bootstrap` is passed.
- `construct init` scaffolds a minimal `construct.config.json` (version / profile / deployment.mode) on every init so the configuration surface is discoverable via `construct config get/set`; previously written only when `--profile` was passed.
- `construct install` no longer reads or writes the current working directory: project profiling and git-hooks (`core.hooksPath`) wiring move to `construct init`, and the downstream-corpus vector seed is dropped from install. Install writes machine state only.
- Codex MCP sync omits any server whose credential env var (e.g. `GITHUB_TOKEN`) is unresolved at sync time, preventing Codex's "Environment variable … is not set" startup abort. OpenCode keeps its runtime `{env:VAR}` ref unchanged — the hosts differ because Codex hard-fails while OpenCode resolves at runtime.
- `.beads/contention-stats.json` (per-machine bd telemetry) untracked: it was committed despite being listed in `.beads/.gitignore`, dirtying the working tree on every bd command.
- Local-service identifiers are derived per home (`lib/home-namespace.mjs`): the Postgres container/port and memory port now hash from the resolved home directory, so isolated HOMEs — test sandboxes, multiple users on one machine, parallel CI — no longer collide on `construct-postgres`:54329 or memory :8765. Explicit `CONSTRUCT_PG_PORT` / `MEMORY_PORT` / `CONSTRUCT_PG_CONTAINER` overrides still win; the LaunchAgent label stays singular (a machine-level guard). `startManagedPostgres` now returns a database URL whose port reflects the target home rather than a fixed constant.
- OpenCode global-config hygiene emitted by `syncOpencode` (`scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`): the `github` MCP `Authorization` header is now an env ref (`Bearer {env:GITHUB_TOKEN}`) instead of a resolved plaintext token (ADR-0027 problem #9 / `construct-n6h7`); the OpenRouter `HTTP-Referer`/`X-Title` attribution headers carry real values instead of unsubstituted `__OPENROUTER_REFERER__`/`__OPENROUTER_TITLE__` placeholders (reconciled to the verified origin remote across all 7 occurrences); the orchestrator `bash` permission is scoped via `opencodePermissions` to deny `rm -rf *` and prompt on `git push`/force/`reset --hard`; and `small_model` is seeded non-destructively (only when absent) as a cost lever. User-personal keys (`model`, `share`, `autoupdate`, user agents, user OpenRouter models, `tui.json`) are preserved on merge.
- `construct uninstall` now reverses the four pieces of install/init state ADR-0027 §Consequences
  enumerates and the empirical isolation test had to clean up by hand. New reversers in
  `lib/uninstall/uninstall.mjs`: the `dev.construct.pressure-release` LaunchAgent
  (`launchctl bootout` + unload, then plist removal; darwin-only, matching install); a Construct-set
  git `core.hooksPath` (unset only when it equals `.beads/hooks`, never a user-customized value); the
  memory MCP registration stripped from `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`,
  and `~/.codex/config.toml` (preserving every other entry, including the legacy `cass` OpenCode key);
  and opt-in removal of the shared `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` Docker image behind a new `--with-images`
  flag (the image stays by default since other projects may share it). Each reverser is
  detect-before-remove and idempotent. Test: `tests/functional/uninstall-coverage.functional.test.mjs`.
- Dashboard static export is reproducible and no longer churns the working tree. `apps/dashboard/next.config.mjs` pins `generateBuildId` to a version-derived value — the random per-build `buildId` (`_next/static/<buildId>/`) was the sole source of churn; every chunk is already content-hashed and stable. The built tree is treated as a build artifact: `lib/server/static/index.html` is untracked (the directory is gitignored), so a local `next build` no longer dirties git. `npm pack` still ships the full on-disk tree via the `files` allowlist; `construct dashboard:sync --build` regenerates it and `dashboard:sync --check` verifies `apps/dashboard/out` ↔ `lib/server/static` alignment.
- **Document export** (markdown → PDF / DOCX / HTML) via Pandoc + Typst, per ADR-0024. New module `lib/document-export.mjs` (`detect(format)` + `exportMarkdown({inputPath, outputPath, format})`); new CLI command `construct export <markdown> --to=<pdf|docx|html> [--output=<path>] [--detect]`; new MCP tool `document_export` (with `detect_only` for availability checks). Both engines are optional system binaries discovered at runtime — when absent, the operation returns a structured `{ok:false, missing:[...], message:"Install pandoc..."}` instead of crashing. ADR-0024 promoted Proposed → Accepted. Functional test `tests/functional/document-export.functional.test.mjs` covers detect, bad-format, missing-input, missing-binary, and the happy path (via a stubbed pandoc on a tmpdir PATH so CI runners without pandoc still exercise the spawn contract).
- Live-LLM tests (`tests/functional/llm/*.functional.test.mjs`) move off the per-PR critical path. They are true integration tests of specialist prompts against real OpenRouter behavior — never in `ci-required`'s gate list, dependent on a paid `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` secret, and silently skipping when the key is absent. The `live-llm-tests` workflow job now runs on the weekly schedule (Sun 09:00 UTC) plus `workflow_dispatch` only; the silent skip is replaced with a fail-loud "OPENROUTER_API_KEY is required" so a manual run that forgets the secret reports an honest error instead of a green-but-empty pass. Per-PR CI loses a paid-key dependency without losing real gate coverage.
- ADR-0025 (`docs/adr/0025-explicit-activation-model.md`) promoted Proposed → Accepted. The decision (explicit, host-initiated activation via `orchestration_run`; never ambient) is locked in; the implementation half landed via the `orchestration_run` MCP tool and the ACP server, both with functional tests asserting no silent single-persona fallback.
- **ADR-0028** (`docs/adr/0028-js-yaml-frontmatter-exception.md`, Accepted): `js-yaml` is sanctioned as an ADR-0001 exception scoped narrowly to markdown frontmatter parse/emit (three callsites: the skills validator + two migration scripts). The dependency moves from `PENDING_ADR` to `SANCTIONED` in `tests/core-dependency-policy.test.mjs`; the row leaves the pending map. Hand-rolling YAML emit with proper quoting and `noRefs` discipline would consume hundreds of LOC for a non-protocol concern. `node-webvtt` is removed from `package.json` entirely (zero in-tree usage) and dropped from the pending map.
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` documents safe multi-branch integration practice (no `-X ours` on protected branches; keep integration branches off `staging`; gate entry to `staging` via the eight-check gate, not post-hoc cleanup). Captures the audit finding behind the v1.0.17-prep CI-infra breakages.
- Inbox watcher only records a dropped file's dedup-manifest SHA when the intake packet is actually created. Previously the SHA was recorded whether or not `prepareIntakeForIngestedFile` returned a packet id; a silent packet-creation failure left the file stranded — explicit `construct intake process` reported "Processed: 0, Skipped (unchanged): 1" while `construct intake list` was empty, with no path back. The watcher now gates `recordFile` on a non-null `intakeId` and surfaces the failure ("ingest succeeded but intake-packet creation failed; rerun `construct intake process` to retry") to stderr so the user knows where the signal went. `InboxWatcher` gains a `prepareIntakeFn` constructor option (test seam).
- `construct knowledge search` from a foreign project now surfaces the project's own `.cx/knowledge/**` — including the `external/research/` tree written by `construct knowledge add --source=research` — alongside (and ahead of) the bundled Construct docs. Previously `knowledgeSearch` built its source set from the Construct repo only, so a freshly added project research file was invisible while Construct's own concepts pages dominated the results. Hits now carry `origin: 'project' | 'bundled'` so callers can distinguish provenance. `gitignore-coverage` reconcile additionally detects when it is running inside the Construct package repo itself (the repo intentionally omits `.construct/` from `.gitignore` to keep `.construct/version` tracked for distribution) and skips, so `construct sync` no longer mutates the repo's own `.gitignore`. Cosmetic: `specialists/prompts/cx-researcher.md` deduplicated the second `## Output format` heading (renamed to `## Evidence-brief format`).

## [1.0.22] - 2026-06-04

### Fixed
- Tool-invisibility linter hardening (audit `construct-l9sk`): the `cx-*` leak detector used an open-ended
  `/cx-[a-z-]+/` regex that false-positived on unrelated npm packages (`cx-oracle`, `cx-ray`, `cx-pro`) and
  would fail the release gate on legitimate user content. The pattern is now anchored to the 28 real role ids
  from `specialists/registry.json` (exported `KNOWN_CX_ROLE_IDS`, drift-guarded by a registry-comparison test).
  Fence handling fixed alongside: `~~~` fences are now skipped, and an unclosed fence no longer suppresses
  scanning of the rest of the document — the leak backstop fails closed, not open.
  `tests/tool-invisibility.test.mjs` grows to 16 cases (+5).
- construct-mcp MCP server identity (`construct-dwfv`): the server reported a hardcoded stale `version: "1.0.0"`
  and advertised no `instructions` or resources, so MCP hosts (Claude Code, VS Code, OpenCode, Cursor) rendered
  the "Construct" entry as a bare name + OK badge with an empty detail panel. It now sources the real version
  from `lib/version.mjs`, returns a descriptive `instructions` string, and exposes a `construct://status`
  resource (version, deployment mode, broker, capabilities). Also fixed `getInstalledVersion()` being used as a
  string when it returns an object. Test: `tests/mcp-server-identity.test.mjs`; tools unchanged (65).

### Changed
- Research-grade remediation Phase C — adequate templates raised to expert-grade (epic `construct-7zrh.7`):
  `runbook.md` (severity→response/error-budget mapping atop the diagnostic flowchart), `strategy.md`
  (per-bet kill criteria + leading indicators, leading/lagging metrics, milestones, risk register),
  `signal-brief.md` (Admiralty-graded evidence + counter-signal), `prfaq.md` (spokesperson/customer quote,
  launch metrics), `customer-profile.md` (evidence-graded pain points), `product-intelligence-report.md` and
  `backlog-proposal.md` (Admiralty-graded evidence base). `one-pager`, `persona-artifact`, and the above are
  now structure-enforced by `STRUCTURE_REQUIREMENTS`.

### Added
- **ADR-0026** (`docs/adr/0026-beads-git-native-sync.md`, Accepted) — beads issue history now syncs
  git-native via the existing origin (Dolt data on `refs/dolt/data`), not a hosted data SaaS. Resolves the
  "issues stranded on one machine" gap; verified end-to-end (`bd dolt remote add origin <git-url>` +
  `bd dolt push` → `refs/dolt/data` on origin, branches untouched). DoltHub/DoltLab/filesystem remotes
  rejected for the touch-free, vendor-agnostic, local-first reasons in the ADR (research brief
  `.cx/research/dolt-sync-options-for-beads.md`). Also sets `export.git-add: false` in
  `.beads/config.yaml`: beads' per-write auto-export was trying to `git add` the gitignored
  `issues.jsonl`, logging `auto-export: git add failed` on every mutation — disabling the staging
  step keeps the local export and removes the noise (verified: no warning on `bd create`/`bd delete`).
- Architecture decisions for the document-I/O and activation initiative (research brief
  `.cx/research/doc-io-and-invocation-research.md`): **ADR-0024** (`docs/adr/0024-document-io-optional-capability.md`)
  records that document I/O is an optional, externally-bound capability — Docling for ingestion (sidecar),
  Pandoc + Typst spawned binaries for export — never bundled in core, honoring ADR-0001/0014. **ADR-0025**
  (`docs/adr/0025-explicit-activation-model.md`) records that Construct activation is explicit and
  host-initiated via the `orchestration_run` MCP tool (aware but never ambient; selecting a non-Construct
  agent never invokes Construct), closing the Copilot honor-system gap (`construct-f66u`). Both status
  Proposed; refs `construct-yrdd`, `construct-6zqs`, `construct-i1mt`.
- Tool-invisibility guardrail (`rules/common/tool-invisibility.md`) — deliverable artifacts are about the
  user's project, never about Construct or its internal machinery. Caught after a strategy authored through
  the specialist chain came back saying "Construct's bet is…" and named `cx-product-manager` as a metric
  owner — the tooling had written itself into a third-party project's deliverable. **Prevention:** a new
  `sharedGuidance` entry (reaches every specialist) + a persona directive instruct agents never to name
  Construct, the `cx-*` role ids, or internal orchestration mechanics in artifact content unless the subject
  project is Construct itself. **Backstop:** `lib/comment-lint.mjs` flags `cx-*` role-id tokens in a consuming
  project's deliverable markdown (full-content scan so a leak in a table cell is caught; HTML comments exempt
  so provenance stays allowed), skipped on the Construct repo itself (package `@geraldmaron/construct`).
  Severity follows `CONSTRUCT_ARTIFACT_LINT_MODE` (warn by default, block in the release gate). Covered by
  `tests/tool-invisibility.test.mjs`; registered in `specialists/policy-inventory.json`. (Detector later
  hardened — see Fixed/`construct-l9sk` — to anchor the pattern to real role ids and fail closed on fences.)
- Orchestrator dispatch-guard hook (`construct-8ahq`) — `lib/hooks/orchestration-dispatch-guard.mjs` is a new
  Claude-only PreToolUse/PostToolUse backstop that blocks substantial deliverable writes when
  `orchestration_policy` classified the request as `orchestrated` but no specialist dispatch
  (`orchestration_run` / Task) has run. Isolation-tested at `tests/hooks/orchestration-dispatch-guard.test.mjs`
  (12 cases: PostToolUse arm/disarm, PreToolUse block/allow, fail-open conditions). The persona now
  *mandates* calling `orchestration_policy` MCP first ("do not classify from memory; when orchestrated, you
  may not author the deliverable yourself") so non-Claude surfaces honor the same contract via instruction.
- Prompt anti-restatement test gates (`construct-dgxj`, `construct-hpbp`) — `tests/prompt-surface.test.mjs`
  now enforces (1) no skill or specialist enumerates ≥4 `INTENT_CLASSES` inline, (2) no prompt outside the
  persona enumerates ≥3 `detectRiskFlags` keys, (3) every backticked MCP tool name in `sharedGuidance`
  resolves in `lib/mcp/server.mjs` (catches renames), (4) every specialist prompt sits at ≤90% of the
  1200-word cap, (5) no prompt restates fence JSON (the renderer reads from `specialists/role-manifests.json`),
  (6) `renderRoleFrameworkSection` produces a manifest-backed block for onboarded personas and the empty
  string for unonboarded ones. Each gate fails with an actionable message naming the code authority.
- `renderRoleFrameworkSection` in `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` (`construct-hpbp`) — emits the
  `## When invoked via the role framework` section from `specialists/role-manifests.json` (events,
  `fence.allowedPaths`, `fence.allowedBdLabels`, `fence.approvalRequired`, `handoffCandidates`,
  `outputs.docTypes`). The inline section was stripped from 28 specialist prompts; the manifest is now the
  sole source of truth, so a fence change ripples through every surface on next sync without touching prose.
- Eight new artifact templates under `templates/docs/` (`construct-iqod`, `construct-h7wz`) —
  `code-review-report`, `security-audit-report`, `qa-report`, `debug-investigation`, `verdict`,
  `accessibility-audit`, `architecture-review`, `task-packet`. Each carries the canonical section list its
  owning specialist used to restate inline; prompts now reference `get_template("<name>")` and trust the
  template for structure. `lib/template-registry.mjs` is the owner→template map and is gate-enforced —
  every wired pair, template file, and `claudeTools` grant is asserted at test time.
- Neurodivergent-output rule (`rules/common/neurodivergent-output.md`) — every human-facing Construct output
  (terminal prose, agent messages, dashboard, error wording) is formatted for neurodivergent readers without
  ever reshaping machine-readable output. `lib/term-format.mjs` is the single tested presentation-layer
  module (color enable/disable, palette completeness, width fallback/cap, wrap, ANSI strip); the rule's
  `enforced_by` linkage in the decision registry resolves to a new `tests/term-format.test.mjs` (9 cases,
  including no-color.org compliance and palette key-set parity).
- Dashboard accessibility pass completing the neurodivergent-output rule's dashboard surface — keyboard focus
  is now visible on every interactive element (`:focus-visible` ring with a theme-aware `--focus-ring` token,
  WCAG 2.4.7); a skip-to-content link is the first tab stop; the sidebar is a labelled `<nav>` landmark and the
  icon-only controls (theme toggle, density toggle, repo link, search) carry `aria-label`s; the async
  "Loading…" state is a polite live region (`role="status"`) and the status-pill color dot is marked
  decorative so its text label carries the meaning; and a `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block
  honors the OS setting on first paint, independent of the in-app motion toggle. Source:
  `apps/dashboard/app/theme.css` + `apps/dashboard/components/{app-shell-client,page}.tsx`; the served
  `lib/server/static/` snapshot is rebuilt via `construct dashboard:sync --build` and gated by `--check`.
- ACP (Agent Client Protocol) server — Construct is now a first-class native agent in ACP editors (Zed,
  JetBrains, the VS Code ACP client) via `construct acp` (ADR-0023, GH #215). Speaks JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio
  (`initialize` / `session/new` / `session/prompt` / `session/cancel`); a prompt runs a real multi-specialist
  orchestration through the same engine the daemon and MCP tool use, streaming progress as `session/update`
  chunks and returning a terminal `stopReason`. New `lib/acp/server.mjs` (dependency-free, reuses
  `planRun`/`executeRun` + the run event bus). Test: `tests/functional/acp-server.functional.test.mjs`.
- Honest host capability model — `detectHostCapabilities()` (`lib/host-capabilities.mjs`) now tags each host
  with a normalized `capability`: `full-native` (Claude Code, OpenCode run the chain themselves),
  `mcp-orchestrated` (Codex, VS Code, Cursor, Copilot reach the same outcome via the `orchestration_run` MCP
  tool + daemon), or `prompt-only` (no supported 2026 host). Surfaced in `construct hosts` and
  `connect-your-editor`, and the stale notes ("VS Code is not a multi-agent runtime", "Copilot does not expose
  dispatch") are replaced with how to actually run a chain on each host. No surface is described as functional
  when it isn't.
- Sterile cross-host config-parity gate — `tests/functional/host-config-parity.functional.test.mjs` spawns
  the real sync into an isolated HOME + project and asserts every IDE surface lands at its **canonical path
  with the host's canonical top-level key and entry shape** (VS Code `servers`, Cursor `mcpServers`, OpenCode
  `mcp` with array `command`, Codex `[mcp_servers.*]`, Claude `mcpServers`), and that **global scope never
  seeds a host config the user didn't already have**. Catches the "looks installed but the host ignores it"
  class (the OpenCode and VS Code bugs below) before it reaches users. Schema sourced from each host's official
  docs + the host binaries' own resolvers (2026-06-03); hosts are never executed.

### Fixed
- Orchestrator mis-routed a Terraform agent-strategy request to Immediate (solo-authored) instead of
  Orchestrated (`construct-8ahq`). Three layers fixed: (1) `detectRiskFlags` in `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`
  was missing infrastructure-as-code and credential vocabulary — added `terraform`, `infrastructure`, `iac`,
  `provisioning`, `blast radius`, `rollout`, `deployment strategy` to `architecture`; added `credential`,
  `oidc`, `iam`, `access token` to `security`; added `tfstate`, `drift` to `dataIntegrity`. A new regression
  test (`tests/orchestration-policy.test.mjs`) pins that the exact shape ("design our terraform agent
  strategy with blast radius controls, OIDC credential handling, and a phased production rollout") classifies
  as `orchestrated` and pulls cx-architect + cx-security + cx-devil-advocate. (2) The persona now mandates
  calling `orchestration_policy` MCP rather than classifying from memory, on every surface (Claude, OpenCode,
  Copilot, Codex). (3) The new dispatch-guard hook (above) is the Claude-only backstop against solo-authoring.
- Agent permissions audit across surfaces (`construct-iel1`) — the `construct` agent had **no internet on
  Codex**: `sandbox_mode = workspace-write` blocks the network by default, yet the persona tells the agent to
  use `WebFetch`/`context7`. Sync now writes `[sandbox_workspace_write] network_access = true` (non-destructive
  — skipped when the user already manages that table). On **OpenCode**, `construct` had `webfetch` but not
  `websearch` (added `websearch: allow` to the registry), and `opencodePermissions` defaulted *every* specialist
  to `edit: allow` — so review-only specialists (`canEdit:false`, e.g. cx-architect) could modify files; they
  now map to `edit: deny`, matching Claude and the registry contract. Claude (WebSearch+WebFetch) and the
  MCP/host-controlled editor surfaces were reviewed as already appropriate.
- Pre-push gate false-refused any push command containing a `.claude/` path (`construct-7f4q`) —
  `lib/hooks/pre-push-gate.mjs` tested the whole command string with `/\bclaude\//`, so a `git push` chained
  with a `.claude/agents` path was blocked as a "claude/* branch." It now matches the agent-branch prefix
  against the branch name(s) actually pushed (current branch + explicit refspecs), anchored at the start.
  Tested in isolation. Also broadened to `cursor|copilot|codex|aider|devin` prefixes.
- VS Code/Cursor parity flagged the `memory` MCP server as drift — sync writes the optional local cm bridge,
  but parity excluded `memory` from the *expected* set, so a populated `mcp.json` reported `drift — extra:
  memory`. `memory` is now treated as allowed-but-not-required (filtered from both sides of the diff), and an
  empty/0-byte user `mcp.json` is treated as "not configured" rather than a hard `unreadable` failure
  (`lib/parity.mjs`).
- VS Code/Copilot agent picker showed every agent in triplicate (`construct-j6vv`) — Copilot agent mode reads
  the project's `.claude/agents/*.md` natively, so a briefly-added `.github/agents/*.agent.md` writer duplicated
  the whole set in the picker. That writer is removed (and a pre-existing `.github/agents/` is swept on sync);
  agents come from the single `.claude/agents/` set VS Code already reads. The orchestrator itself stayed
  doubled because global sync wrote `~/.claude/agents/construct.md` (the Claude front door) **and** VS Code reads
  that user scope too — so the global Claude front-door **agent** is no longer written (Claude global keeps its
  hooks + `CLAUDE.md`; the Codex/Copilot front doors stay since those hosts don't cross-read a sibling's agent
  dir). The project orchestrator is the single front door; `@construct` in Claude Code now requires an
  initialized project. Parity expects an empty global `~/.claude/agents/`, and a leftover `construct.md` there is
  treated as a sweepable legacy state. Touches `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`, `lib/parity.mjs`; legacy `cx-*` in
  global scope clear with `construct sync --global`.
- `construct init` output ND-UX cleanup (`construct-0uga`) — `bd init` ran with inherited stdio, leaking beads'
  verbose output into the flow: a doubled "Claude Code integration installed", a Claude-only "Restart Claude
  Code" line (the project syncs six hosts), and jumbled ordering. Construct now captures that output and prints
  one clean line (`✓ Task tracker ready — issues prefixed \`<x>-\``). The "Local SQLite database" banner claim
  was replaced (the backend is dolt). The services summary now shows the **real** dashboard URL (was a hardcoded
  port that could mismatch), reports a failed service honestly instead of hiding it, and surfaces `construct stop`.
- Orchestration API rejected every run on a no-token daemon (`construct-apd5`) — the default post-init daemon
  has no dashboard token, so programmatic clients (the `--remote` CLI, the `orchestration_run` MCP tool, editor
  adapters) sent no cookie and no `Authorization` header and hit `403 csrf_token_missing_or_invalid`. CSRF
  defends a browser cookie session, not a loopback programmatic API, so `/api/orchestration/*` is now CSRF-exempt
  (`lib/server/csrf.mjs` `defaultSkip`); the auth gate still protects it in token mode. Without this the headline
  "orchestrated outcome on any host" path failed in the default setup.
- `construct dashboard` was advertised everywhere but not a real command (`construct-lf2b`) — docs, ADR-0022,
  `connect-your-editor`, and the MCP tool's fail-fast message all say to run `construct dashboard`, but `cmdServe`
  was never registered in the handler map, so it printed "Unknown command". Now registered (`bin/construct`) with
  a CLI catalog entry.
- VS Code global sync polluted user config with a deprecated key (`construct-tkb3`) — it wrote
  `settings.json → github.copilot.mcpServers` and *seeded an empty settings.json* in every VS Code install,
  the exact every-window pollution the docs already claimed Construct had stopped. Global VS Code now merges
  into the canonical user-profile `mcp.json` (top-level `servers`, the file "MCP: Open User Configuration"
  edits) **only when it already exists** — never seeding — and the deprecated `github.copilot.mcpServers` key
  is no longer written. The verify (`lib/parity.mjs`) and detect (`lib/features.mjs`) sides moved to the same
  canonical location so write/verify stay consistent.
- Codex never received Construct's MCP servers (`construct-otlv`) — `syncCodex` only *updated* pre-existing
  `[mcp_servers.*]` tables and never *seeded* them, so a fresh Codex config got zero MCP servers, leaving
  `construct-mcp` (hence `orchestration_run`) unreachable in Codex. It now seeds the registry MCP servers like
  every other host.
- OpenCode project install landed on a path the host never reads (`construct-09tf`) — `construct sync`
  wrote `<project>/.opencode/config.json`, but OpenCode's resolver only reads `opencode.json`,
  `opencode.jsonc`, or `.opencode/opencode.json`, so the orchestrator + 28 specialists + MCP servers
  silently never loaded (confirmed against the v1.15.4 binary's embedded resolver). Project sync now writes
  `<project>/.opencode/opencode.json` and migrates a stale `.opencode/config.json` from a prior install onto
  the canonical name without losing content. `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` (`syncOpencode`); docs corrected
  in `connect-your-editor`. Tests: `tests/functional/opencode-config-path.functional.test.mjs`.
- `construct init` advertised a dead dashboard (`construct-mzlo`) — service startup passed the **project**
  dir as `rootDir`, but `startDashboard`/`startDoctor` resolved the install entrypoints (`lib/server/index.mjs`,
  `lib/doctor/index.mjs`) against it, so the dashboard crashed on boot with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` while init
  still printed its URL and persisted a dead PID. `lib/service-manager.mjs` now resolves those entrypoints
  against an `INSTALL_ROOT` derived from the module's own location, and `startDashboard` polls the port for
  readiness before persisting state — a failed boot is reported honestly (with the log tail) instead of
  advertised as running. Without this the daemon-backed `orchestration_run` MCP path had no engine to reach.
  Test: `tests/functional/init-dashboard-rootdir.functional.test.mjs`.
- `.construct/version` pin drift — the project-local launcher pin had been stuck at `0.1.0` since the
  initial commit while the package shipped `1.0.21`, so any host falling through to resolution step 2
  (`npx -p @geraldmaron/construct@<version>` — no `CONSTRUCT_DEV_PATH`, no `node_modules`) hit
  `npm error code ETARGET / No matching version found for @geraldmaron/construct@0.1.0` and the hook
  failed before any Construct code ran (observed during `/compact`'s PreCompact hook). The pin is now
  `1.0.21`, and a new `version` npm lifecycle script (`scripts/sync-construct-version.mjs`) regenerates
  it from `package.json` on every `npm version` bump and stages it into the release commit, so it can no
  longer drift behind a published release. The release preflight re-asserts the pin with
  `node scripts/sync-construct-version.mjs --check`.

### Added
- Local orchestration daemon — typed HTTP+SSE API (`construct-pdx0`, GH #215): Construct's orchestration
  runtime is now reachable as a local service so any thin client (a VS Code/Copilot extension, OpenCode, CI,
  the bundled `construct orchestrate … --remote` CLI) can trigger a real multi-specialist run and stream the
  outcome — the engine owns orchestration; the editor is a thin client (the Claude Code / OpenCode pattern).
  This is what makes orchestrated outcomes attainable on hosts with no native subagent primitive. New routes
  on the existing server (`lib/server/index.mjs`): `POST /api/orchestration/runs` (starts a background run,
  returns `runId` immediately), `GET /api/orchestration/runs[/:id]`, `GET /api/orchestration/runs/:id/events`
  (SSE lifecycle stream), `POST /api/orchestration/runs/:id/cancel` (cooperative between-task stop). New
  `lib/orchestration/events.mjs` run event bus + `startRun` (background execution). Responses use the
  versioned, secret-free embedded-contract envelope; requests reuse the dashboard auth, and CSRF is now
  exempted for `Authorization`-header (bearer) requests so programmatic clients don't need the browser CSRF
  dance (CSRF defends cookie auth, not header tokens). The daemon — a long-lived, detached service not bound to
  one project — persists runs under the user data root (`~/.cx/runtime/orchestration/`) rather than the install
  directory, which can be shared or read-only across projects; HOME is always writable and survives reinstalls.
  Decision: ADR-0022. Tests: `tests/functional/orchestration-server.functional.test.mjs`,
  `tests/orchestration-events.test.mjs`.
- MCP orchestration client — `orchestration_run` + `orchestration_status` tools (`construct-f66u`, GH #215):
  the executing counterpart to `workflow_invoke` (which only plans). `orchestration_run` drives the local
  daemon to run a real multi-specialist chain and returns per-specialist output, so MCP hosts with no
  subagent primitive (VS Code/Copilot, Cursor) reach orchestrated outcomes through a tool they actually
  expose — the engine owns orchestration, the tool is the thin client. Resolves the daemon URL from
  dashboard state and the token from `~/.construct/config.env`; an unreachable daemon **fails fast** with
  how to start it (`construct dashboard`) instead of silently degrading to a single-persona pass. New
  `lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`. Test: `tests/functional/orchestration-mcp.functional.test.mjs`.

## [1.0.21] - 2026-06-03

### Added
- Local orchestration runtime — provider worker backend and pluggable run stores (GH #207, completing the runtime).
  A new `provider` worker backend (`lib/orchestration/worker.mjs`, `runTaskViaProvider`) **executes** a specialist
  task by calling the configured provider/model (Anthropic `/v1/messages` for Claude-family models, OpenRouter
  `/v1/chat/completions` otherwise) with the specialist persona prompt as system context — recording real model
  output as `task.output` and `executor='provider:<provider>:<model>'`, status `done`; a provider failure is recorded
  (status `failed`, `task.error`) and the run completes `completed-with-failures` rather than crashing. The `inline`
  backend stays the default and remains prepare-only (ADR-0020). Pluggable run stores behind one interface
  (`lib/orchestration/store.mjs`, `resolveRunStore`): filesystem (Mode-A default), SQLite Mode-B
  (`lib/orchestration/run-store-sqlite.mjs`, lazy `node:sqlite`, `sqliteAvailable()` — falls back on Node <22.5),
  Postgres Mode-C (`lib/orchestration/run-store-postgres.mjs`, `PostgresRunStore` over `createSqlClient`). Store
  selection follows `orchestration.store` config / `CONSTRUCT_ORCHESTRATION_STORE` env override, else deployment mode
  (solo→filesystem, team/enterprise→postgres), degrading to filesystem with a warning when a backend is unavailable.
  New config block `orchestration: { workerBackend, store }` (`lib/config/schema.mjs`). Filesystem + inline stays the
  byte-for-byte-unchanged default. Decision recorded in ADR-0021 (refining ADR-0020 for the provider backend; extending
  ADR-0019). Tests: `tests/orchestration-{worker,run-store-sqlite,run-store-postgres,store-resolver}.test.mjs`,
  extended `tests/orchestration-runtime.test.mjs` (sqlite suite skips on Node <22.5; postgres suite skips with no DATABASE_URL).
- Execution metadata on live embedded runs (GH #206, completing the cluster): `invokeWorkflow` now returns an
  `execution` block (`executionMode`, `effectiveStrategy`, `requestedStrategy`, `constructCapabilitiesActive`,
  `degraded`/`degradationReason`, `semantics`) reporting the **planned** execution mode for the run, and
  accepts a `constructStrategy` request field (`auto`|`orchestrated`|`prompt-only`). `recommendPlan` gains an
  optional `execution` preview, populated only when host context (`hostModel`/`hostProvider`/`constructStrategy`)
  is supplied so triage never forces a model resolution. Both reuse `resolveExecution` and stay descriptive,
  not enforced (ADR-0019) — the planned mode, never observed host execution. Parity-safe (deterministic).
  `lib/embedded-contract/{workflow-invoke,triage}.mjs`. Tests: extended
  `tests/embedded-contract-{workflow-invoke,triage}.test.mjs`.
- Explicit ingest orchestration axis (GH #203): `construct ingest` gains an orchestration strategy
  (`prompt-only` default | `orchestrated`) independent of the existing extraction strategy
  (`adapter`|`provider`). `prompt-only` runs ingest as a deterministic extraction pass; `orchestrated`
  engages the Construct specialist chain for the `evidence-ingest` workflow. Resolved env
  (`CONSTRUCT_INGEST_ORCHESTRATION`) > config (`ingest.orchestration`) > `--orchestration=` flag > default,
  and surfaced in ingest run metadata as `orchestration` plus a full `execution` block (reusing the
  execution-capability contract, so the runtime logs the selected strategy and effective execution path).
  `lib/ingest/strategy.mjs`, `lib/config/schema.mjs`, `lib/document-ingest.mjs`. Tests:
  `tests/ingest-strategy.test.mjs`.

### Fixed
