- doctor: VS Code / Cursor adapter parity no longer reports `unreadable` on a valid JSONC `settings.json`
  (GH #214). `lib/parity.mjs` now parses editor settings with a string-aware JSONC reader (line/block
  comments and trailing commas tolerated; `//` inside a `"https://…"` value preserved); the canonical
  `specialists/registry.json` is still parsed strictly. Test: `tests/parity.test.mjs`.

## [1.0.20] - 2026-06-03

### Added
- Host-independent local orchestration runtime — Mode-A vertical slice (`construct-d6pf`, GH #207): a
  Construct-owned runtime so non-Claude hosts reach equivalent orchestration outcomes instead of depending on
  whichever editor exposes native multi-agent execution. New `construct orchestrate run|status [--json]`
  (modules `lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs` + `run-store.mjs`). Mode-A is zero-dependency: single-process,
  filesystem-backed run/task store under `.cx/runtime/orchestration/`, no Docker. A run intakes a request,
  plans a sequenced specialist chain (reusing the `orchestration-policy` planner), resolves the
  execution-capability contract (reusing `resolveExecution`), persists a durable, resumable task lifecycle
  (`queued → running → prepared`), and emits lifecycle traces. Host adapters receive structured metadata —
  `executionMode`, `constructCapabilitiesActive`, `workerBackend`, `hostRole`, `degraded`/`degradationReason`,
  `selectedProvider`/`selectedModel`, `traceId`. **Honest boundary** (ADR-0020): the Mode-A `inline` backend
  owns planning/sequencing/handoff-state/persistence/observability and *prepares* each specialist task for a
  downstream executor — it does not itself perform specialist LLM reasoning (a provider-backed worker backend
  is a later increment), and prompt-only/host-direct runs own no specialist sequence rather than implying
  orchestration. ADR-0020 registered + bound. Tests: `tests/orchestration-runtime.test.mjs`,
  `tests/functional/orchestration-mode-a.functional.test.mjs`.
- Execution-capability contract for embedded workflows (`construct-1txt`, GH #206): a fifth Embedded
  Contract Layer surface — `resolveExecution` (SDK) / `construct execution resolve --json` (CLI) /
  `construct_execution_resolve` (MCP) — that reports, before or at workflow start, whether a run will engage
  Construct orchestration or degrade to a prompt-only envelope, and why. Returns `executionMode`
  (`construct-orchestrated` | `construct-prompt-only` | `host-direct` | `same-family-fallback`),
  `constructCapabilitiesActive` (subset of `personas`/`skills`/`workflow-routing`/`prompt-envelope`),
  `degraded` + machine-readable `degradationReason`, `requestedStrategy` vs `effectiveStrategy`, and the
  resolved provider/model (reusing `resolveEmbeddedModel`). The contract is **descriptive, not enforced**
  (ADR-0019): Construct returns a plan and the host runtime executes it, so every response carries a
  `semantics` disclaimer and never claims observed host execution — asserting personas ran would violate the
  no-fabrication rule. Same-family fallback and config-error both surface as `degraded`. Additive: Embedded
  Contract `CONTRACT_VERSION` bumped `1.0.0 → 1.1.0` (old clients remain compatible). New module
  `lib/embedded-contract/execution.mjs`; ADR-0019 registered + bound (`enforced-baseline.json`). Tests:
  `tests/embedded-contract-execution.test.mjs`, extended `tests/embedded-contract-parity.test.mjs`.
- Capability-aware SessionStart output mode (`construct-oqzk`, GH #209): the SessionStart hook no longer
  unconditionally writes its verbose context payload to stdout, which polluted machine-oriented output in
  non-interactive / SDK / `claude -p` one-shot invocations. New policy `hookOutputMode` — `auto` (default) |
  `silent` | `stderr` | `stdout` — resolved env (`CONSTRUCT_HOOK_OUTPUT_MODE`) > config (`hooks.outputMode`
  in `construct.config.json`) > default. `auto` keeps the rich payload on stdout for interactive sessions and
  routes it to a debug log (`~/.cx/session-start-last.log`) for non-interactive ones, so a one-shot command's
  stdout stays reserved for its own output while diagnostics stay recoverable. Claude Code exposes no reliable
  in-hook interactive/print signal (`CLAUDECODE=1` is set in both modes; a hook's `isTTY` is `false` even
  interactively), so `auto` detects non-interactive only from reliable signals (`CI=true`, `NODE_ENV=test`,
  `CONSTRUCT_NONINTERACTIVE=1`) and SDK / host-adapter callers set the knob explicitly; `auto` never suppresses
  on an ambiguous signal, so interactive sessions keep their full startup context. New module
  `lib/hooks/_lib/output-mode.mjs`. Tests: `tests/hook-output-mode.test.mjs`,
  `tests/functional/session-start-output-mode.functional.test.mjs`.
- Explicit ingest strategy (issues #201, #203): `construct ingest` now resolves an extraction strategy
  through config and an optional `--strategy=adapter|provider` flag instead of silently using local binary
  adapters. New `ingest.strategy` (`adapter` default — current local-extractor behavior, unchanged — or
  `provider`) and `ingest.fallback` (`none` default, or `provider`/`adapter`) config keys in
  `construct.config.json`, resolved with the standard env > config > default precedence
  (`CONSTRUCT_INGEST_STRATEGY`, `CONSTRUCT_INGEST_FALLBACK`). The ingestion result now carries an
  `ingestion` block (`strategy`, `fallback`, `model`, `provider`, `fallbackApplied`) so the selected
  strategy and provider/model are visible in CLI output and the embedded-contract surfaces.
  New module `lib/ingest/strategy.mjs`. Tests: `tests/ingest-strategy.test.mjs`, extended
  `tests/embedded-contract-ingest.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/ingest-strategy.functional.test.mjs`.
- Concrete provider-backed extraction (`construct-3z9u`, closing #201): the `provider` ingest strategy now
  performs a real provider call instead of deferring. New `lib/ingest/provider-extract.mjs` sends text-class
  files inline and images/PDFs as multimodal blocks, selecting the Anthropic Messages API for Claude-family
  models and OpenRouter chat-completions otherwise. Capability is honest — audio/video and Office/zip raise a
  specific `PROVIDER_MEDIA_UNSUPPORTED`, a missing key raises `PROVIDER_KEY_MISSING`, and an unresolved model
  raises `PROVIDER_MODEL_UNRESOLVED`; on any failure the `ingest.fallback` policy decides (route to the local
  adapter and record the fallback, or surface the structured error) — `provider` never silently uses the
  adapter. The old blanket `PROVIDER_EXTRACTION_UNWIRED` placeholder is removed. Key discovery is hermetic
  when an explicit `env` is injected; `fetchImpl` is injectable for tests. Tests:
  `tests/ingest-provider-extract.test.mjs` (12 cases) and updated `tests/embedded-contract-ingest.test.mjs`.

### Fixed
- construct-mcp MCP server identity (`construct-dwfv`): the server reported a hardcoded stale `version: "1.0.0"`
  and advertised no `instructions` or resources, so MCP hosts (Claude Code, VS Code, OpenCode, Cursor) rendered
  the "Construct" entry as a bare name + OK badge with an empty detail panel. It now sources the real version
  from `lib/version.mjs`, returns a descriptive `instructions` string, and exposes a `construct://status`
  resource (version, deployment mode, broker, capabilities). Also fixed `getInstalledVersion()` being used as a
  string when it returns an object. Test: `tests/mcp-server-identity.test.mjs`; tools unchanged (65).

## [1.0.19] - 2026-06-03

### Changed
- Toolchain pinning (`construct-y3i5`): added `.tool-versions` (Node + Terraform) as the single source of
  truth read by `mise`/`asdf` locally and by `actions/setup-node` (`node-version-file`) across CI — replacing
  ~12 hardcoded `node-version: 22` entries. npm pinned via `package.json` `packageManager` (Corepack).
  The cross-version test matrix (Node 20/22) and the publish job (Node 24 for npm 11 / Trusted Publishers OIDC)
  remain intentionally explicit. CONTRIBUTING "Required tools" updated (was the stale "Node 18 or later").
- Research-grade remediation Phase C — adequate templates raised to expert-grade (epic `construct-7zrh.7`):
  `runbook.md` (severity→response/error-budget mapping atop the diagnostic flowchart), `strategy.md`
  (per-bet kill criteria + leading indicators, leading/lagging metrics, milestones, risk register),
  `signal-brief.md` (Admiralty-graded evidence + counter-signal), `prfaq.md` (spokesperson/customer quote,
  launch metrics), `customer-profile.md` (evidence-graded pain points), `product-intelligence-report.md` and
  `backlog-proposal.md` (Admiralty-graded evidence base). `one-pager`, `persona-artifact`, and the above are
  now structure-enforced by `STRUCTURE_REQUIREMENTS`.
- Research-grade remediation Phase D — adequate specialist prompts deepened with named methodology, in their
  `skills/roles/*` overlays (epic `construct-7zrh.8`): ux-researcher (validity types, sampling, inter-rater
  reliability), devil-advocate (FMEA / risk priority number), trace-reviewer (statistical process control),
  debugger (causal-chain RCA), business-strategist (Porter's Five Forces, scenario planning), data-engineer
  (lineage, data SLAs, observability maturity), platform-engineer (IaC maturity, SBOM/supply-chain),
  release-manager (progressive delivery, SLI-threshold rollback rules), operations (critical-path method,
  slack, resource leveling), legal-compliance (obligation→control risk register), security (STRIDE/PASTA
  threat-modeling process), SRE (error-budget burn-rate policy), designer (atomic design / design-system
  maturity), accessibility (screen-reader + POUR + cognitive load). Also fixed a garbled token in
  `skills/roles/operator.md`.

### Added
- Research-grade remediation (epic `construct-7zrh`), source taxonomy:
  - `docs/adr/0017-source-credibility-taxonomy.md` + `rules/common/research.md` — **claim-relative source
    classing** (community/forum content is *primary* evidence for sentiment/demand/friction claims, tertiary
    for factual ones), a community-source admissibility checklist, and the **Admiralty grade** (source
    reliability `A`–`F` × information credibility `1`–`6`, mapped onto `high/medium/low` confidence).
  - `rules/common/research-sources.md` — per-domain community starting-points catalog (which subreddits / SO
    tags / HN by domain) for sentiment research.
  - Research templates (`research-brief`, `evidence-brief`) gain `Reliability`/`Credibility` columns; the
    cx-researcher prompt teaches the rule. ADR-0017 enforced via `tests/source-taxonomy.test.mjs`.
  - Document quality standard (`docs/adr/0018-document-quality-standard.md`, `docs/concepts/doc-quality-rubric.md`):
    a seven-dimension definition of "research-grade" with an enforced structural floor — `STRUCTURE_REQUIREMENTS`
    in `lib/templates/visual-requirements.mjs` declares the required sections per doc type, and
    `tests/structure-requirements.test.mjs` fails if a template drops one. ADR-0018 enforced.

### Changed
- Thin templates raised to research-grade (epic `construct-7zrh`): `templates/docs/incident-report.md` (trigger
  vs root cause vs contributing factors, severity rationale, mitigators, timeline phases, action items with
  owner/priority/tracking, glossary — Google SRE postmortem culture), `skill-artifact.md` (competency rubric,
  prerequisites, failure modes, worked example), `research-finding.md` (sources table with Admiralty grade,
  observation/inference split, confidence reasoning, refresh trigger). Each is now enforced by
  `STRUCTURE_REQUIREMENTS`.
- Evaluator and orchestrator role overlays deepened with positive methodology: `skills/roles/reviewer.evaluator.md`
  gains rubric design, ground-truth/inter-rater reliability, false-positive-vs-false-negative cost asymmetry, and
  statistical-significance discipline; `skills/roles/orchestrator.md` gains dependency-graph/wave sequencing,
  critical-path focus, and fan-out bounding.
- `docs/concepts/template-prompt-audit.md` — quality audit of every template and specialist prompt against the
  rubric (strong/adequate/thin + sourced gaps); the backlog source of truth for Phases C/D.
- Enforcement & Decision-Durability program (epic `construct-wvbf`), first slice — the anti-drift spine:
  - `docs/adr/0015-affirm-hybrid-architecture.md` — affirms the hybrid markdown + deterministic-enforcement
    architecture (do not migrate to a pure policy engine), with a completeness audit and a gap register.
  - `lib/decisions/registry.mjs` + `construct decisions` — a machine-readable registry computed from source
    (not a committed snapshot) that indexes every load-bearing decision (ADRs, contracts, rules) and binds
    each to its enforcement via an `@enforces <decision-id>` annotation. `list` / `validate` / `json` /
    `check` / `baseline` subcommands.
  - Decision-durability ratchet (`lib/decisions/enforced-baseline.json`): `construct decisions check` fails
    on a dangling `@enforces` marker or on any baseline decision that loses its enforcement (e.g. its test
    was deleted) — turning silent decision reversal into a loud, attributable CI failure. Enforcement is
    added only by consciously regenerating the baseline. Tests: `tests/decisions-registry.test.mjs`,
    `tests/decisions-bindings.test.mjs` (incl. tmpdir failure-path coverage).
  - Citation validity in artifact lint (`lib/comment-lint.mjs#findDanglingCitations`): a `[^n]` footnote
    reference with no definition, or a `[source: <repo-path>]` pointing at a missing file, now fails the
    artifact lint (block at the release gate, warn at write-time). URLs, free-text sources, inline code, and
    fenced/table content are deliberately untouched. Strengthens no-fabrication from "a marker is nearby" to
    "the marker resolves." Test `tests/citation-validity.test.mjs` (`@enforces rule:common/no-fabrication`).
  - Capability parity contract (`docs/adr/0016-capability-parity-contract.md`, `lib/deployment/parity-contract.mjs`,
    `construct deployment parity`): classifies every `RESOURCE_TOPOLOGY` dimension as `parity` (present in
    every mode, backend may differ — queue, memory, database, telemetry) or `mode-specific` (workers, policy,
    mcp), and a validator fails when the contract and the live topology drift apart — so a divergence across
    deployment surfaces must be declared, never silent. Test `tests/deployment-parity.test.mjs`
    (`@enforces ADR-0016`). A live solo-vs-team dual-run harness remains a follow-up (needs a provisioned DB).
  - Supersede-validation gate (`lib/decisions/registry.mjs#checkSupersessionOn`, folded into
    `construct decisions check`): fails on a `Supersedes` pointing at an unknown decision, a superseding edge
    whose target is not marked `superseded`, a `superseded` decision that nothing supersedes, or a cycle —
    so a decision is replaced consciously, not silently reversed. Test `tests/decisions-supersede.test.mjs`.
  - Rule → enforcer linkage: rules may declare `enforced_by` / `adr_reference` in frontmatter (seeded on
    `rules/common/no-fabrication.md`, `rules/common/comments.md`); `construct decisions check` fails if a
    declared enforcer path is missing or the ADR reference does not resolve (a rule cannot claim enforcement
    that is not there). ADR body-hash drift is now gated in the suite (`tests/adr-stamp-integrity.test.mjs`,
    via the existing `construct doc verify` stamp check). Test `tests/decisions-rule-linkage.test.mjs`.
  - Extended executable postcondition vocabulary (`lib/contracts/validate.mjs`): `artifact-has-mermaid`
    (optionally by diagram kind), `artifact-has-table`, and `artifact-section-nonempty`, plus conversion of
    structural prose postconditions in `specialists/contracts.json` (ADR Reversibility/Consequences,
    strategic-memo Options) into executable checks. Test `tests/postcondition-checks.test.mjs`. The
    mermaid/table checks are the enforcement primitive the template-visual work builds on.
  - Prompt-optimize loop is now scheduled (`lib/scheduler/index.mjs`, job `optimize-loop`, weekly in solo):
    a cadence surfaces proposed prompt patches for review and, by construction, never passes `--apply` — the
    existing apply gate remains the only path that writes. Test `tests/scheduler-optimize-job.test.mjs`.
  - Table schema postcondition `artifact-table-has-columns` (`lib/contracts/validate.mjs`): a declared table
    must carry the required column headers and at least one data row. Test `tests/table-schema.test.mjs`.
  - Rule precedence (`lib/decisions/precedence.mjs`, `rules/common/precedence.md`): a canonical tier order
    (safety > security > correctness > durability > performance > style) resolves conflicting guidance
    deterministically; rules declare `precedence_tier` in frontmatter and `construct decisions check` fails on
    an unknown tier. Tier-based precedence, not NLP contradiction detection. Test `tests/precedence.test.mjs`.
  - Core-surface golden snapshot (`lib/decisions/golden.mjs`, `construct decisions golden`,
    `tests/fixtures/golden/surface.json`): pins the CLI command set, specialist roster, and hook execution
    order; a change fails the suite until the snapshot is regenerated on purpose. Test
    `tests/golden-surface.test.mjs`.
  - Document visual matrix + enforced template visuals (`docs/concepts/doc-visual-matrix.md`,
    `lib/templates/visual-requirements.mjs`): each doc type's required visual (runbook → diagnostic
    `flowchart`, incident-report → `Time (UTC) | Event` timeline table, rfc → `sequenceDiagram`) is encoded as
    a postcondition and the shipped templates carry the scaffolding, so `tests/template-visuals.test.mjs`
    fails if a template drops its required visual. Runbook and RFC templates gained Mermaid scaffolds. Matrix
    rationale is sourced to current Mermaid/diagram best practice.
  - Azure and GCP deployment parity with AWS: Terraform scaffolds (`deploy/terraform/azure` — Container Apps +
    PostgreSQL Flexible Server + pgvector + Key Vault + Log Analytics; `deploy/terraform/gcp` — Cloud Run +
    Cloud SQL Postgres + pgvector + Secret Manager + Cloud Logging) plus guides (`docs/deploy/azure.md`,
    `docs/deploy/gcp.md`) and a service-equivalence table in `docs/deploy/README.md`. Both modules pass
    `terraform fmt` and `terraform validate` against the provider schemas; live-apply validation is tracked by
    `construct-1fdp` (Azure) and `construct-30z8` (GCP), mirroring `construct-49j` for AWS.
- ESLint (flat config, `eslint.config.mjs`) for AST-level bug detection, complementing the custom comment
  policy. Deliberately bug-focused (no style rules): `no-undef`, `no-unreachable`, `no-empty`, etc.;
  `no-unused-vars` is a non-gating warning. Wired into the CI `lint` job (`npm run lint:js`).
- `c8` code coverage (`npm run coverage`), report-only on `main` pushes.
- Observation embedding reconciliation — an idempotent pass (`lib/embed/reconcile.mjs`,
  `construct storage reconcile`) that re-embeds Postgres observation rows that are missing (DB was down
  at write) or stale (content edited, or embedding model changed), keyed on `(content_hash, model)`.
  Schema migration `db/schema/003_observation_reconciliation.sql` adds the `content_hash`/`model` stamp to
  `construct_observations`; the `construct embed` model-change re-embed now re-dimensions and reconciles
  observations alongside documents, so both corpora stay in one vector space. Validated end-to-end against
  pgvector 0.8.2.
- `lib/storage/rrf.mjs` — Reciprocal Rank Fusion (rank-only, scale-independent) for hybrid retrieval.
- `docs/research/vector-search-best-practices.md` — cited, adversarially-verified research note grounding
  the reconciliation and hybrid-search decisions.
- Role-pending queue auto-resolution: unresolved invocations now expire from listings after a 14-day TTL
  (`lib/roles/gateway.mjs`), and a new `construct role prune` compacts the queue on disk — dropping
  resolved, TTL-expired, and pre-guard test-fixture entries so it can't accrue noise forever.
- `tests/functional/loop-closure.functional.test.mjs` — hermetic, offline-deterministic regression
  guards for the learning loop (observation capture/search/consume, outcome record/read-back, role-queue
  fixture-guard + manual-resolve, deterministic intake classification).
- `tests/functional/telemetry-backend-agnostic.functional.test.mjs` — pins the trace data plane as
  vendor-agnostic (local default, OTel/OTLP first-class, Langfuse only with Langfuse-style keys) and
  guards the vendor-neutral login bridge.

### Changed
- Consolidated hybrid search into one path (`lib/storage/hybrid-query.mjs`): file BM25 + native pgvector
  HNSW ANN (`<=>`, with `hnsw.iterative_scan` enabled on pgvector ≥ 0.8) + keyword, fused by Reciprocal
  Rank Fusion instead of a dedupe-union of incompatible score scales (BM25 vs cosine vs keyword). The
  vector search now uses the HNSW index rather than a JS full scan. Validated against pgvector 0.8.2.
- Telemetry is now vendor-agnostic at the wiring level: the dashboard telemetry-UI sign-in route is
  provider-aware (`/api/services/telemetry/login`, with `/langfuse/login` kept as a back-compat alias) —
  an external `CONSTRUCT_TELEMETRY_URL` backend uses the neutral bridge, local Langfuse keeps zero-touch.
  CLI/observability strings relabeled from "Langfuse" to backend-neutral wording; `docs/concepts/observability.mdx`
  documents the `CONSTRUCT_TRACE_BACKEND=otel` path and the two-layer agnostic stance.
- `docs/audit/implementation-audit-20260601.md` — added §8–§12: empirical validation of the loop on a
  clean isolated instance (which refuted the earlier "loop is open" / "70:1 synthesis" inferences), a
  conservative prune log, the telemetry-agnosticism finding, and evidence-based priority recommendations.

### Fixed
- `construct install` now runs the global front-door sync (`sync --global`) so a fresh machine populates
  every user-scope surface (opencode/claude/codex/copilot) and passes cross-surface adapter parity.
  Previously install wrote an empty opencode agent table that immediately failed `doctor` (exit 1).
  Guarded by `tests/functional/install-parity.functional.test.mjs`.
- **`lib/hooks/guard-bash.mjs` had a `SyntaxError`** (`await` inside a non-async IIFE) — the command-fence
  safety hook failed to parse and ran no protection. Now parses and enforces. Caught by ESLint.
- Five `no-undef` runtime bugs surfaced by ESLint: undefined `INDEX_FILE` / `PROFILES_DIR` / `multiSelect`
  / `selectOption` / `path` / `TRACES_DIR` references (`lib/embed/conflict-detection.mjs`,
  `lib/embed/customer-profiles.mjs`, `lib/init-docs.mjs`, `lib/mode-commands.mjs`,
  `lib/telemetry/backends/local.mjs`) — each would throw on its code path. Includes removing ESM-broken
  `require()` calls in the same files.
- `lib/hooks/config-protection.mjs` no longer blocks *introducing* a new code-quality config — it only
  protects a config once it is git-tracked (an established contract). Previously first-time setup (e.g.
  adding ESLint) was blocked the same as weakening an existing config. Guarded by
  `tests/hooks/config-protection.test.mjs`.

### Removed
- `lib/knowledge/postgres-search.mjs` — its tag-aware selectivity routing is subsumed by the native
  `hnsw.iterative_scan` in the consolidated path; the module had zero callers.
- `lib/storage/unified-storage.mjs` — provably-dead module (zero references, abandoned since 1.0.1,
  wired to nothing). Verified: `construct doctor` green, `construct sync` clean, storage/sync tests pass.
- `lib/services/pattern-promotion-service.mjs`, `lib/embed/jobs/vector-sync.mjs`, and
  `lib/bootstrap/lazy-install.mjs` — unwired modules with zero export consumers (verified by export-name
  cross-check). `proactive-activation.mjs` was kept (live CLI consumer `construct activation:status`);
  `postgres-search.mjs` was removed by the hybrid-search consolidation above. Stale doc/comment
  references to the removed modules were corrected.

## [1.0.18] - 2026-06-03

### Security

- **Removed the vulnerable `@xenova/transformers` → onnx → `protobufjs@6` chain from consumer installs (bead `construct-yvxf`, GitHub #184).** A downstream `npm install` of the published CLI inherited high/critical `protobufjs` advisories via the deprecated `@xenova/transformers`, and `npm audit fix` could not remediate them. Root cause was two-layered: (1) npm `overrides` in a *published* package are ignored by consumers — they apply only to the top-level project doing the install — so the repo's `protobufjs` override kept the in-repo audit green while every consumer stayed vulnerable; and (2) the heavy ML stack sat in runtime `dependencies` in violation of [ADR 0001](docs/adr/0001-zero-npm-core.md). Fixed by migrating the sole `@xenova` caller (`lib/embed/semantic.mjs`) to the already-present `@huggingface/transformers`, removing `@xenova/transformers` entirely, and dropping the now-unneeded `protobufjs` override. A clean downstream install now resolves `protobufjs@7.6.2` (patched) and `npm audit --omit=dev` reports zero findings.

### Added

- **Consumer-perspective audit gate (`npm run audit:published`).** New `scripts/audit-published-artifact.mjs` packs the artifact, installs the tarball into a throwaway project with no `overrides` in scope, and audits the dependency tree a consumer actually resolves — the check that would have caught #184 before publish. Wired into the CI `audit` job, the `release.yml` gate, and `scripts/pre-release-check.mjs`. Documents the remediation ladder in `docs/dependencies.md` (bump → replace → demote-to-optional → ADR-accept) with the load-bearing rule that a published package's `overrides` never reach consumers.
- **ADR-0001 enforcement (`tests/core-dependency-policy.test.mjs`).** A ratchet test that fails on any new runtime dependency outside the sanctioned set, freezing the known pre-existing drift (`js-yaml`, `node-webvtt`) into a tracked allowlist pending their own replace-or-ADR decisions (beads `construct-7vj6`, `construct-bh2y`), and locking in that `@xenova/transformers` is never reintroduced and the ML stack stays optional.
- **`SECURITY.md`** with a private vulnerability-reporting channel, response targets, and the interim mitigation that *does* work for consumers (a pin in their own top-level `overrides`, or `CONSTRUCT_EMBEDDING_MODEL=hashing` to skip the ONNX stack). New [ADR 0014](docs/adr/0014-local-embeddings-optional.md) records that local ONNX embeddings are an optional capability, not a core dependency.

### Changed

- **`@huggingface/transformers` moved from `dependencies` to `optionalDependencies`.** Local ONNX embedding is now an opt-in enhancement over the zero-dependency in-tree hashing embedder, so a default install is lighter and cannot fail on native ONNX binary resolution in constrained environments — aligning with ADR 0001's reliability goal.

- **Wired the template-authoring personas to `get_template`.** `cx-architect` now fetches `adr`, `cx-operations` and `cx-sre` fetch `runbook`/`incident-report`, and `cx-rd-lead` fetches `research-brief` before authoring, so their artifact structure comes from the canonical template rather than from memory. The design surfaces (`commands/design/ui.md`, `commands/design/flow.md`, `cx-designer`) now name the exact `construct wireframe "<desc>" --type=<…>` invocation that emits a diffable artifact to `.cx/wireframes/` — text-first, no new diagramming dependency (ADR 0001).

### Fixed

- **Personas instructed to call template tools they were never granted.** `cx-docs-keeper` and `cx-researcher` prompts called `get_template`/`list_templates`, but `specialists/registry.json` omitted those tools from their `claudeTools` grant, so the documented call would fail at runtime. Added the grants. New `tests/workflows/template-resolution.test.mjs` fails on any `templates/workflows/*.yml` `template:` reference that does not resolve to a `templates/docs/<name>` file, closing the silent-stub gap that left `new-feature.yml`'s `test-plan.md` reference dangling; added the missing `templates/docs/test-plan.md`.

## [1.0.17] - 2026-06-02

### Added

- **All 149 skills carry structured `inputs`/`artifactType` metadata (bead `construct-c7a8.2`).** Completed the capability-discovery skill metadata: the remaining 139 skills were annotated by reading each file's actual content (purpose, steps, output shape) and deriving `inputs` (what the skill consumes) and `artifactType` (what it produces) — never folder- or title-stamped, per the no-fabrication rule. The distribution is content-derived, not uniform (e.g. security skills split across `vulnerability-report`/`pentest-report`/`threat-model`/`guidance`; role anti-pattern overlays correctly resolve to `role-guidance`/`guidance` rather than a fabricated document type). Capability discovery now reports zero un-annotated skills and drops its coverage warning; `tests/embedded-contract-capability.test.mjs` asserts full coverage so the metadata cannot silently regress.

- **Skill coverage for the new artifact workflows + rebalanced under-resourced roles (bead `construct-c7a8.2`).** Authored four workflow-support skills with real methodology: `docs/transcript-synthesis` (transcript → summary/decisions/action-items, → researcher), `operating/raw-data-structuring` (parse/validate/profile a dataset, → data-analyst + data-engineer), `docs/memo-and-decision-capture` (→ docs-keeper), and `operating/unstructured-triage` (brain-dump → intents/candidates/open-questions, → orchestrator) — each backing the matching workflow type from this initiative. Doubled the four single-skill roles flagged in the coverage audit from 1→2 skills with genuine additions: `quality-gates/premortem` (devil-advocate), `frontend-design/screen-reader-testing` (accessibility), `ai/trace-triage` (trace-reviewer), `strategy/jobs-to-be-done` (ux-researcher). All eight are wired into `specialists/registry.json` role `skills` arrays (149 skills total) and carry accurate `inputs`/`artifactType` frontmatter; every one embeds a no-fabrication verification bar matching the repo's existing skills.

- **Intake taxonomy + workflow coverage for memos, transcripts, raw data, and unstructured notes (bead `construct-c7a8.2`).** The R&D classifier (`lib/intake/tables/rnd.mjs` + `lib/intake/classify.mjs`) gains three validated intake types — `memo` (→ docs-keeper), `transcript` (→ researcher), `raw-data` (→ data-analyst) — each with content keywords, filename hints, and title-family patterns. Four embedded workflow types are added (`lib/embedded-contract/workflow-defs.mjs`): `transcript-process`, `data-structure`, `memo-draft`, and `structure-notes` (a generic path for unclassified brain-dumps/rough notes); `INTAKE_TO_WORKFLOW` maps each new type to its workflow and routes `unknown` → `structure-notes` so a brain-dump still gets a suggested workflow while remaining non-auto-executable (`canExecute` stays false on `unknown`). New classifier-accuracy cases (`tests/intake-classifier-accuracy.test.mjs`) gate the three types (correct routing + no false-positive on unrelated prose); `tests/embedded-contract-workflow-defs.test.mjs` pins the expanded type set and that every chain references real registry roles. Honest-degradation by design: transcripts and raw data are extracted by format (Phase A) and classified by content rather than treated as topic-free formats, and a type ships only because it classifies its fixtures confidently.

- **Embedded contracts ingest any supported file, not just UTF-8 text (bead `construct-c7a8.1`).** The triage and workflow-invocation surfaces now route a `--file` / `file_path` input through Construct's real extraction pipeline (`extractDocumentTextAsync`: docling for PDF/Office, whisper for audio/video, the transcript/calendar/email extractors, UTF-8 for text incl. CSV/TSV) instead of reading raw bytes. New `lib/embedded-contract/ingest.mjs#resolveInput` is the shared file→text resolver used by all three surfaces (CLI, MCP `file_path` arg, SDK `extractFileForContract`); the plan/invoke cores stay synchronous and receive `{ text, ingestion }`. Resolution is honest: audio/video without an ASR backend returns a structured `ASR_REQUIRED` error with remediation (not gibberish), an unrecognized extension degrades to a flagged `raw-utf8` read with a note, and a missing file returns a structured error rather than throwing. The contract response carries an `ingestion` block (`extractionMethod`, `characters`, `truncated`, `droppedInfo`, `note?`, `error?`) and low-text-yield / truncation / extraction-error signals surface as `warnings`, so a thin classification is always attributable to thin source text. Covered by `tests/embedded-contract-ingest.test.mjs` (6 unit cases) and new file-based cases in `tests/functional/embedded-contract-triage.functional.test.mjs` (.txt → utf8, .vtt → transcript extractor).

- **ECL SDK packaging, cross-surface parity guards, and documentation (bead `construct-bxd7.6`).** Adds `package.json` `exports` so the embedded contract layer is importable as a published SDK: `import { describeCapabilities, recommendPlan, resolveEmbeddedModel, invokeWorkflow } from '@geraldmaron/construct/embedded-contract'`. A `./lib/*` passthrough preserves existing deep imports so adding `exports` introduces no encapsulation regression (verified by self-reference resolution). New `tests/embedded-contract-parity.test.mjs` (6 cases) is the durable anti-drift guard: it proves the MCP and SDK surfaces return structurally identical envelopes for every contract (modulo the surface label and volatile ids), that every surface stamps the same valid `contractVersion`, and that no contract leaks a credential value present in the environment. Documentation: `docs/concepts/architecture.mdx` gains an "Embedded contract layer" section; new `docs/concepts/embedded-contract.mdx` (contract model, envelope, versioning, approval modes, provenance, safety invariants) and `docs/reference/embed-api.mdx` (request/response field schemas for all four contracts); new cookbook page `docs/cookbook/embed-construct-in-your-app.mdx` walks discover → resolve → triage → invoke end to end. All three docs registered in their `meta.json` indexes.

- **ECL capability discovery contract + skill frontmatter extension (bead `construct-bxd7.5`, GitHub #162).** Adds `lib/embedded-contract/capability.mjs` (`buildCapabilityContract`) — a read-only, secret-free description of what a Construct install can do: versions + `contractVersion`, the three contract interfaces (CLI/MCP/SDK, each versioned), roles (from the live registry), skills, workflows (from `workflow-defs`), schemas (shipped artifacts), models/providers, policies (`policy-inventory.json`), telemetry posture, and plugins. Every section reads from the live registries so the published contract cannot drift from reality, and each is independently guarded — a failing source degrades to a warning instead of breaking discovery. Provider entries carry env-key **names** and a `configured` boolean only (via the spawn-free `listModelFamilies`), never a credential value; the envelope's no-secrets guard is the backstop. Exposed on all three surfaces: CLI `construct capability describe --json`, MCP tool `capability_describe`, SDK `describeCapabilities`. **Skill frontmatter convention extended:** optional `inputs` (list of strings) and `artifactType` (string) keys are now part of the skill frontmatter spec — the parser (`lib/sync/skill-frontmatter.mjs`) already reads them, the authoring validator (`lib/validators/skills.mjs`) validates their shape when present (opt-in; absent fields never error), and sync continues to drop them from emitted `SKILL.md` to stay Anthropic-spec-compliant. Capability discovery surfaces the structured metadata where present and honestly flags the count of un-annotated skills; an accurate starter set (`quality-gates/review-work`, `security/vuln-research`) is annotated, with the remainder migrated incrementally rather than auto-populated (auto-populating per-skill inputs/artifactType would fabricate metadata, which the no-fabrication rule forbids). Covered by `tests/embedded-contract-capability.test.mjs` (6 cases including registry-drift and no-secret-leak), `tests/skill-frontmatter-metadata.test.mjs` (4 cases), and `tests/functional/embedded-contract-capability.functional.test.mjs` (2 cases).

- **ECL workflow invocation contract (bead `construct-bxd7.4`, GitHub #159).** Adds `lib/embedded-contract/workflow-invoke.mjs` (`invokeWorkflow`) and `lib/embedded-contract/workflow-defs.mjs` (six workflow types: evidence-ingest, proposal-review, prd-draft, architecture-review, risk-review, research-synthesis). Invoking a workflow returns a **provenanced execution plan** — selected roles (auto/explicit/constrained strategy), per-role rationale, applied skills, the resolved model (via the #160 model-resolution contract), evidence requirements + satisfied/missing accounting, the output contract, structured risks, and a `traceId`. Construct returns the orchestration plan and output contract; the host agent runtime performs specialist reasoning, so the contract never fabricates specialist output. Durable side effects are gated by approval mode (`resolveWriteGate`, `lib/embedded-contract/audit.mjs`): `proposal-only` writes nothing (`status: proposed`); `requires-human-approval` records an approval request to `~/.cx/approval-pending.jsonl` and writes nothing durable (`status: awaiting-approval`); `allow-durable-write` records the invocation to the observation store as durable provenance (`status: recorded`), flagged `audited` on team/enterprise deployments. Workflow definitions declare only real registry role chains and real schema artifacts (no invented ids); skills are derived from the selected roles. Shared role/contract enrichment is centralized in `lib/embedded-contract/role-facts.mjs` (also adopted by the triage contract). The triage contract now emits `suggestedWorkflowType` (via `workflowTypeForIntake`) so a plan bridges directly to invocation. Exposed on all three surfaces: CLI `construct workflow invoke --json`, MCP tool `workflow_invoke`, SDK `invokeWorkflow`. Covered by `tests/embedded-contract-workflow-invoke.test.mjs` (9 unit cases), `tests/embedded-contract-workflow-defs.test.mjs` (6 cases), and `tests/functional/embedded-contract-workflow-invoke.functional.test.mjs` (3 cases including approval-gate and credential-leak assertions against the real binary).

- **ECL triage/planning contract (bead `construct-bxd7.3`, GitHub #161).** Adds `lib/embedded-contract/triage.mjs` — `recommendPlan(request)` classifies an artifact and returns a role-aware plan (classification, primary owner, recommended chain with per-role rationale, suggested skills, evidence requirements, expected outputs, approval requirements, structured risks, next-step options, `canExecute`) **without enqueuing or executing anything**. It wraps the deterministic classifier (`classifyRdIntake`, `lib/intake/classify.mjs`) and enriches only from verifiable sources: role rationale + skills from the role catalog (`lib/roles/catalog.mjs`), and evidence requirements + expected outputs from the owner's specialist contract (`getIncomingContracts`, `lib/specialist-contracts.mjs` — `input.mustContain`/`preconditions`/`output.mustContain`). Classification confidence is reported as a distinct, labeled field (`confidenceKind: 'classification'`, `classificationConfidence`) and never conflated with downstream generation confidence (produced only when a workflow runs). `canExecute` is a soft check in this phase (confident, non-unknown, usable chain) and tightens to a workflow-type mapping once workflow invocation lands. Enterprise deployment mode mandates approval; `availableRoles` filters the chain and warns on dropped roles. Exposed on all three surfaces: CLI `construct intake classify --json` (and the alias `construct graph recommend --json`) reading from `--text`/`--file`/stdin, MCP tool `triage_recommend`, and SDK `recommendPlan`. The CLI branch runs before any queue instantiation, guaranteeing no `.cx/intake/pending` write. Covered by `tests/embedded-contract-triage.test.mjs` (8 unit cases) and `tests/functional/embedded-contract-triage.functional.test.mjs` (3 cases, including a no-durable-write assertion against the real binary).

- **ECL model resolution contract (bead `construct-bxd7.2`, GitHub #160).** Adds `lib/embedded-contract/model-resolve.mjs` — `resolveEmbeddedModel(request)` resolves which model an embedded workflow should use from host/IDE provider context, following a fixed precedence: host-model → same-provider-family fallback → Construct tier default → structured config error. Cross-provider fallback is only taken on explicit `allowCrossProviderFallback` opt-in; otherwise unhonorable host context yields a `MODEL_UNRESOLVED` error with remediation rather than a silent provider switch. The contract never reads or returns a credential value — `requiresCredential` is an env-derived boolean — and never claims unverifiable provider health (`healthStatus` defaults to `unknown`, `estimatedLimits` to `null`). Reuses two new env-only (deterministic, no probe) helpers in `lib/model-router.mjs` — `describeModelFamily(modelId, {env})` and `listModelFamilies({env})` — plus `resolveModelTiers` (`lib/model-registry.mjs`) for the tier-default layer and `resolveProviderCapabilitiesSync` for best-effort capability matching (unverifiable capabilities surface as warnings, never asserted). Exposed on all three surfaces from one core: CLI `construct models resolve --json`, MCP tool `model_resolve`, and SDK `resolveEmbeddedModel` (`lib/embedded-contract/index.mjs`). The shared `wrapContractResult` envelope helper (`lib/embedded-contract/envelope.mjs`) keeps the three surfaces structurally identical and runs the no-secrets guard on every response. Covered by `tests/embedded-contract-model-resolve.test.mjs` (10 unit cases) and `tests/functional/embedded-contract-model-resolve.functional.test.mjs` (3 cases, including a planted-credential leak assertion against the real binary).

- **Embedded Contract Layer (ECL) foundation (bead `construct-bxd7.1`).** First slice of the app-facing embeddable orchestration layer tracked by `construct-bxd7` (GitHub #159–#162). Adds a shared core under `lib/embedded-contract/` that every contract surface (CLI-JSON, MCP, SDK) will delegate to: `contract-version.mjs` introduces `CONTRACT_VERSION` (semver `1.0.0`, versioned independently of the package and of `contracts.json`/plugin `MANIFEST_VERSION`) with `isClientCompatible` for client negotiation; `redaction.mjs` provides the load-bearing no-secrets guard — `assertNoSecrets` derives the credential set from environment keys at call time and throws if any live secret value appears anywhere in a payload, so a surface cannot leak a token even if a future field copies one in; `envelope.mjs` wraps every payload with contract/Construct versions, deployment mode, and timestamp, running the redaction guard before serialization so the three interfaces stay structurally identical; `audit.mjs` owns the approval-mode → write-gate decision (`proposal-only`/`requires-human-approval`/`allow-durable-write`, mandatory audit on team/enterprise durable writes) and per-invocation `traceId`. Covered by `tests/embedded-contract-foundation.test.mjs` (8 cases).

- **`construct dev` service selector — `--select` / `--only=` (bead `construct-c7a8.3`, Phase C remainder).** `construct dev` previously started every available service unconditionally. It now accepts `--select` (an interactive checklist via `lib/tty-prompts` `multiSelect`, all pre-checked) and `--only=postgres,dashboard,telemetry,memory,opencode` (non-interactive). `lib/service-manager.mjs` exports `SELECTABLE_SERVICES` and `startServices` takes a `selected` Set — `null` keeps the start-everything default; a Set gates the five optional services. Pressure Guard (cleanup) and Doctor (the L0 supervisor) are always-on infrastructure and intentionally not selectable. Unknown `--only` keys fail loud (exit 1, valid list printed); `--select` on a non-TTY errors and points at `--only` so scripts get a deterministic path. New tests in `tests/service-manager.test.mjs` assert the gate (not availability) decides what starts and that no unselected daemon is spawned.

- **Per-operation structured log files for `dev` / `install` / `init` / `sync` (bead `construct-c7a8.3`, Phase C remainder — C2).** New `lib/op-log.mjs` writes a correlation-id-stamped JSONL trace to `<home>/.cx/<op>-<timestamp>.log` for each long-running CLI op, reusing `lib/logger.mjs`'s line format. Every line shares one `op_id`; `op.start`/`op.end` bracket the run with a final `status` (`ok`/`degraded`/`failed`) plus context (selected services, prereq results, per-service status, exit code). Human-facing stdout is unchanged — this is a parallel machine trace for post-hoc debugging. Writes are synchronous (open fd + `writeSync`) so a `process.exit()` can't drop the tail, and the handle silently no-ops if the log file can't open (logging never breaks the operation). `runNodeScript` gained an `exitOnError` opt-out so `sync`/`init` record the subprocess outcome before propagating its exit code. Covered by `tests/op-log.test.mjs` (trace shape, shared id, status stamping, no-op-on-unwritable-home).

### Changed

- **Committed next 16's generated `tsconfig.json` + `next-env.d.ts` for `apps/dashboard` and `apps/docs` (bead `construct-5i7o`).** Dependabot's next 15→16 bump touched only `package.json`/lockfile, but next 16's build rewrites these generated configs (the `jsx` / `.next/dev/types` tsconfig settings and the `next-env.d.ts` triple-slash→`import` syntax). The committed state lagged, so every local `next build` left a dirty tree. Re-built both apps, confirmed the regeneration is idempotent, and committed the result so builds no longer churn the working tree.

- **TypeScript 5.6 → 6.0.3 in dashboard + docs apps (supersedes dependabot #172).** Adopted the current GA TypeScript major. TS 6 promotes the deprecated `baseUrl` compiler option to a build error; added `"ignoreDeprecations": "6.0"` to both app tsconfigs (the remedy TS itself prescribes) — `paths` resolution is unchanged. Both apps type-check and build clean on TS 6.0.3. **Gate refinement:** `lib/hooks/config-protection.mjs` previously hard-blocked all `tsconfig.json` edits; it now treats tsconfig as an audited meta edit (advisory) rather than a blocked code-quality contract, because TS compiler options legitimately change with the toolchain (this version-compat flag being the example), while the lint-*rule* configs (eslint/prettier/biome/stylelint) remain hard-blocked. Hook verified in isolation (tsconfig → advisory, eslint/prettier → still blocked). Also corrected the hook's stale meta-file patterns (`agents/registry.json` → `specialists/registry.json`; phantom `install.sh` → `lib/setup.mjs` + `bin/construct-postinstall.mjs`).

- **Dashboard + docs apps migrated to Tailwind CSS v4 (supersedes dependabot #169).** Bumped `tailwindcss` 3.4 → 4.3 in `apps/dashboard` and `apps/docs` and applied the v4 migration: added `@tailwindcss/postcss` (Tailwind v4 moved its PostCSS plugin to a separate package — the v3 "use tailwindcss directly as a PostCSS plugin" path is removed), updated both `postcss.config.mjs` to use it, and changed `app/theme.css` from `@tailwind base/components/utilities` to `@import "tailwindcss"` + `@config "../tailwind.config.ts"`. The `@config` directive loads each app's existing Tailwind config, preserving the theme tokens and minimizing visual change. Both apps build clean (`next build` ✓; docs exports 106 static pages) and the `dashboard-build` functional test passes. Added a `css.d.ts` ambient `declare module '*.css'` in each app so side-effect CSS imports keep a type under stricter TypeScript resolution.

### Fixed

- **`construct init --with-docs` resolves singular aliases and warns on unknown lanes (bead `construct-c7a8.3`).** `parseWithDocs` filtered lanes against the canonical list *before* applying aliases, so `--with-docs=adr,rfc` silently dropped both (the alias map was dead code), and any unrecognized lane vanished with no feedback. Aliases (`adr→adrs`, `rfc→rfcs`, etc.) are now mapped before validation, and unknown lanes emit a `Warning:` listing the valid set while valid lanes still proceed (one typo no longer aborts init). Verified: `--with-docs=adr,bogus` creates the `adr` lane and warns about `bogus`.

- **`construct init` now runs its prerequisite check + install offer, and drops debug noise (bead `construct-c7a8.3`).** `init`'s `checkPrerequisites()` — which verifies machine deps and offers to run `construct install` — was defined but never called (dead code; prerequisites were silently unchecked beyond the git preflight). It is now invoked at the start of `main()`, gated to interactive TTY sessions (`--interactive`/`-i`), so a missing dependency surfaces before scaffolding while non-interactive/CI runs are unaffected. Removed four stray `[TRACE init:*]` debug `console.log` markers that leaked into normal output. Verified by the existing init functional tests (non-interactive path unchanged).

- **`construct install` gains error resilience, `--reconfigure`, and flag validation (bead `construct-c7a8.3`).** `cmdSetup` now wraps `runSetup` so an unexpected failure prints actionable remediation (`construct doctor`, re-run hint) and exits non-zero instead of dumping an unhandled stack trace. `runSetup` rejects unknown flags loudly and fails fast *before* any machine setup (a typo like `--reconfig` no longer silently runs defaults). New `--reconfigure` flag forces re-prompting for service consent (Postgres, cheapest-provider), ignoring cached `BOOTSTRAP_*` answers — so a previously-declined service can be reconsidered without hand-editing `config.env`. Documented in the CLI catalog + `runSetup` help. Covered by `tests/functional/setup-arg-validation.functional.test.mjs` (bad flag → exit 1, no config written).

- **Corrected phantom `install.sh` references to the real install surface (bead `construct-c7a8.3`).** Four places named `install.sh` as a protected/meta file, but no `install.sh` has ever existed in the repo — the install path is `lib/setup.mjs` (`runSetup`) + `bin/construct-postinstall.mjs`. `lib/hooks/config-protection.mjs` `META_FILES` matched the nonexistent `install.sh` (and a stale `agents/registry.json` instead of `specialists/registry.json`), so its meta-edit advisory never fired for the actual install/registry files; it now matches `specialists/registry.json`, `lib/setup.mjs`, and `bin/construct-postinstall.mjs` (hook verified in isolation — advisory for meta files, still blocks code-quality configs). The protected-file lists in `CLAUDE.md`, `specialists/role-manifests.json` (specialist approval gate), and `specialists/prompts/cx-engineer.md` now reference `lib/setup.mjs` instead of `install.sh`.

- **`npm install` no longer hides a failed Construct postinstall (bead `construct-c7a8.3`).** `bin/construct-postinstall.mjs` previously logged staging/global-sync failures as a benign-looking stdout line and exited 0, so a consumer's `npm install` looked clean while leaving the project with incomplete `.claude/` adapters. Failures now print a prominent, actionable WARNING block to **stderr** with the exact recovery command (`npx construct init` for staging, `construct sync --global` for global). The script deliberately stays exit-0 (documented inline): a best-effort adapter-staging step must not hard-fail the consumer's entire install — the package is installed and usable, and staging is recoverable on demand. This makes the failure visible without the blast radius of breaking installs.

- **MCP tool reference completeness + drift guard (bead `construct-c7a8.4`).** `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` documented only ~40 of the 65 registered MCP tools (stale "7 modules / ~40 tools" header) — 24 were undocumented, including the 4 embedded-contract tools plus the profile/outcomes/learning and workflow-orchestration families. Added accurate entries (name, description, parameter tables generated from the live `inputSchema` definitions) under new "Workflow orchestration", "Profile, outcomes & learning", "Embedded contract", and telemetry-addendum sections. New `tests/mcp-tools-doc-parity.test.mjs` parses the tool registry from `lib/mcp/server.mjs` and fails if any registered tool lacks a `### \`name\`` doc heading (or if a doc heading maps to no registered tool), so the reference can no longer drift. CLI catalog (`lib/cli-commands.mjs`) gains the previously-undocumented `doctor` subcommands (`status/logs/tick/report/consistency/watch/stop/credentials`) and `plugin` subcommands (`validate`, `engine`).

- **`listRoles()` read absent registry keys and returned an empty list (bead `construct-bxd7.1`).** `lib/roles/catalog.mjs` read `registry.agents` / `registry.consolidatedRoles` / `registry.departments` — none of which exist in `specialists/registry.json`; the live shape is a flat `specialists` array of 28 roles. The function silently returned `[]`, breaking `construct roles:list` and blocking the embedded capability contract that needs a real role source. Rewrote the reader to map `registry.specialists` to descriptors (`id`, prefixed `name`, `description`, `modelTier`, `internal`, `canEdit`, `skills`). The `--departments` and `--consolidated` flags referenced grouping data that has no source anywhere in the repo (verified: `subDepartments`/`consolidatedRoles` appear only in the dead code itself), so they are removed from the `roles:list` handler and the one stale doc reference in `docs/concepts/intake-loop.md` rather than left to return empty. Regression coverage in `tests/roles-catalog.test.mjs` (4 cases) pins the reader to the live array, asserts the 28-role count and descriptor shape, and breaks if the registry schema drifts.

- **`construct init` NEXT STEPS list numbering (surfaced by live validation of bead `construct-c7a8.3`).** The post-init checklist hard-coded `1.`/`2.`/`4.` with the `3. Use Intake` step gated behind the intake lane — so any init without intake printed `1, 2, 4`, a visible gap users hit on the common path. Steps now number from a running counter and stay sequential regardless of which optional steps appear.

- **Test-suite flakiness under parallel / clean-isolated execution (bead `construct-65e6`).** Root cause: `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` keyed its `--global`-tier lock and staging dir to the *repo root* (`<repo>/.cx/sync.lock`, `sync-staging`), even though the global tier only mutates user scope (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, …). Any two `--global` syncs — e.g. the parallel test files `sync-no-double-frontmatter`, `sync-respects-scope`, `sync-contract`, and the `doctor --fix-legacy-agents` path, each running against its own isolated `HOME` — collided on that one shared repo-root lock, so the loser aborted with exit 1 and its assertions failed intermittently. Fix: the global tier now scopes its lock and staging to `HOME` (`stateBase = projectDir || home`), so isolated-`HOME` syncs never contend and staging renames stay on the destination filesystem. This also aligns the lock path with the embed daemon's existing stale-lock cleanup (`lib/embed/daemon.mjs` already expected `~/.cx/sync.lock`). The `a2-research-persistence` functional test now spawns under a private `HOME` (and drops a leaked `CX_TOOLKIT_DIR`) so construct's startup side effects can't bleed across parallel test files. `sync-contract` lock/staging assertions updated to the HOME-scoped path. Verified: full clean-env repro (`HOME=$(mktemp -d) npm test`) green at 2355 pass / 0 fail, and the global-sync test set green across 5 consecutive parallel runs.

## [1.0.16] - 2026-06-01

### Highlights

CLI hygiene and follow-up cleanup across two phases. Phase 1 (PRs #140–#143): `construct <cmd> --help` is safe everywhere, `construct install --yes` no longer crashes, role-gateway no longer files beads for test-fixture events, doctor's stale warnings cleaned up, next-mdx-remote HIGH CVE closed. Phase 2 (PRs #144–#148): cookbook playbooks for the shipped workflow templates, doc-hygiene baseline stamps, `platforms/claude/CLAUDE.md` realigned with canonical structure, tag auto-attribution producer wired into intake, team-mode hourly doc-hygiene cadence, and the `construct_cx_scores` schema + `construct_skill_quality_correlation` view + producer + CLI consumer landed end-to-end. **88 beads resolved this cycle** (15 shipped across the 9 PRs, 73 closed as resolved-already / wont-do / superseded / test-fixture-noise — including 58 noise beads now prevented by the new tmpdir-path gateway filter).

### Added

- **`construct_cx_scores` table and `construct_skill_quality_correlation` view (bead `construct-0xkx`).** New migration `db/schema/010_cx_scores.sql` creates a per-trace quality-score log and a 90-day-window aggregate view that joins it against `construct_skill_invocations` (from 008) by `session_id`. The view exposes `sessions`, `score_count`, `mean_score`, `median_score`, `p10_score`, `p90_score`, `last_scored_at` per `skill_id`. Producer wired in: `cxScore` in `lib/mcp/tools/telemetry.mjs` now writes to `construct_cx_scores` when `DATABASE_URL` is set (best-effort; never blocks the remote-telemetry or observation-store writes). Consumer wired in: `construct skills correlate-quality` (`bin/construct:2425-2470`) replaces its prior "Run with Postgres configured" stub with a real `select … from construct_skill_quality_correlation` and prints a formatted rollup. Closes the user-visible false-advertisement gap the CLI had since the skill-usage observability work landed without its correlation half. CI: the new `tests/functional/skills-correlate-quality.functional.test.mjs` is added to the `postgres-integration` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (5 cases — schema parse, migration order, producer-writes, view-aggregates, end-to-end CLI rollup).

### Fixed

- **`doc-hygiene-scan` scheduler job is now deployment-mode-aware (bead `construct-9egp`).** Single contributor solo deployments run nightly at 02:00 with limit 25 — doc drift accumulates slowly. Team and enterprise run at the top of every hour with limit 50 — multiple writers shift the doc surface within a workday and the higher cadence + headroom keeps the reconcile worker from falling behind. New exported `resolveDocHygieneSchedule(env)` in `lib/scheduler/index.mjs` is the single source of truth and resolves at both registration time (for the cron string) and handler-invocation time (for the limit). Five test cases in `tests/scheduler-doc-hygiene.test.mjs` pin the matrix (solo, team, enterprise, unknown-mode fallback, empty-env fallback).

- **Intake packets now carry tag suggestions from `suggestTags` (bead `construct-rxr8`).** The `suggestTags` export from `lib/intake/classify.mjs` had zero callers — the deterministic tag inference path was a no-op despite shipping in v1.0.x. `lib/intake/prepare.mjs` now invokes `suggestTags(triage, related, vocab)` after classification and stamps the result onto the packet's `tags` field (omitted when no suggestions, so empty results don't pollute the packet shape). Vocab loads via `loadVocabulary(rootDir)` with a try/catch fall-through so a missing or malformed `config/tag-vocabulary.json` never blocks the packet write. Two upstream improvements made this safe: (a) `related` reshape now accepts both `{ results: [...] }` and raw-array shapes from `hybridSearchFn` so test mocks and production wrappers stay aligned; (b) `related` entries preserve `tags[]` for the related-inherit suggestion path. Two new test cases in `tests/intake-prepare.test.mjs` cover the happy path (triage + 2 related docs with shared tag → both shapes appear in `entry.tags` with proper `source` + `confidence`) and the no-fabrication case (triage `unknown` + no related → `tags` field absent from packet).

- **`platforms/claude/CLAUDE.md` realigned with canonical `/CLAUDE.md` structure (bead `construct-cr6` D2).** The shipped reference template at `platforms/claude/CLAUDE.md` was a stale generic stub that didn't mention any of the critical rules from the canonical project file. Diagnostic note: the file is actually orphaned in the live sync path — `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs:738` composes the user-scope `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` managed block inline from persona data, not from this platform file — so the "drift to users" the bead described was theoretical. The fix is still worth landing because the platform file ships as guidance in the npm package; users reading it for the shape of their own `/CLAUDE.md` were getting outdated guidance. Rewrote the file as a project-agnostic mirror of the canonical structure (critical rules, workflow roles, beads tracker, session completion). New regression test `tests/platforms-claude-md-parity.test.mjs` (3 assertions) asserts the platform file references every critical rule the canonical file does, has matching section headings, and breaks loudly if the canonical file removes a rule without the template catching up.

### Documentation

- **Three workflow-template cookbook pages added (bead `construct-432h`).** [`docs/cookbook/onboard-a-new-engineer.mdx`](docs/cookbook/onboard-a-new-engineer.mdx), [`track-research-findings.mdx`](docs/cookbook/track-research-findings.mdx), [`cross-team-handoff.mdx`](docs/cookbook/cross-team-handoff.mdx) each maps 1:1 to a shipped `templates/workflows/*.yml`. CHANGELOG line 298 (v1.0.8 entry) had claimed these landed but they didn't — closes a no-fabrication gap. `docs/cookbook/meta.json` and `docs/cookbook/index.mdx` updated to surface them under a new "Workflow templates" section.

- **`docs/concepts/gates-and-enforcement.mdx` documents the two local-only-by-design pre-push behaviors (bead `construct-rxn` F2).** Adds a "Local-only by design" subsection explaining why the `claude/*` branch-push refusal and the SHA-aware red-CI re-push block are intentionally local — stylistic and time-saving respectively, not safety-critical, and cited in `tests/ci-parity.test.mjs` so a future PR re-adding them to CI fails the build. Future maintainers won't accidentally mirror them.

- **`last_verified_at` baseline stamp on 8 PRD/ADR/RFC artifacts (bead `construct-6o1b`).** The doc-hygiene scanner classifies any artifact without a `last_verified_at` as "no last_verified_at" → reconcile candidate. Without a baseline every artifact looked stale on day one. Stamped today's date + `verified_by: cx-docs-keeper · PR-D baseline stamp` across `docs/adr/0001-zero-npm-core.md`, `0002-layered-architecture.md`, `0003-provider-interface.md`, `0013-skills-on-disk-layout.md`, `docs/prd/0001-construct-org-in-a-box.md`, `docs/rfc/0001-embed-yaml-schema.md`, `0002-provider-capability-matrix.md`, `0003-dashboard-chat-protocol.md`. Three docs without frontmatter also got a minimal `intake: none` block matching the pattern in `docs/prd/0001-construct-org-in-a-box.md`.

### Security

- **`next-mdx-remote` bumped from `^5.0.0` to `^6.0.0` in `apps/docs/package.json` (bead `construct-jymn`).** Closes the HIGH-severity CVE [GHSA-g4xw-jxrg-5f6m](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-g4xw-jxrg-5f6m). v6 changes the default JS-expression handling (`blockJS: true`, `blockDangerousJS: true`) to a security-first stance — first-party docs under `docs/` are trusted, so `apps/docs/app/[...slug]/page.tsx` opts into `{ blockJS: false, blockDangerousJS: true }`: expressions allowed, dangerous APIs (`eval`, `Function`, `process`, `require`) still blocked. Verified locally: `npm install` clean, `npm audit --audit-level=high` reports 0, `next build` produces all 103 static pages without prerender failures (the failures the bead called out on `/cookbook/plug-in-your-own-llm` and `/concepts/learning-loops` are gone). `.github/workflows/pages.yml` gains a `npm audit --audit-level=high` step before the build so a future MDX/Next.js CVE can't ship to Pages silently.

### Added

- **`formatOverlaySelection(roleFlavors)` in `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs` (bead `construct-e2s`).** Emits one line per non-null role flavor in the shape `cx-<role>: loaded <role>.<flavor> overlay`. Verbose-chat surface + `cx_trace` span attribute so a post-hoc reviewer can see which overlays drove a given dispatch. Two regression tests in `tests/orchestration-policy.test.mjs` cover the happy path and edge cases (null, empty, all-null, non-object).

### Fixed

- **`construct sync --global` bootstraps `~/.../Code/User/settings.json` when missing (bead `construct-9r6c`).** Previously, when VS Code was installed but the user had never opened Preferences, `getVSCodeSettingsPaths()` filtered out the candidate via `fs.existsSync`, and `syncVSCode` silently skipped — leaving the MCP block unwritten and the doctor unable to fix it. The candidate filter now also accepts paths whose parent User dir exists (the installation signal); `syncVSCode` writes a minimal seed `{}` when the file is absent and merges the MCP block in.

- **Dashboard intake queue summary carries profile-aware `label` and `itemNoun` (bead `construct-f6v`).** `summarizeIntakeQueue` in `lib/server/insights.mjs` now calls `getRebrand(cwd)` and returns `{ label, itemNoun }` alongside the count fields so the Mission Control panel can render the active profile's rebrand text (e.g. `rnd` → "Intake queue"/"signal"; future `sales-ops` → "Lead inbox"/"lead") instead of hardcoding "intake"/"signal". CLI (`construct intake list|show`) and session-prelude already honor the rebrand; dashboard now closes the gap.

- **Construct persona enumerates the task-packet shape it owes the orchestrator (bead `construct-bp7u`).** `personas/construct.md` now references `goal`, `intent`, `workCategory`, `riskFlags`, `acceptanceCriteria` by name — the five fields `specialists/contracts.json:construct-to-orchestrator.input.mustContain` requires. Without the persona surface, dispatched packets failed `validateHandoff` and the orchestrator's input check emitted `BLOCKED_CONTRACT` four times in the last 24h on this project (the warning that surfaced through doctor after PR #140). New regression test in `tests/prompt-surface.test.mjs` asserts every contract `mustContain` field is named in the persona.

- **Intake demo refuses to publish to real GitHub Issues (bead `construct-qaoy`).** `lib/integrations/intake-integrations.mjs:createGitHubIssue` now runs an `isDemoIntakePacket()` gate before any `gh issue create` / REST POST. Returns `{ ok: false, skipped: 'demo-source', error: '…' }` when the packet's `intake.sourcePath` is under `tests/fixtures/`, `.cx/intake/demo/`, or `cx-intake-demo*`; or when `CONSTRUCT_DEMO=1` / `CONSTRUCT_INTAKE_DEMO=1` is set. Override via the new `--publish-issues` flag on `construct intake integrate <id> github` (forwards `publishDemo: true` to the integration). Five tests in `tests/integrations/intake-integrations.test.mjs` cover the matrix: path-based gate, env-based gate, real-source pass-through, override unlock, edge cases.

- **Read-tracker deltas flush on session end (bead `construct-ddu`).** `lib/hooks/stop-notify.mjs` now imports `flushReadTrackerDeltas` and calls it once near the top of the Stop hook, folding accumulated PostToolUse Read deltas back into the persistent JSON. Already wired into `pre-compact.mjs` and `audit-reads.mjs`; Stop is the third anchor and the canonical end-of-session flush point.

- **Role gateway no longer files beads for test-fixture events.** `lib/roles/gateway.mjs` `shouldEscalate` is preceded by an `isTestFixturePath` guard that suppresses escalation when an event's `cwd`, `project`, or `context.filePath` resolves under the OS tmpdir (`os.tmpdir()`, `/private` + that prefix, `/tmp/`, or the macOS canonical `/private/var/folders/<2>/<long>/T/` and bare `/var/folders/<2>/<long>/T/` patterns). Prior to this fix, every test run that exercised `scan-secrets` against a `cx-secrets-*/fixture.env` tempdir or initialized a `init-cxignore-*` project produced a persistent `[security] secrets.detected` or `[sre] service.down` bead — 58 such beads accumulated and re-surfaced as "stuck/stale open" on every doctor run. **All 58 noise beads closed in this PR with reason "test-fixture path".** Two regression tests in `tests/roles/gateway.test.mjs` lock the filter in place (one for the escalation path, one for `isTestFixturePath` shape coverage).

- **Resolved beads:** `construct-k6lc` (pre-push gate fix-forward) — verified resolved in v1.0.15 by `lib/hooks/pre-push-gate.mjs:144-169`, which now checks `headSha` from the failed CI run against `git rev-parse HEAD` and allows the push when HEAD has advanced past the failed SHA. `construct-ik8r` (docs audit) — work completed 2026-05-28 per its own notes. Both closed in this PR.

- **`construct install --yes` no longer crashes with `ReferenceError`.** Two bugs surfaced when simulating install in a clean environment: a missing `applyToEnv` import (referenced in the cheapest-provider branch but not imported from `lib/model-router.mjs`) and two references to an undefined `env` local (should have been `process.env`) in the post-install summary block at [lib/setup.mjs](lib/setup.mjs). Both now fixed; install completes cleanly through to the doctor pass.

- **`construct install` wires `~/.construct/lib` → `<install>/lib` automatically.** Claude Code's Stop hooks resolve commands under `$HOME/.construct/lib/hooks/*`; without that symlink every Stop hook fails silently (including `stop-notify`, which feeds the dashboard). The doctor surfaced "Claude Stop hooks broken: 8 hooks missing — symlink the install" and told users to run `ln -sfn` by hand. New [`ensureLibSymlink`](lib/setup.mjs) in `lib/setup.mjs` runs during `construct install`: creates the symlink on first install, leaves it alone if it already matches, replaces a stale symlink that points elsewhere, refuses to overwrite a real directory at the target. Five regression tests in `tests/setup.test.mjs` cover the matrix.

- **Test fixtures no longer pollute the developer's `.cx/contract-violations.jsonl`.** `validateHandoff` accepted a `repoRoot` option for resolving artifact paths but `logViolation` always wrote to whatever the cwd-based project-root resolver returned — so tests with a tmpdir `repoRoot` still logged into the real Construct project. `logViolation` now accepts a `repoRoot` extra (extracted from the persisted record), and `validateHandoff` threads it through. An explicit `repoRoot` bypasses the marker walk and writes directly under `<repoRoot>/.cx/contract-violations.jsonl`. `tests/contracts-postconditions.test.mjs` already passes the tmpdir `repoRoot`; the fix takes effect automatically. New exported `violationLogPath(repoRoot)` returns the resolved path for the doctor surface.

- **`construct <cmd> --help` no longer runs the command (bead `construct-x259`).** Per-command handlers in `bin/construct` silently ignored `--help` and executed their action. `construct stop --help` stopped services, `construct dev --help` started them, `construct init --help` scaffolded a project, `construct uninstall --help` almost ran the uninstall flow. The top-level dispatcher now intercepts `--help`/`-h` for every command (visible or internal) after handler resolution and prints a uniform help block rendered from the `CLI_COMMANDS` entry. The per-command guard in `lib/setup.mjs` is now redundant but harmless. New regression test `tests/functional/cli-help-safety.functional.test.mjs` asserts every command exits 0 with `--help`, prints its name, takes <5s, and does not mutate `HOME`.

- **`construct --all` works as a top-level flag.** The help footer advertised `construct --all` but the dispatcher only honored `--all` when combined with `--help`. Standalone `--all` / `-a` now route through `usage({ all: true })`.

- **`construct install --help` and the internal docs no longer call the command `init`.** `lib/setup.mjs` printed `Usage: construct init` and its file header described itself as "invoked by `construct init`" — install vs init confusion in a file dedicated to install. Help text and header reconciled. `CLI_COMMANDS` descriptions for `install` and `init` now lead with their boundary ("Machine setup (once per machine)" vs "Project setup (once per repo)"). `lib/install/first-invocation.mjs` SKIP_COMMANDS list now includes `install` (the actual handler key), `upgrade`, and `update` so the bootstrap probe never re-prompts during the very command meant to satisfy it.

- **28 hidden handlers in `bin/construct` now appear in `--all` or are explicitly marked internal.** `lib/cli-commands.mjs` grew entries for `ask`, `handoffs`, `feedback:record`, `feedback:history`, `roles:list`, `roles:set`, `docs:check`, `docs:verify`, `docs:update`, `docs:reconcile`, `docs:site`, `beads:stats`, `telemetry-setup`. Eleven internal-only handlers (`hook`, `seed-traces`, `migrate`, `dashboard:sync`, `init:update`, `lint:agents`, `lint:comments`, `lint:contracts`, `lint:research`, `lint:templates`, `evaluator:rubrics`, `activation:status`, `prune`, `overrides`, `resources`) carry `internal: true` and are hidden from `--help`, `--all`, and shell completions but stay callable. New regression test `tests/functional/cli-catalog-parity.functional.test.mjs` asserts that every handler has a catalog entry and every catalog entry has a handler.

- **Three stale doctor warnings cleaned up.**
  - *Contract violations* path. The warning pointed at `~/.cx/contract-violations.jsonl`, but the log moved to project-scope in v1.0.15 (`<project>/.cx/contract-violations.jsonl`). `bin/construct` now prints the resolved path via new `violationLogPath()` export from `lib/contracts/violation-log.mjs`.
  - *Pre-push bypasses* (v1.0.15 deleted the entire skip-var + bypass-log writer infrastructure, but the doctor check survived and surfaced stale entries forever). The check is removed; the orphaned `~/.construct/audit/prepush-bypass.log` is left in place (next cleanup tick may sweep it).
  - *VS Code settings missing — wait for support*. Roadmap status is not a doctor finding. The check no longer emits a line when settings.json is absent; it only confirms presence when the file exists.

- **`construct upgrade` and `construct update` sweep legacy agents and advance the cleanup stamp.** Without this, the doctor surfaced `legacy v1.0.<old> install — N stale agents` after every upgrade until the user manually ran `construct doctor --fix-legacy-agents`, and the per-command cleanup pass re-ran every CLI invocation because the stamp at `~/.construct/.cleanup-stamp` never advanced past the previous version. Both flows now run `construct doctor --fix-legacy-agents` (optional step — skipped on failure) and write the new version stamp via `writeVersionStamp` from `lib/maintenance/cleanup.mjs`.

- **Bare `construct` interactive menu reads from `CLI_COMMANDS` instead of a hardcoded list of 5 commands.** Replaces the divergent 5-command snippet in `showInteractiveMenu` with the same `usage()` rendering used by `--help`, prefixed by a context line ("Project: <name>") and suffixed by exactly one suggestion based on local state ("services not running" or "not a Construct project").

- **Unknown-command error suggests the closest match by prefix/substring scan.** `construct sycn` previously returned only "Unknown command"; the dispatcher now hints `Did you mean: construct sync?` when a single-letter typo collides cleanly.

## [1.0.15] - 2026-06-01

### Added

- **High-fidelity document ingestion via docling Python sidecar + whisper.cpp (bead `construct-bc43`).** Replaces the custom regex/CLI extractors in `lib/document-extract.mjs` for PDF, DOCX, XLSX, PPTX, RTF/DOC, and legacy iWork formats with a long-lived Python sidecar wrapping [docling](https://github.com/docling-project/docling) (IBM, MIT, donated to LF AI & Data early 2026). Provisioned on first use via [`uv`](https://github.com/astral-sh/uv) into `.cx/runtime/docling/.venv` with a pinned docling release; subsequent runs reuse the warmed sidecar (no per-call ~2s startup). Audio and video formats route through [`whisper.cpp`](https://github.com/ggml-org/whisper.cpp) (`brew install whisper-cpp` on macOS for Metal acceleration); the `base.en` model auto-downloads on first audio request. New API: `extractDocumentTextAsync(filePath)` returns `{ markdown, metadata, droppedInfo, ... }`. The sync `extractDocumentText` stays for backwards compat with the 9 existing callers. `construct ingest` gains `--strict` (fail on any extraction drops) and `--legacy-extractor` (regex path) flags. Ingested chunks land in a new content-addressed store at `.cx/ingest/<sha256>/{source,markdown,meta}.json`, indexed by `lib/knowledge/search.mjs` so dropped PDFs become searchable. New helpers: `lib/runtime/uv-bootstrap.mjs`, `lib/runtime/whisper-bootstrap.mjs`, `lib/document-extract/docling-{sidecar.py,client.mjs}`, `lib/document-extract/whisper-client.mjs`, `lib/ingest/{store,chunker,pipeline}.mjs`. 11 new tests cover chunker + store. README + MCP tool description updated to reflect actual fidelity (no more macOS-bias claim). Per 2025-2026 RAG consensus (docling = layout-aware MIT default; uv = Astral consensus Python tooling; whisper.cpp + Metal = ~10× RT on M-series; paragraph chunking with sentence overlap = high-recall default at low cost vs semantic chunking).

### Changed

- **Migrated every metadata-carrying markdown surface to YAML frontmatter; removed HTML-comment headers (bead `construct-onnm`).** Skills (141), personas (1), commands (30), and rules (46) all moved from the legacy `<!-- skills/<path>.md (Title) Description -->` HTML-comment header to spec-compliant YAML frontmatter (`name`, `description`, plus surface-specific keys preserved). The HTML-comment pattern was custom to Construct and predated Anthropic's Agent Skills spec (October 2025); it was the root cause of the markdown-bleed bug where table syntax in headers leaked into shipped descriptions (`description: "| Sev | User impact | ..."`). Now every consumer reads via a real YAML parser. Migration scripts: `scripts/migrate-skill-frontmatter.mjs` and `scripts/migrate-surface-frontmatter.mjs`. Sync layer (`lib/sync/skill-frontmatter.mjs`) refactored to read YAML at source with backwards-compat fallback to extraction, using an in-tree minimal YAML reader so the production sync path stays zero-dep (runs from isolated cpSync'd tmpdirs without `node_modules`); the migration script and validator use `js-yaml` for richer needs. Validator (`lib/validators/skills.mjs`) now enforces Anthropic spec: kebab-case `name` (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$`, ≤64 chars, no reserved tokens), `description` ≤1024 chars with required "use when" trigger clause, parseable YAML (catches the bleed bug definitively). All 53 generic role descriptions (`Anti-pattern guidance for the [Role] role`) replaced with programmatically generated role-specific triggers from role + applies_to. Comment-lint rule (`lib/comment-lint.mjs`) accepts YAML frontmatter with `description:` as a valid file header for markdown files. `prompt-composer.readPromptBody` strips leading YAML frontmatter so sync no longer emits double-frontmatter blocks for personas. 17 new validator tests cover name/description/XML/duplicate constraints. Per Anthropic Agent Skills best-practices guidance (October 2025).

- **Every MCP tool dispatched by `lib/mcp/server.mjs` is now advertised in `ListToolsRequestSchema` (bead `construct-efwp`).** An audit found nine tools dispatched but not registered: the entire `workflow_*` family (`workflow_init`, `workflow_add_task`, `workflow_update_task`, `workflow_needs_main_input`, `workflow_validate`, `workflow_status`, `workflow_contract_validate`, `workflow_import_plan`) plus `cx_trace_telemetry`. LLM clients introspecting via `tools/list` could not discover them, and callers who knew the name passed args with no schema enforcement.
