Added registration entries with `type: object` `inputSchema` and `required` field lists derived from the actual handler signatures. New regression test `tests/mcp-tools-list-coverage.test.mjs` pins the rule both ways: every dispatched name must be advertised, every advertised name must be dispatched, every entry must declare `type: object` with a non-empty description. Closes the discovery + transport-level validation gap surfaced by the prior contract audit; per MCP Best Practices Guide (2025) and the NSA MCP Cybersecurity Information Sheet (May 2026) on per-tool schema as a trust boundary.

- **Binary postconditions for the five producer roles are now actually enforced on the workflow runtime path.** The runtime check existed in a parallel module with zero production callers; it is now wired into `validateHandoff` in `lib/contracts/validate.mjs`. The validator refuses to be silently skipped: producers with rules must supply a packet, or the call is itself a contract violation. Binary postconditions fire regardless of whether the producer→consumer contract is registered in `contracts.json`, so an unregistered consumer cannot bypass the producer's invariants. The `CONSTRUCT_CONTRACT_ENFORCEMENT` env-var override is removed; enforcement is hard-default `block`. Three function renames eliminate name shadowing across the contract surface: `lib/handoffs/contract.mjs:validateHandoff` → `validateHandoffFile`, `lib/contracts/validate.mjs:validatePostconditions` → `validateArtifactPostconditions`, `lib/specialists/postconditions.mjs:validatePostconditions` → `validateBinaryPostconditions`. Aligned with CISA Secure-by-Design Pledge (May 2024) hard-default principle, NSA MCP Cybersecurity Information Sheet (May 2026) on naming-collision risk, and MCP Best Practices Guide (2025) on no-silent-skip at the tool boundary. Follow-up: `workflow_contract_validate` is not currently advertised in the MCP `tools/list` registration — adding the schema with explicit `packet` validation is tracked separately.

- **Contract violation log moved to `lib/contracts/violation-log.mjs`; dead `specialist-contracts-enforce.mjs` module removed.** The append-only tamper-evident log was previously owned by `enforcePacket` in a parallel contract module that had zero production callers (only tests + the read-side `recentViolations` surface for `construct doctor`). The chain-hashed JSONL writer + reader port over verbatim — existing `.cx/contract-violations.jsonl` chains keep reading without migration. Each record now also carries a monotonic `sequence` field so whole-tail truncation is detectable on `verifyChain()`, not just modification (sequence-number gap detection per current tamper-evident audit log practice). The dead module and its two test files are removed; CI path filter updated.

### Security

- **Eliminate shell command injection in `lib/ollama-manager.mjs` (bead `construct-w5kv`).** Every shell-out (`ollama pull/rm`, `curl GET/POST` to the Ollama HTTP API, `brew install/services`, `sleep`) was rewritten from `execSync(template-string)` to `execFileSync(bin, [...args])` array form. JSON bodies are piped via `--data-binary @-` over stdin instead of embedded in a shell-quoted argument. User-supplied model names from argv (e.g. `args[0]` in `pull`, `rm`, `show`, `test` subcommands) can no longer break out of the command.

- **Eliminate shell injection in MCP `searchSkills` tool (bead `construct-z64u`).** `lib/mcp/tools/skills.mjs` previously called `execSync(\`rg -i "${pattern.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}" "${skillsDir}"\`)` where `pattern` is a model-controlled MCP tool argument and the double-quote escape left `$(...)`, backticks, and backslash sequences exploitable. Rewritten to `execFileSync('rg', ['-i', pattern, skillsDir])` (and the same for the `grep` fallback) — pattern is a discrete argv entry, no shell intermediary.

- **Dashboard refuses non-loopback bind without configured auth (bead `construct-qrrh`).** `lib/server/index.mjs` previously flipped `BIND_HOST` to `0.0.0.0` whenever `NODE_ENV=production` was set, silently disabling the localhost-trust path used by the credentials and config endpoints. Default is now `127.0.0.1` regardless of `NODE_ENV`. New `assertSafeBind()` writes a startup error and exits 1 if `BIND_HOST` is non-loopback and `isAuthConfigured()` is false — operators have to make the exposure decision explicitly.

- **Bead IDs validated before reaching the `bd` shell boundary (bead `construct-gpa4`).** New `assertBeadId(id)` exported from `lib/beads-client.mjs` enforces `/^[a-z]+-[a-z0-9]+$/` on every ID. Applied in `showIssue`, `claimIssue`, `closeIssue`. `lib/tracking-surfaces.mjs:closeBeadsFromPrRefs` validates per-ID before `spawnSync('bd', ['close', id, ...])`; malformed IDs (from a crafted PR body) bail to a structured `invalid-bead-id` error instead of corrupting the beads log.

- **`session-start` no longer logs `JIRA_BASE_URL` value to session output (bead `construct-qqre`).** `lib/hooks/session-start.mjs:274` now logs `"Jira: configured"` instead of the literal URL. The audit-trail captures session output verbatim; the URL is non-secret in most setups but defense-in-depth says log presence, not value.

### Fixed

- **Dead queue-timeout check in `lib/beads-client.mjs` (bead `construct-myxv`).** The condition `if (Date.now() - startWait + maxQueueWaitMs > maxQueueWaitMs)` simplified algebraically to `if (Date.now() > startWait)` — always true on first call. Effect: every entry to the queue path returned a fake `"Timeout waiting for lock"` instantly without ever queuing or polling, and callers could not distinguish lock-failed from queue-full. Corrected to `if (Date.now() - startWait > maxQueueWaitMs)`. The polling loop at `lib/beads-client.mjs:277` is now reachable.

- **Stale `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_COMMENT_LINT` bypass advertisement removed from settings template (bead `construct-a2rl`).** `platforms/claude/settings.template.json:154` described a bypass env var that the hook (`lib/hooks/comment-lint.mjs:10`) explicitly does not implement, and that CLAUDE.md's "no `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_*` on enforcement gates" rule forbids. Replaced with `"No bypass — repair the policy if it fires wrong."` `rg 'CONSTRUCT_SKIP_|CONSTRUCT_ALLOW_|CONSTRUCT_QUIET_' platforms/claude/settings.template.json` now returns zero hits.

- **`writeContextState` is now atomic across concurrent writers (bead `construct-uiva`).** `lib/context-state.mjs` previously used plain `writeFileSync` for both `.cx/context.json` and `.cx/context.md`; four hooks plus `lib/sandbox.mjs` could race and leave half-written files. New `atomicWrite(target, content)` writes to `<target>.<pid>.<timestamp>.tmp` and `renameSync`s into place — POSIX `rename(2)` is atomic on the same filesystem, so observers either see the prior content or the new content, never an interleaved write.

- **`embed/daemon.mjs` probe failures surfaced to daemon log (bead `construct-ly0i`).** The inner `catch { /* probe call failed */ }` around Anthropic/OpenRouter probe `fetch` calls now writes `"[embed] Probe call failed: <message>"` to stderr. Operators can distinguish a 0ms success from a hard failure via the daemon log without changing the no-crash behavior.

- **`observation-store` surfaces cap-eviction to the caller (bead `construct-1pyl`).** `lib/observation-store.mjs:writeIndex` and `writeVectors` now return `{ dropped }`; `addObservation` propagates it as `record.capDropped` and emits a one-line stderr warning when the cap is reached. The `cap-drops.jsonl` cumulative log and the `construct doctor` "no recent drops" surface are unchanged.

### Changed

- **End-to-end best-practice audit (audit-2026-06-01 batch).** Three Explore agents in parallel surveyed every CLI subcommand, every `lib/hooks/*.mjs`, the specialist handoff chain, and the runtime/data surfaces (session-start, audit, embed, beads, observations, dashboard, providers). Twenty-one raw findings, ten verified, eleven false positives (one false-positive on `await emitEvent` ordering in `config-protection.mjs`; one mis-scoped skip-env-var claim covering bootstrap helpers outside the hook surface; two unit-confusion findings on `@p95ms` timeouts; remainder duplicates or already-fixed in v1.0.13). The verified ten are listed under Security / Fixed above. Audit summary in `.claude/plans/begin-crawling-the-non-radiant-harp.md`; one follow-up bead (`construct-an0s`) for the specialist-contracts postcondition asymmetry left open for a deeper trace.

### Removed

- **Em-dash prose rule and `lint:prose` gate retired.** The rule never enforced anything actionable; the script's `BANNED` array contained exactly one regex and its CI step only fired on diffs touching `docs/**`, so most em-dash drift was grandfathered. Removing the rule removes a gate that under-fired and surfaces no real correctness signal. Deleted: `scripts/lint-prose.mjs`, `.proseignore`, the `lint:prose` entry in `package.json`, the "prose policy" step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, and the `lint:prose` entry in `tests/ci-parity.test.mjs`'s `CI_ONLY_SUBCOMMANDS` array. Rule text removed from `CLAUDE.md` ("Critical rules"), `docs/STYLE.md` (Punctuation, Tooling, Exceptions sections), and `CONTRIBUTING.md` (eight-check list collapsed to seven; "No em-dashes" line dropped from the Tone section). Stale references cleaned up in `docs/maintenance/release-and-deploy.md` (three lines), `tests/AUDIT.md` (the lint-prose unit-test gap), and the file-header comment in `lib/flavors/loader.mjs`. Existing em-dashes in committed docs are left in place.

- **Three unused JSON schemas removed: `schemas/{flavor,registry,workflow}.schema.json`.** Zero consumers anywhere in `lib/`, `scripts/`, `commands/`, `apps/`, `tests/`, or `specialists/`. `lint:profiles` validates flavors via the hand-rolled `validateFlavor()` in `lib/flavors/loader.mjs`, not the schema; the registry and workflow schemas were never wired to a validator. `schemas/profile.schema.json` stays — it is consumed by `lib/profiles/validate-custom.mjs` and `lib/profiles/lifecycle.mjs`.

- **Three orphaned rule files removed: `rules/common/{agents,performance,development-workflow}.md`.** None were in `specialists/policy-inventory.json`; none were referenced from any specialist prompt or runtime code. `agents.md` and `performance.md` were generic guidance not specific to Construct; `development-workflow.md` enumerated TDD/commit steps already covered by `rules/common/{testing,code-review,git-workflow}.md` and the `CLAUDE.md` "Documentation is mandatory" section. The cross-reference in `docs/runbooks/day-2-operations.md` re-pointed at CLAUDE.md; the cross-reference in `rules/common/review-before-change.md` dropped.

### Changed

- **Two surviving rules wired into `specialists/policy-inventory.json`.** `rules/common/review-before-change.md` registered as `review-before-change` (honor-system, persona prompt) — it is the artifact-audit discipline cited from `STRATEGY.md` and pairs with `framing.md`. `rules/common/efficiency.md` registered as `session-efficiency` (deterministic, `lib/read-tracker-store.mjs` + session-start digest) — the file already declares its enforcement machinery in the header comment; the inventory entry catches it up. `construct policy show` now lists 24 policies (was 22).

## [1.0.14] - 2026-05-31

### Fixed

- **Release gate `npm audit` scope aligned with CI.** v1.0.13's release workflow (run `26722200292`) failed at `release:check / npm audit` because the gate ran `npm audit --audit-level=high` against the entire monorepo, including the `apps/docs` workspace, which carries a HIGH advisory in `next-mdx-remote@5` (GHSA-g4xw-jxrg-5f6m). The npm-published package's `files` list excludes `apps/docs`, so this gate was over-broad: it refused a clean CLI release on a vulnerability that ships exclusively to the docs site. Release gate now runs `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high --workspaces=false` — exactly the audit invocation that already gates every CI run. Apps/docs migration to `next-mdx-remote` v6 tracks as `construct-jymn`; a follow-up will add an audit step to `.github/workflows/pages.yml` so the docs-site deploy gates on its own audit. v1.0.13's tag remains on the remote without published artifacts; v1.0.14 ships the same content of v1.0.13 plus this gate fix.

## [1.0.13] - 2026-05-31

### Added

- **Tracking surfaces stay current by design — no more manual catch-up of `.cx/context.md`, `plan.md`, or merged-but-still-open beads (bead `construct-074s`).** New `lib/tracking-surfaces.mjs` exports four pure-utility functions that own *what* to refresh; new hooks own *when*. `refreshContextMd({ rootDir })` rewrites the managed `## Active Work` / `## Recent Decisions` / `## Architecture Notes` sections of `.cx/context.md` from recent observations (`.cx/observations/`), commits, open beads, and recently-closed beads while preserving every other user-authored section; stamps `lastRefreshAt` + per-section counts into `.cx/context.json`. `syncPlanFile({ rootDir })` is a stable wrapper around the existing `syncPlanWithBeads()` in `lib/beads-automation.mjs` — the implementation already worked; it just needed to fire automatically. `archivePlanIfLanded({ rootDir })` retires a `plan.md` when every referenced bead has status `closed` AND the plan has been idle for ≥1 h, copying the body into `.cx/handoffs/<date>-plan-landed.md` with a "Landed" header and resetting `plan.md` to the template. `closeBeadsFromPrRefs({ prNumber, mergeCommitSha, cwd })` parses a merged PR's `Refs:` / `Closes:` / `Fixes:` lines via `gh pr view` and closes every open `construct-XXX` bead with reason `Merged via PR #N (<sha>)`; idempotent on already-closed beads. Two new hooks wire the triggers: `lib/hooks/session-tracking-refresh.mjs` (Stop) runs `archivePlanIfLanded` → `syncPlanFile` → `refreshContextMd` in that order — archive first so the still-fresh idle mtime is intact before `syncPlanFile`'s write bumps it; `lib/hooks/post-merge-tracking.mjs` (PostToolUse / Bash) detects a successful `gh pr merge` and calls `closeBeadsFromPrRefs`, resolving the PR number from the command argument or, for bare `gh pr merge --auto`, from the most recent merge commit subject's `#N` suffix. Both are best-effort with structured failure results, `logHookFailure` swallows, and Stop-hook wall-clock budget guards so the session-close path is never blocked. `tests/hooks-budget.test.mjs` gains one new contract: every tracking-refresh Stop hook must declare `@maxBlockingScope none`. Coverage: 12 unit cases in `tests/tracking-surfaces.test.mjs` (managed-section preservation, in-progress bead pull-through, context.json stamping, archive guard rails for recently-touched / still-open / no-refs, idempotent close, gh-unavailable degradation), 4 functional cases in `tests/functional/post-merge-tracking.functional.test.mjs` (happy path with multiple refs, failed-merge no-op, non-merge Bash no-op, PR-number resolution from merge commit when `--auto`), 5 functional cases in `tests/functional/session-tracking-refresh.functional.test.mjs` (non-Construct-project no-op, Active Work update, lastRefreshAt stamping, end-to-end archive + reset, bd-unavailable resilience). Closes the root-cause loop: `detectBeadsDrift()` in `lib/beads/drift.mjs` already surfaced merge-drift in `construct doctor`; remediation now happens upstream at the merge event itself.

### Fixed

- **Audit-trail `prev_line_hash` chain preserved across rotation boundaries (bead `construct-hufb`).** When `appendBounded` rotated `audit-trail.jsonl` (or `audit-reads.jsonl`), the new active file started empty; `readPrevLineHash()` saw `size === 0` and returned `null`; the first record on a fresh segment had `prev_line_hash: null` and broke the tamper-evident chain at every rotation boundary. New `lib/logging/rotate.mjs` export `readLastLineAcrossSegments(filePath)` falls back to the most recent rotated segment (decompressing gzipped segments via `gunzipSync`) when the active file is empty, and walks the whole buffer to recover the last newline-bounded record (the prior 2 KB tail read truncated audit records larger than the window — content_hash fields routinely push records past 50 KB). Both `lib/hooks/audit-trail.mjs` and `lib/hooks/audit-reads.mjs` route through it. Toothpaste sweep: unused `statSync` imports removed from both hooks; the shared buffer-walk logic was factored into a single `lastLineOfBuffer` helper. Coverage: 5 new unit cases in `tests/logging-rotate.test.mjs` (active-file tail, empty-active fallback, gzip decompress fallback, no-data null, highest-index preference) and one end-to-end functional case in `tests/functional/audit-trail-chain-integrity.functional.test.mjs` that spawns the real `audit-trail.mjs` 60 times under a tiny 1 KB cap, forces multiple rotations (audit-trail caps at `maxSegments: 4` gzipped survivors), and asserts the replayed chain has zero breaks across every surviving record.

### Changed

- **Docs reconciled with the post-cleanup hook surface; canonical hooks doc added.** New `docs/concepts/hooks.mdx` is the single source of truth for the hook surface: the no-skip-vars principle, the header schema, the bounded-write channel rules, the project-scope rule, the exit-code convention, the cross-host caveat (hooks fire only under Claude Code), and a table of every surviving hook with its lifecycle / matcher / purpose. `docs/concepts/gates-and-enforcement.mdx` rewritten — the "every blocking gate has an env-var bypass" framing was the wrong direction; updated to "gates fire unconditionally; correctness is repaired at the policy, not bypassed at the call site." `docs/cookbook/fix-a-policy-violation.mdx` "emergency bypass" lines replaced with "fix the policy" guidance for every gate. `docs/concepts/architecture.mdx` Layer 1/2 entries updated to drop deleted skip-var references and reflect the post-shrink pre-push behavior. `docs/maintenance/release-and-deploy.md` push-time "Bypass with CONSTRUCT_SKIP_PREPUSH=1" replaced with "fix the underlying issue / advance HEAD." `docs/hooks-inventory.md` rows for `read-tracker.mjs` and `env-check.mjs` removed; `audit-reads.mjs` and `session-start.mjs` rows reflect their absorbed responsibilities. `docs/hooks-deprecated.md` gains formal entries for both consolidations with the lifecycle + behavior + new-home columns the rest of the file follows. `docs/reference/hooks.md` regenerated via `construct docs:site` to drop the stale `read-tracker` row.

- **Hook header convention enforced + shared input helper + new budget contracts.** Every hook in `lib/hooks/*.mjs` now declares `@lifecycle`, `@matcher`, and `@exits` annotations in its file-header docstring (or `@unwired` for the two hooks not referenced from `platforms/claude/settings.template.json` — `proactive-activation.mjs` and `rule-verifier.mjs`). The schema and `@exits` claim are derived from the actual `process.exit(...)` calls in the file, so the documented contract matches the runtime behavior. `tests/hooks-budget.test.mjs` gains five new contracts: every hook has `@lifecycle` (or `@unwired`); every hook has `@matcher` (or `@unwired`); every `@exits` claim matches the actual `process.exit(2)` presence; every `settings.template.json` hook command resolves to a present `.mjs` file; every wired `.mjs` is referenced from settings or carries `@unwired`. New `lib/hooks/_lib/input.mjs` exports `readHookInput({ env, argv })` normalizing stdin JSON + `TOOL_INPUT_FILE_PATH` env + `--input-json` argv into one shape; `registry-sync.mjs` and `model-fallback.mjs` migrate onto it (`registry-sync.mjs` was env-only; `model-fallback.mjs` had its own private stdin reader). Toothpaste sweep: `model-fallback.mjs` no longer imports an unused `readFileSync`.

- **Hook family consolidation: 35 hooks → 33. `read-tracker.mjs` folded into `audit-reads.mjs`; `env-check.mjs` folded into `session-start.mjs`.** Both merges close clean lifecycle matches; both target hooks gained the absorbed responsibility and the standalone files (and their `settings.template.json` entries) are gone. `audit-reads.mjs` now has two stages: stage 1 (always-on) upserts `~/.cx/file-hashes.json` so `edit-guard` can detect staleness on a subsequent Edit and emits a read-tracker delta for the efficiency snapshot; stage 2 (`CONSTRUCT_AUDIT_READS=1` opt-in, unchanged) writes the tamper-evident audit chain to `<project>/.cx/audit-reads.jsonl`. Splitting the always-on hash store from the opt-in audit log fixes a quiet correctness bug — the prior split meant edit-guard's staleness check broke when audit-reads was off, because nothing else maintained the hash store. `session-start.mjs` absorbs `env-check.mjs`'s `.env.example` ↔ `.env` ↔ `process.env` comparison into a single `buildEnvCheckNote(cwd)` helper appended to the existing session-start output; one fewer SessionStart process spawn. The third candidate in the plan (`edit-accumulator` → `edit-guard`) is **not** consolidated: edit-guard runs PreToolUse to validate `old_string`; edit-accumulator runs PostToolUse to track typecheck queue and edit counter. Different lifecycles; merging them would require lifecycle bridging that fights the existing structure. The honest call is to leave both standalone — they're separate concerns, not near-duplicates. Doctor now lists 33 hooks; every `settings.template.json` entry resolves to a present file (asserted by a one-shot sweep of every `node "$HOME/.construct/lib/hooks/<file>.mjs"` reference).

- **Five hook audit channels now bounded; `audit-trail.jsonl` is project-scoped; agent-tracker handoffs are observable via the role event-bus.** `lib/logging/rotate.mjs` `LIMITS` registers two new channels: `audit-trail` (50 MB, 4 segments, gzip) for `lib/hooks/audit-trail.mjs` and `edit-accumulator` (5 MB, 2 segments) for the pending-typecheck queue in `lib/hooks/edit-accumulator.mjs`. `lib/hooks/dep-audit.mjs` was writing direct to `warn-flags.txt` with `appendFileSync` despite the existing `bash-warn-flags` channel — fixed to route through `appendBounded`. `audit-trail.mjs` migrated from `~/.cx/audit-trail.jsonl` (global) to `resolveProjectScopedPath('audit-trail.jsonl')` so mutations in project A no longer mix into project B's tamper-evident chain — matches the construct-wwf2 pattern already applied to `audit-reads.jsonl`. `agent-tracker.mjs` continues to write the `next:cx-<role>` handoff into `~/.cx/role-pending.jsonl` (the source of truth, kept user-scope because a handoff can legitimately span projects) and now also emits a `handoff.received` event via `lib/roles/event-bus.mjs` so dispatches are observable through the same surface as every other lifecycle event. Toothpaste sweep on touched files: `audit-trail.mjs` swapped its silent `catch` for `logHookFailure` in three phases (parse / hash / append); `edit-accumulator.mjs` swapped string-concatenated paths for `path.join` + `path.extname` and added `logHookFailure` calls; `dep-audit.mjs` migrated to `node:`-prefixed imports and shared `logHookFailure`; `test-watch.mjs` removed a dead `appendFileSync(flakeTrackerPath, '')` no-op + an unnecessary dynamic re-import of `node:fs.writeFileSync`; `agent-tracker.mjs` replaced `console.error` with `logHookFailure` and pruned an unused `existsSync` import. Coverage: 3 new cases in `tests/logging-rotate.test.mjs` (audit-trail and edit-accumulator channels register + rotate; the registry contract now enumerates 12 channels).

### Removed

- **All hook skip / quiet env vars deleted; auditing a mechanism that shouldn't exist is wasted code.** `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_COMMENT_LINT`, `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_PR_LINT`, `CONSTRUCT_ALLOW_CLAUDE_PUSH`, `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_PREPUSH`, `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_DOCS`, `CONSTRUCT_QUIET_REGISTRY_REMINDER` are gone. The earlier `chore/pre-push-shrink-and-ci-concurrency` branch added an audit log + protected-branch refusal + doctor warning around these — treating bypass-audit as best practice. It isn't. Auditing the skip tells you who skipped; it doesn't address why a quality gate fires when it shouldn't. A skip env var on a quality gate is an admission the policy is wrong, the gate is over-scoped, or the team will route around it. The principle: hooks fire unconditionally. If a check fires wrong, fix the check. Files retired with the skip vars: the `logBypass` writer in `lib/hooks/pre-push-gate.mjs`, the inline-env-var parser (only existed to honor bypasses), the doctor "Pre-push bypasses in last 7 days" check in `bin/construct`, `~/.construct/audit/prepush-bypass.log`, `tests/hooks/pre-push-gate-bypass.test.mjs`, and `lib/policy/unified-gates.mjs` (whole-file dead infrastructure built around 4 hypothetical bypass-able policies + a `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_ALL_GATES` kill switch; not referenced from anywhere except a broken `policy:list` command that imported a non-existent `formatPolicyList`). The notice-quiet (`CONSTRUCT_QUIET_REGISTRY_REMINDER`) was the only var with a legitimate use case (silent reminders in CI); it's replaced with auto-detection (`CI=true` or `NODE_ENV=test`). `process.stderr.isTTY` is intentionally NOT used as a signal — Claude Code captures hook stderr rather than passing it through to a TTY, so a TTY check would silently kill the reminder in real interactive sessions. New regression test `tests/hooks/no-skip-vars.test.mjs` greps `lib/hooks/*.mjs` for `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_` / `CONSTRUCT_ALLOW_` / `CONSTRUCT_QUIET_` and fails on any match outside an explicit (initially empty) allowlist. Coverage: also rewrote `tests/hooks/registry-sync-reminder.test.mjs` (5 cases) for the auto-detect path. Stale agents/specialists rename fixed at `bin/construct` for `policy show` while touching the file.

### Added

- **Profile `capabilities.intake` archetype: init scaffolding + runtime CLI + manifest dedup + attribution provenance + atomic poll lock.** Closes issue #104. Profiles whose `capabilities.intake.inbox` is `true` (curated `rnd` and `research`; `operations` and `creative` stay opt-in) cause `construct init` to scaffold `inbox/` at the project root with a permissive `.gitignore` (`*` plus `!.gitignore` — raw drops never enter source), seed `.cx/intake/manifest.json` with the empty SHA-256 dedup shape, and flip a new `includeArchetypeInbox: true` flag in `.cx/intake-config.json`. The flag is symmetric with the existing `includeProjectInbox` / `includeDocsIntake` toggles, so `parentDirs` stays reserved for user-explicit opt-ins (preserving the regression guard from `tests/init-intake-defaults.test.mjs`). `resolveInboxDirs` honors the new flag. `capabilities.intake.attribution: true` adds a two-field provenance stamp (`createdBy` from `git config user.name <user.email>`, `createdByAgent` from `CONSTRUCT_AGENT_ID || CLAUDE_AGENT_ID`) to `AGENTS.md`, `plan.md`, `.cx/context.md` (markdown frontmatter) and `.cx/context.json` (top-level fields), with `CONSTRUCT_ATTRIBUTION_DISABLE=1` as the opt-out for tests and privacy-sensitive setups. `gatherAttribution({ cwd })` scopes the git-config lookup to the project root so non-spawned callers (the daemon, tests) get the right identity. New runtime CLI `construct intake process [--dry-run] [--wait[=<sec>]]`: a thin one-shot wrapper around the same `InboxWatcher.poll()` the daemon runs, exits when the queue is empty. **Concurrency is closed at the source, not papered over at the CLI.** `InboxWatcher.poll()` now wraps its body in `acquirePollLock` / `releasePollLock` (`lib/intake/poll-lock.mjs`, pid-stamped lockfile at `.cx/runtime/inbox-poll.lock`, stale detection via PID liveness and timeoutAt — API shape mirrors `lib/beads-lock.mjs` for cohesion). Two concurrent pollers — the embed daemon and a manual `intake process`, two parallel daemons, two CLI invocations — serialize cleanly; the second caller fails fast with `POLL_LOCK_BUSY` (exit 2 with an error naming the holder pid/actor/startedAt and surfacing `--wait` / `construct down` as remediation) or blocks for up to `--wait=<sec>` seconds. `--dry-run` skips the lock entirely. Dedup is layered: the existing path+mtime state file is the fast path; SHA-256 manifest check on top catches identical content under a renamed path. `InboxWatcher.poll` records the SHA, source path, intake id, and attribution on each successful ingest. Intake packets written to `.cx/intake/pending/` carry `createdBy` / `createdByAgent` / `createdAt` when the manifest is present. New helpers: `lib/intake/manifest.mjs` (`loadManifest` / `saveManifest` / `recordFile` / `hasFile` / `sha256Of`, atomic write-rename so a crash mid-write cannot half-write the manifest); `lib/intake/attribution.mjs` (`gatherAttribution` / `stampAttribution` / `touchAttribution`); `lib/intake/poll-lock.mjs` (`acquirePollLock` / `releasePollLock` / `readPollLock` / `isPollLockStale`). `lib/doc-stamp.mjs` `stampFrontmatter()` now accepts an optional `attribution` arg and preserves the original `created_by` on re-stamp by a different author, writing `last_modified_by` instead. Also fixes a pre-existing bug in `lib/init-unified.mjs:815` where re-running `construct init --profile=<id>` against an existing `construct.config.json` wrote the loader's wrapper object back to disk (failed schema validation on `version: required`) — the code now persists the original `.raw` JSON with only the profile field updated, so user customization survives idempotent re-init. Coverage: `tests/intake-manifest.test.mjs` (7 cases — empty contract, hashing determinism, idempotent record, corrupted-file recovery, atomic write), `tests/intake-attribution.test.mjs` (6 cases — disabled path, env-precedence, stamp/touch), `tests/intake-poll-lock.test.mjs` (9 cases — acquire/release, foreign-pid release no-op, fail-fast on live holder, stale-pid cleanup, stale-timeout cleanup, wait-then-acquire, predicate behavior, corrupt-file recovery), `tests/doc-stamp.test.mjs` adds 3 attribution cases (initial stamp, re-stamp preserves created_by, no-attribution stays clean), `tests/functional/init-intake-archetype.functional.test.mjs` (5 cases — scaffold + manifest + .gitignore shape, AGENTS.md attribution, attribution disable env, idempotent re-run preserves customized .gitignore, context.json fields populated), `tests/functional/intake-process-archetype.functional.test.mjs` (8 cases — `--dry-run` lists without ingesting, lock-held fail-fast with pid in message, `--wait` waits and acquires after release, stale-lock cleanup, `--dry-run` unaffected by lock, `InboxWatcher.poll` records SHA + attribution, identical-content dedup across renamed paths, intake packets carry attribution, **two parallel polls serialize through the lock — no double-ingest**).

- **Automated main → staging sync workflow** (`.github/workflows/sync-main-to-staging.yml`). Every push to `main` opens a `chore/sync-main-to-staging-<sha>` PR against `staging` via `peter-evans/create-pull-request`. Closes the drift window after a release squash-merge: previously a release PR (e.g. PR #105) would land on `main` but `staging` would have to hand-pick the merge back. The workflow checks `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD` first and no-ops if `staging` already contains `main`. Conflict path: workflow fails with an actionable error pointing at the local resolution steps; branch protection on `staging` stays intact (no direct push from CI). `workflow_dispatch` trigger supported for one-shot alignment.

### Changed

- **Pre-push hooks shrunk to local-only signals; CI is the source of truth.** Both `lib/hooks/pre-push-gate.mjs` (Claude Code PreToolUse) and `.beads/hooks/pre-push` (git native) previously re-ran the full CI matrix locally on every push — `npm test`, `npm run build`, `npm audit`, `construct evals retrieval`, `docs:verify`, `docs:update --check`, `lint:comments`, `lint:agents`, `lint:contracts`, `lint:prose`, `lint:profiles`. Wall time ~65s. The duplication trained the team to use a daily escape hatch, which is the gate-is-skipped-by-default failure mode. Hooks now do only what only the developer's machine can know: refuse `claude/*` branch pushes (Claude Code hook), block re-pushing the exact SHA CI just rejected (both hooks via `gh run list`), lint the PR body on `gh pr create` / `gh pr edit` (Claude Code hook — body doesn't exist until the PR is created). New wall time: ~2s. Test/build/audit/evals/lint coverage is unchanged — every check still runs in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- **CI concurrency cancellation on PR pushes.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` declares `concurrency: { group: ci-${{ workflow }}-${{ pull_request.number || ref }}, cancel-in-progress: ${{ event_name == 'pull_request' }} }`. Force-pushes to an open PR now cancel the superseded run instead of letting it complete in parallel. Pushes to `main` never cancel — every merged commit gets its own complete run for audit + release purposes.
- **ci-parity test contract inverted.** `tests/ci-parity.test.mjs` previously enforced that every `bin/construct lint:*` invocation in CI also appeared in both local pre-push hooks (the symmetric-gate anti-pattern). The new contract asserts the opposite: `docs:update`, `docs:verify`, `lint:comments`, `lint:agents`, `lint:contracts`, `lint:prose`, `lint:profiles`, `lint:templates`, `evals`, `gates:audit`, `doctor` must NOT appear in either local hook. A sanity check confirms CI still invokes each one (so we didn't drop a check entirely). `dashboard:sync --build` remains a CI-only invocation by the same asymmetry as before.

### Fixed

- **Release-gate test no longer reads polluted real-HOME state.** `tests/functional/release-gate.functional.test.mjs` invoked `construct doctor` against `process.env.HOME` unchanged, so a dev box with legacy v1.0.10 `~/.claude/agents/cx-*.md` files (regenerated mid-suite by `construct sync`) deterministically failed the parity check. The doctor subtest now uses an isolated tmpdir HOME, matching the pattern in `tests/sync-contract.test.mjs:43-78`. Other subtests are unchanged — they don't read user-scope state.
- **Doctor parity check now distinguishes "legacy v1.0.10 install" from "real drift."** `lib/parity.mjs:checkParity()` previously hard-failed when extra `cx-*` agents appeared at user scope, but that's the expected state during a v1.0.10 → v1.0.13+ upgrade (v1.0.10 populated all 28 specialists at user scope before the two-tier sync contract moved them to project scope). The check now reclassifies drift to `status: 'legacy-install'` when *every* extra matches the registry's `cx-*` roster, soft-warns with the `--fix-legacy-agents` hint, and lets the overall doctor exit 0. A single non-registry extra (typo, user-authored file) still hard-fails as `drift`. Same soft-warn applied to the standalone "legacy cx-* files at user scope" doctor counter in `bin/construct:495-501`. Coverage: 2 new cases in `tests/parity.test.mjs` (legacy roster + mixed-with-unknown), 1 updated case (`cx-orphan` instead of `cx-engineer` to pin the still-drifts-on-unknown branch).
- **GitHub Pages deploy no longer fails on workspace-package resolution.** `apps/docs` depends on the local workspace package `@cx/ui` (`packages/cx-ui`). Root `npm ci` resolves it via the `workspaces` config in `package.json`. The Pages workflow ran a redundant second install — `npm --prefix apps/docs install` — that was scoped to `apps/docs/` only, ignoring workspaces, so npm tried to fetch `@cx/ui@*` from the public registry and got a 404. The fix removes the redundant install step from `.github/workflows/pages.yml`; the subsequent `npm --prefix apps/docs run build` only invokes a script and doesn't need its own install. New CI guard `docs site build` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the apps/docs build on every PR that touches `apps/docs/**`, `packages/cx-ui/**`, `docs/**`, or `.github/workflows/pages.yml` — catches workspace-resolution drift before merge instead of after.
- **Dashboard drift check is now correct in CI and non-duplicated in local pre-push gates.** Three-part fix:
  - **CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:153`): the `dashboard bundle drift` step now runs `node ./bin/construct dashboard:sync --build` (one command — builds, copies into `lib/server/static/`, verifies parity). Earlier the step ran `npm --prefix apps/dashboard run build:next` followed by `dashboard:sync --check`, but `build:next` is the Next.js-only script — it never invokes the post-build copy into `lib/server/static/`, so the subsequent `--check` always tripped on a fresh checkout where the destination is gitignored.
  - **Local gates** (`.beads/hooks/pre-push:104`, `lib/hooks/pre-push-gate.mjs:239`): the standalone dashboard-drift job was removed. Both gates run `npm test`, which includes `tests/functional/dashboard-build.functional.test.mjs` — that test already builds the dashboard end-to-end and asserts on the static export. Running a second `next build` in the same gate races the first on `apps/dashboard/.next/` (intermittent ENOENT on the 500.html rename, ENOTEMPTY on cache wipe, ENOENT on routes.d.ts). The asymmetry is intentional: CI runs the build standalone because it has no shared test runner that builds the dashboard for it; local gates rely on the test for the same coverage.
  - **Parity test** (`tests/ci-parity.test.mjs`): asserts that (a) CI's invocation is exactly `dashboard:sync --build`, not `--check`, and (b) `tests/functional/dashboard-build.functional.test.mjs` exists and runs `next build` (the load-bearing local coverage). The test catches both halves of the contract — if CI's flag drifts back to `--check` or someone deletes the functional test, the regression surfaces locally before push.
- **Postgres test harness no longer races the postmaster's external-socket window.** `tests/functional/_lib/postgres-docker.mjs` now probes the container twice before declaring it ready: once via psql-inside-container (confirms postmaster started) and once via the `postgres` driver from the host (confirms the TCP socket accepts client queries). Without the second probe, the first test query intermittently hit `57P03 the database system is starting up` on slower machines because psql goes through a Unix socket and doesn't catch the brief gap between the postmaster opening the TCP socket and accepting client queries. The host probe retries on `57P03` / `ECONNREFUSED` until the readiness deadline.
- **Pre-push gate's "last CI run failed" check is now SHA-aware.** The old logic ran `gh run list --limit=1` and blocked on any prior failure regardless of HEAD. That created a doom loop: the commit that fixes CI couldn't push without an env-var override. New behavior: read the failed run's `headSha` and only block when `HEAD === headSha` (a re-push of the broken commit). When HEAD has advanced past the failed SHA, the gate prints a non-blocking notice with the prior failure URL and lets push through — CI re-evaluates whether the new commits are the fix. The genuine re-push-the-broken-SHA case has no override; the operator amends or adds a fix commit. Coverage: `tests/hooks/pre-push-gate-prior-ci.test.mjs` (3 cases: blocks on same SHA, allows on advanced SHA, silent on success).
- **`registry-sync` PostToolUse hook no longer auto-executes `construct sync`.** Old behavior: every Write/Edit to `specialists/registry.json` spawned a 12-second `sync-specialists.mjs` subprocess. Tests legitimately mutate `registry.json`, so the hook fired repeatedly during a test run, raced test cleanup, and regenerated platform state mid-suite — meaning `construct doctor --fix-all` could "clear" legacy state and then watch it reappear before the next command. The hook now matches its docstring: emit a one-line stderr reminder telling the developer to run `construct sync` when ready, and exit 0. Non-interactive contexts auto-suppress the reminder (`CI=true`, `NODE_ENV=test`, or `!process.stderr.isTTY` — the wrong-context detection is the right mechanism). Coverage: `tests/hooks/registry-sync-reminder.test.mjs` (4 cases: fires for registry paths, silent for unrelated paths, silent in non-interactive context, exits clean).
- **Cross-project state leakage: per-project writers now route to `<project>/.cx/` instead of `~/.cx/`, and cross-project writers tag every entry with a `projectId`.** Before this change, four append-only writers — `contract-violations.jsonl`, `audit-reads.jsonl`, `agent-log.jsonl`, `intent-verifications.jsonl` — wrote to the user-scope `~/.cx/` directory regardless of which project the call originated from, so contract violations from project A could overwrite the chain hash of a violation in project B and `construct doctor` would report aggregated counts from every project the user touched. New module `lib/project-root.mjs` exports `findProjectRoot(cwd)` (walks upward looking for `.cx/` or `.construct/` marker, stops at `$HOME` so the home dir itself never registers as a project), `projectIdFor(projectRoot)` (deterministic 12-char sha256 of the absolute path), `resolveProjectScope(cwd)`, and `resolveProjectScopedPath(basename, opts)`. The four project-scoped writers above now resolve their log path on every call, so the same writer used across `cwd` changes (tests, harness reuse, daemon batching) routes correctly. The three cross-project writers — `skill-calls.jsonl`, `role-pending.jsonl`, `session-cost.jsonl` — keep their user-scope path but tag each entry with `projectId`, so a reader can attribute usage per project. Doctor adds `--fix-migrate-state` to archive existing `~/.cx/<project-scoped>.jsonl` files to `~/.cx/legacy/<name>.<ts>.jsonl` on upgrade. The `session-cost` channel joins the bounded-logging registry (25 MB cap, 4 segments, gzip). Coverage: `tests/project-root.test.mjs` (8 cases — marker detection, HOME stop, memoization, projectId stability), `tests/functional/cross-project-state-isolation.functional.test.mjs` (4 cases — project A/B isolation, projectId tagging, intent-verifications routes to project, no leak into user-scope), plus a `--fix-migrate-state` case in `tests/functional/doctor-fixes-legacy-state.functional.test.mjs`.
- **Channel-registry-bounded logging across every high-traffic writer under `~/.cx/`.** Same anti-pattern class as the embed-daemon-log 34GB bug: append-only telemetry with no rotation. `lib/logging/rotate.mjs` now exports a `LIMITS` registry that documents the per-channel disk budget (max bytes, segment count, gzip), an `appendBounded(channel, path, line)` helper that refuses to write to an unregistered channel, and per-channel env-var overrides. Seven previously-unbounded writers are now wired through it: `audit-reads.jsonl`, `skill-calls.jsonl`, `agent-log.jsonl`, `role-pending.jsonl` (two call sites: `lib/hooks/agent-tracker.mjs` + `lib/doctor/watchers/bd-watch.mjs`), `intent-verifications.jsonl`, `contract-violations.jsonl`, `warn-flags.txt` (`lib/hooks/bash-output-logger.mjs`). Channels with existing in-file rotation (`lib/hooks/_lib/log.mjs`, `lib/roles/event-bus.mjs`) keep their own logic. Coverage: 4 new test cases in `tests/logging-rotate.test.mjs` (12 cases total) — registry enforcement, env-override resolution, real-channel rotation.
- **Seven stale `agents/registry.json` paths that silently returned null.** Same class as the `lib/server/index.mjs:39` fix earlier in this branch. The `agents/` → `specialists/` rename had been completed in earlier releases but the following call sites lagged: `lib/status.mjs:667`, `lib/auto-docs.mjs:155`, `lib/roles/catalog.mjs:14`, `lib/mcp/tools/telemetry.mjs:73`, `lib/mcp/tools/project.mjs:284` + `:326`, `lib/storage/backup.mjs:128` + `:277`, `scripts/optimize.mjs:130`. Each one read from a non-existent path and downstream code treated the result as "no specialists registered" — silently degraded `construct status`, `docs:update`, MCP `cx_trace`/`workflow_status`/`workflow_needs_main_input` tools, role catalog, backup, and the optimize script. One-line fix per file.
- **Two unbounded-disk bugs in `.cx/` runtime state.** Both beads ship a shared rotation primitive at `lib/logging/rotate.mjs` and dedicated tests at `tests/logging-rotate.test.mjs` (12 cases) + `tests/functional/init-gitignores-cx.functional.test.mjs` (4 cases).
- **Migration story for users upgrading from pre-2.0 installs — fully automatic where possible, doctor-flagged where consent matters.** Most legacy state cleans itself up on the next normal operation: `sweepLegacyPrefixedFiles` removes `~/.claude/agents/cx-*.md` + `~/.codex/agents/cx-*.toml` + `~/.github/prompts/cx-*.prompt.md` on every `--global` sync (auto-fires from the `npm i -g` postinstall); the channel registry rotates oversized append-only files on the next append; the embed daemon's `daemon-log-rotation` scheduler job rotates `~/.cx/runtime/embed-daemon.log` on the next 60s tick. `.gitignore` requires consent because it's a project file the user owns, so `construct doctor` now flags missing `.cx/` coverage and offers `--fix-gitignore`. Three new doctor checks + four `--fix-*` flags: `--fix-gitignore` (append `.cx/` to project `.gitignore`), `--fix-legacy-agents` (invoke `construct sync --global` to sweep), `--fix-embed-log` (force-rotate immediately instead of waiting 60s), and `--fix-all` (run every available fix). User-authored `cx-mytool.md`-style files at user scope survive — the sweep is registry-bounded. Coverage: `tests/functional/doctor-fixes-legacy-state.functional.test.mjs` (4 cases: each fix flag in isolation + the `--fix-all` orchestration).
  - **`construct init` now adds `.cx/` to the project `.gitignore`** (bead `construct-1vv5`). Creates the file if missing, appends with a one-line explanation when one exists, and is idempotent — won't double-add if `.cx/`, `.cx`, `*`, or `**` is already present. Prevents the failure mode where a session-heavy project commits hundreds of churning JSON files per push.
  - **Trace files cap at 100 MB and rotate** (bead `construct-1vv5`). `lib/worker/trace.mjs` now calls `appendWithRotationSync(shardPath, line, { maxBytes })` so every `.cx/traces/<date>.jsonl` shard stays strictly under GitHub's 100 MB single-file ceiling. Rotated segments are named `<date>.<n>.jsonl`. Override via `CONSTRUCT_TRACE_MAX_MB`; `=0` disables (not recommended).
  - **Embed daemon log rotates at 50 MB with 5-segment gzip retention** (bead `construct-88i`). New scheduler job `daemon-log-rotation` in `lib/embed/daemon.mjs` runs every minute, calling `rotateIfOversized('~/.cx/runtime/embed-daemon.log', { maxBytes, maxSegments, gzip: true })`. Fixes the reported 34 GB log file from a stuck `[embed] Telemetry setup skipped` line. Overrides: `CONSTRUCT_EMBED_LOG_MAX_MB` (default 50), `CONSTRUCT_EMBED_LOG_MAX_SEGMENTS` (default 5).

### Changed

- **Per-project event bus + four new lifecycle events that the recommendation pipeline can route through.** `lib/roles/event-bus.mjs` now resolves `events.jsonl` to `<project>/.cx/` when cwd is inside a Construct project (falls back to `~/.cx/` outside one); `CONSTRUCT_ROLES_ROOT` keeps overriding for tests. Closes the cross-project fingerprint-suppression bug tracked as `construct-wwf2`. Four new emitters wire lifecycle signals into the bus so subscribed specialists can act: `recommendation.generated` from `lib/embed/recommendation-store.mjs` after each new recommendation lands (the record also gains `state: 'raw' | 'enriching' | 'enriched'`, `enrichedAt`, `enrichedBy` for the upcoming enrichment loop); `document.stale` from `lib/embed/docs-lifecycle.mjs` on each stale-doc gap; `profile.updated` from `lib/profiles/lifecycle.mjs` on draft creation and archive; `signal.promoted` from `lib/intake/prepare.mjs` when a triage confidence crosses 0.70. `cx-docs-keeper` adds `document.stale` to its declarative subscriptions; the other three events are emitted with no default subscriber, ready for projects (or the planned enrichment loop) to bind via `.cx/specialists/`. All emits are best-effort: a bus failure cannot break the writer that fired it. Coverage: 3 new cases in `tests/functional/event-bus-per-project.test.mjs` (cwd-inside-project, env override, two-project isolation) + updated `tests/functional/routing-tables.test.mjs` for the new event in the expected set.

- **Routing tables (event ownership, doc-artifact ownership, proactive watch conditions) moved from hardcoded `.mjs` constants to declarative fields on `specialists/registry.json`.** Each specialist entry now carries optional `subscriptions` (event types it owns), `docArtifacts` (typed documents whose authoring it owns), and `watchConditions` (named signal-driven triggers it engages on). A new resolver `lib/orchestration/routing-tables.mjs` builds the forward maps at load time, supports project-level overlays from `.cx/specialists/*.json`, and validates that every referenced watcher exists. `EVENT_OWNERSHIP`, `DOC_OWNERSHIP`, and the inline `proactiveTriggers` heuristics are deleted from `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`; consumers (`lib/roles/router.mjs`, `lib/hooks/proactive-activation.mjs`, `routeRequest`) import `ownerForEvent` / `ownerForDoc` / `evaluateWatchConditions` from the resolver. The named-watcher pattern keeps the multi-signal predicates (high-ambiguity-deep-work, visual-or-ui-risk, auth-payments-non-narrow, architecture-without-metric, wide-blast-radius) as code while letting the registry decide which specialist owns each — projects can rebind a watcher's owner without patching library code. Migration of routing data is mechanical and exact: parity asserted by `tests/functional/routing-tables.test.mjs` (11 cases against the full event + doc + watcher sets). Removes the "hardcoded routing table" and "two sources of truth for ownership" anti-patterns called out by issue #107.

- **Dashboard rebuilt on Next.js as `apps/dashboard/`; old Vite tree (`dashboard/`) deleted.** Twenty-three routes (overview + 22 page-per-feature surfaces: Agents, Approvals, Artifacts, Audit, Beads, Commands, Config, Doctor, Editor, Hooks, Infrastructure, Intake, Knowledge, MCP, Models, Performance, Plugins, Providers, Resources, Skills, Snapshots, Workflow). Same editorial design language as the docs site — both apps now consume `@cx/ui` (a new shared workspace package at `packages/cx-ui/` with `theme.css` + `Section` / `CodeBlock` / `Mermaid` / `Callout` / `CommandPalette` / `FeatureGrid` / MDX shims). `construct dev` continues to serve the SPA from `lib/server/static/` — the `apps/dashboard/` build script (`next build && node scripts/copy-to-server-static.mjs`) copies the static export there, so `lib/server/index.mjs` is unchanged except for a stale-path fix: `agents/registry.json` → `specialists/registry.json` (the legacy `agents/` rename had been missed in the server). `construct doctor` adds a "Dashboard built (lib/server/static/index.html)" check. Per the approved plan reactive-sleeping-puzzle.md.
- **Docs site adopts the shared `@cx/ui` package.** Pulls in the same editorial primitives the dashboard uses; theme.css moved out of `apps/docs/app/` into `packages/cx-ui/styles/`. Both apps now share font wiring (Geist sans + display + JetBrains Mono).
- **End-to-end verification suite — three new harnesses + nine functional tests.**
  - `tests/functional/_lib/postgres-docker.mjs` spins up a fresh `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` container per suite, applies every `db/schema/*.sql` migration, yields a connection client, and tears down on test exit. Skips cleanly when Docker is absent.
  - `tests/functional/_lib/dashboard-server.mjs` spawns `lib/server/index.mjs` at a random port with an isolated `HOME`, returns a cookie-aware `fetch` wrapper, and supports a `seedHome(homeDir)` hook so tests can pre-populate `~/.construct/config.env` before the server reads it.
  - `tests/functional/_lib/openrouter-llm.mjs` exposes `createLlmHarness({ capUsdCents })` — resolves `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` from env or `~/.construct/config.env`, wraps OpenRouter completions with a per-suite cost guard that throws when total spend exceeds the cap, and skips cleanly when no key is available.
  - **Postgres + pgvector tests** (`postgres-roundtrip` + `vector-roundtrip`): container starts, migrations apply, `construct_intake_items` table is present, vector column round-trips an embedding, cosine-distance ranking returns expected top-k, IVFFlat index builds and queries.
  - **Live-LLM specialist tests** (`specialist-devil-advocate` + `specialist-roster` + `model-tier-resolution`): loads each `specialists/prompts/cx-*.md` against a canonical input via OpenRouter and asserts on response shape. Devil's advocate is checked specifically against a deliberately-flawed proposal — must cite ≥2 specific elements and use ≥3 pushback signal words (assumption, risk, fail, what-if, security, …). Full roster: 28 specialists × 1 call each succeeds (~7¢ per specialist, ~$2/run capped at $0.50). Tier resolution: reasoning/standard/fast tiers all route through `openrouter/auto` and produce tier-appropriate outputs.
  - **Dashboard endpoint tests** (`dashboard-endpoints` + `dashboard-sse` + `dashboard-auth` + `dashboard-build`): every `/api/*` read endpoint returns 2xx/4xx (no 5xx), SPA index.html serves, path-traversal rejected, `/events` SSE channel opens with `text/event-stream`, login flow + cookie unlock work in token mode and reject the wrong token.
- **CI workflow extended.** `postgres-integration` job now runs the new Postgres + vector roundtrip tests after migrations. New `dashboard-integration` job runs build + endpoint + auth + SSE tests (added to `ci-required` aggregator). New `live-llm-tests` job runs the 32 LLM tests via OpenRouter when `secrets.OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is set; informational-only, not blocking branch protection.

- **`construct sync` is now two-tier and project-aware so `cx-*` specialists stop leaking into VS Code / Copilot / Cursor pickers on the user's machine.** The user reported every `cx-*` specialist appearing in VS Code's agent and Copilot slash-command surfaces even outside Construct-managed projects, because every adapter (`syncClaude`, `syncCodex`, `syncCopilot`, `syncOpencode`, `syncVSCode`, `syncCursor`, `syncSkills`) was hard-wired to user-scope (`~/.claude/agents/`, `~/.github/prompts/`, etc.) and emitted all 28 specialists plus the `construct` front-door agent globally. Each host's own docs recommend workspace/project scope for team-shared agents and personal user-scope only for personal defaults; Construct now follows that pattern. Global scope writes only the `construct` front-door agent across every host (plus the existing hook installer in `~/.claude/settings.json`, which has to stay global because hooks are session-level). Project scope writes the orchestrator + all 28 `cx-*` specialists, slash commands, skills, and MCP wiring under the project's `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.opencode/`, `.github/`, `.vscode/`, `.cursor/` trees. `construct sync` auto-detects a Construct project (presence of `.cx/` or `.construct/`) and writes project-only when inside one; outside a project (or with `--global`) it writes only the front-door agent. `construct init` no longer fires a secondary global sync — staging is project-only via `stage-project.mjs`. `npm i -g @geraldmaron/construct` now triggers a `--global` sync from the postinstall so `construct` is reachable from every host immediately after install. A legacy-cleanup pass in the Claude/Codex/Copilot global write paths sweeps any pre-existing `~/.claude/agents/cx-*.md`, `~/.github/prompts/cx-*.prompt.md`, and `~/.codex/agents/cx-*.toml` files (idempotent — safe to re-run). Coverage: a new functional test (`tests/functional/sync-respects-scope.functional.test.mjs`) spawns the real binary in an isolated `HOME` + tmp project and asserts that `--global` writes only `construct.{md,toml,prompt.md}`, `--project` writes 29 agents + 141 skills + 30 commands + per-host MCP wiring, and `HOME` is untouched by project mode; the migration case seeds legacy `cx-*` files and asserts they're swept on the first `--global` run and idempotent on the second. Tracked as bead `construct-uyfc`.

## [1.0.12] - 2026-05-28

### Fixed

- **`construct sync` no longer emits double-frontmatter on host-platform adapter files.** Every `.md` write went through a doc-stamp wrapper (`stampFrontmatter`) that prepended `cx_doc_id` / `body_hash` YAML, which was intended for content artifacts (research findings, knowledge files) but was being applied to host-platform adapters that have their own frontmatter contract. Result: (a) `~/.agents/skills/*/SKILL.md` and `~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` carried only the doc-stamp block (no `name` / `description`), so the Anthropic Agent Skills loader silently dropped all 141 files; (b) `~/.claude/agents/*.md` and `~/.github/prompts/*.prompt.md` had a doc-stamp block on top of their real frontmatter, producing two `---...---` blocks and leaving the parser to pick one — usually the wrong one. `writeFile` now accepts `{ stamp: false }`; every host-adapter write opts out. Skill writes use a new `buildSkillFrontmatter()` (in `lib/sync/skill-frontmatter.mjs`) that extracts the description from the source skill's HTML-comment header and emits proper Anthropic Skills `name` + `description` YAML. The user-managed `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `~/.github/copilot-instructions.md` also stop being doc-stamped. Coverage: 9 unit tests for the new helpers plus 4 functional tests that run the real sync against an isolated tmp HOME and assert no double-frontmatter on any output (Claude agents, Copilot prompts, both skill trees) and that ≥200 SKILL.md files emerge with valid Anthropic Skills frontmatter.
- **Stale docs references cleaned up across the docs site.** Replaced retired `agents/` paths with `specialists/` (including the `lib/hooks/registry-sync.mjs` docstring), updated old `construct up/down/serve/cost/init-docs` instructions to the current `construct dev` / `construct stop` / `construct status` / `construct init --with-*` flows, repaired broken Markdown links in the README and `docs/README.md`, and replaced removed `docs/how-to/*` pointers with the current concept and cookbook pages.

### Changed

- **README and docs-site guidance audited for current Construct behavior.** Public links now match the Fumadocs root routes, the README carries a concise usage loop, `docs/README.md` points to current concept/cookbook pages, and the docs surface a coverage map that matches every user-facing CLI command.
- **Generated CLI reference aligned with the current command registry.** `lib/cli-commands.mjs` adds two new categories (`Models & Integrations`, `Diagnostics`) so `CATEGORY_ORDER` is `[Core, Work, Models & Integrations, Integrations, Observability, Diagnostics, Advanced]`. `docs:site` now emits new reference pages (`core.md`, `advanced.md`, `integrations.md`) under `docs/reference/cli/` and retires the stale `services.md`, `embed.md`, `providers.md`, `docs.md`, and `agents-and-sync.md` pages that no longer match a category.
- **`lib/auto-docs.mjs` retargets `buildSite()` for Fumadocs.** The public site now renders `docs/` directly through Fumadocs and `buildFumadocsReference()` emits the generated reference pages; the legacy MkDocs-style `site/docs/` builder is retained as a compatibility shim only.

## [1.0.11] - 2026-05-28

### Added

- **`--force` flag on `construct init`** bypasses the new project-structure detector and scaffolds the full default tree even when populated lane dirs, a custom intake script, or root `templates/` already exist. Use when you genuinely want the parallel layout (e.g. importing Construct into a project as a second-class doc surface).

### Fixed

- **`construct init` defers to existing project content instead of scaffolding parallel `docs/`/`.cx/inbox/`/`templates/` trees (#97).** A new shared detector at `lib/init/detect-existing-structure.mjs` scans the project root (up to depth 3, skipping `.git`, `node_modules`, `.cx`, `.construct`, `.claude`, `dist`, `build`, etc.) for: (a) populated directories whose basename matches a known lane alias (`internal/meetings/`, `operational/incidents/`, `team/minutes/`, …), (b) custom intake scripts at root (`ingest`, `ingest.sh`, `ingest.mjs`, `ingest.py`) or directories (`data/customers/notes/raw`, `data/intake`, `ingestion`, `intake-pipeline`, `raw`), and (c) root `templates/` files. Both `lib/init-unified.mjs` and the standalone `lib/init-docs.mjs` now consult the detector before scaffolding. When a lane is already covered, init skips that `docs/<lane>/` with a per-skip log line and a final "Deferred to existing project structure" summary. When custom intake is detected, `.cx/inbox/` is not created and `intake-config.json` defaults `includeProjectInbox: false`. Coverage: 18 unit tests in `tests/init/detect-existing-structure.test.mjs` plus three functional cases in `tests/functional/init-respects-existing-structure.functional.test.mjs` (pre-existing layout deferred, `--force` overrides, clean-project regression guard).
- **README disclaimer sharpened** to reflect what Construct actually is: a vibe-coded side project, not production-grade tooling, with a real issues queue and contributions welcome.

### Changed (CI / repo hygiene, no user-facing runtime effect)

- **`ci-required` aggregator job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`** wraps every conditional CI job (`test`, `lint`, `evals`, `audit`, `secret-scan`, `postgres-integration`) with `if: always()` and a jq check on `needs.*.result`. Skipped jobs count as pass, so doc-only PRs satisfy branch protection without admin override. `main` branch protection PATCHed to require only `[ci-required, secret scanning, review]`. `lib/gates-audit.mjs` taught about `requireMergeVia` so wrapped jobs aren't double-counted as missing-from-protection gaps. `parseCIJobs` extended to also read `pr-review.yml` so the `review` context resolves.
- **`docs auto-update` workflow** writes the right filename — `docs/concepts/architecture.mdx`, not the pre-rename `.md` — and `lib/auto-docs.mjs` table + module header updated to match.
- **`tests/mcp-manager.test.mjs:makeRepoCopy`** also excludes `.beads/` from its repo-wide `cpSync` (same readdir → lstat race as the prior `.claude/` exclusion; beads' embedded dolt DB rotates manifest files mid-walk).

## [1.0.10] - 2026-05-28

### Changed

- **`pre-push-gate` PR-body template policy now degrades to a warning when no specialist sub-agent is active.** Previously the hook hard-blocked every `gh pr create` / `gh pr edit` whose body failed the template lint, including sessions where a human was driving the editorial decisions through Claude Code. The hook now checks whether a Construct specialist is active (via the same `CONSTRUCT_AGENT_ID` env flag and `~/.cx/last-agent*.json` fence window that `guard-bash.mjs` uses): if yes, hard-block (exit 2) as before; if no, print a `[pre-push-gate] PR body fails template policy (no specialist agent active — warning only)` notice and let the command through. CI's `template policy` step still enforces the same rules on the resulting PR — this only changes pre-flight ergonomics, not the actual gate. Shared detector extracted to `lib/hooks/_lib/specialist-agent.mjs` with seven unit tests in `tests/hooks/specialist-agent.test.mjs` covering env flag, fresh per-agent file, fresh shared file, stale file, missing file, malformed JSON, and missing timestamp.

### Fixed

- **Workflow path filters point at `specialists/` after the rename.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the `code`, `retrieval`, and `agents` filters), `pages.yml`, and `docs.yml` still referenced `agents/**`, `agents/registry.json`, `agents/contracts.json`, `lib/agents/**`, and `lib/agent-contracts*.mjs` after the v1.0.8 rename moved those files to `specialists/` and `lib/specialist-contracts*.mjs`. The stale globs never matched, so doc/page rebuilds and `retrieval` evals stopped firing on specialist or contracts edits. All references updated. Filters that match the current layout. Bead: construct-qgsm.
- **Branch protection required-status-checks list aligned with what PRs actually emit.** The v1.0.7→1.0.8 CI refactor (a) shrank the PR test matrix to a single `test (ubuntu-latest / node 22)` runner and (b) collapsed nine independent lint contexts (`comment policy`, `template policy`, `gates audit`, `docs drift check`, plus five others) into a single `lint suite` job whose steps are no longer separate check contexts. Branch protection still required all of the old contexts plus the macOS + node-20 matrix variants that only fire on push-to-main, so every PR was BLOCKED and required admin override. Required contexts updated via `PATCH /repos/.../branches/main/protection/required_status_checks` to: `test (ubuntu-latest / node 22)`, `lint suite`, `retrieval evals`, `dependency CVE audit`, `secret scanning`, `postgres + pgvector integration`, `review`. PRs can merge cleanly again. Bead: construct-qgsm.

## [1.0.9] - 2026-05-28

### Fixed

- **`construct init` no longer concatenates the npm install prefix onto project paths.** In `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`, the `lockPath` and `stagingDir` constants were computed against `root` (the construct install directory at `/usr/local/lib/node_modules/@geraldmaron/construct`) before `projectDir` was declared, so `path.relative(root, file)` produced `../../../../../../Users/<user>/<project>/.claude/...` segments that `path.join(stagingDir, rel)` collapsed lexically into `/usr/local/Users/<user>/<project>/.claude/...` — an invalid path that failed with `EACCES` against `/usr/local`. `projectDir` is now declared above the constants and used as `projectDir || root` for the staging base path and inside `writeFile()`'s `path.relative()` call. Global mode (no `--project` flag) is unchanged via the `|| root` fallback. Credit: Sara Kay (#90).
- **Restored `npm ci` on `main` after the v1.0.8 merge.** Commit 66f031b added `node-webvtt` and OpenTelemetry `optionalDependencies` to `package.json` without regenerating `package-lock.json`, breaking every workflow that ran `npm ci` (ci, docs auto-update, GitHub Pages). Lockfile regenerated and committed (#91).
- **`.github/workflows/pr-review.yml` reads from `specialists/role-manifests.json`.** The path was missed in the `agents/` → `specialists/` rename, causing every PR review job to fail with `ENOENT` (#91).
- **Dropped the dangling `construct_skill_quality_correlation` view from `db/schema/008_skill_usage.sql`.** The view referenced `construct_cx_scores`, a table never created in any migration, and was not consumed by any code path. Removing it lets the postgres + pgvector integration test apply the migration set cleanly. The view can return when the planned `construct skills correlate-quality` CLI ships alongside the underlying table (#91).

## [1.0.8] - 2026-05-28

### Added

- `construct claude:allow` CLI for managing `~/.claude/settings.json` `permissions.allow` from outside the agent. Claude Code's auto-classifier denies the agent from editing its own permission allowlist — correctly, but that leaves users hand-editing JSON every time the classifier flags a new pattern. The CLI provides `list`, `add <pattern...>` (idempotent), `remove <pattern...>`, and `check [--apply]` which scans local branch prefixes (`feat/`, `fix/`, `chore/`, `docs/`, `refactor/`, `perf/`, `cleanup/`, `test/`, `build/`, `ci/`, `style/` — never `main`/`master`/`dev`/`claude`/`agent`) against the current allowlist and proposes the missing force-push entries. Implementation in `lib/claude-allow.mjs` with 11 tests. Atomic JSON writes, preserves unrelated fields. Session-start hook reads the same module and appends a one-line "Permission posture" section to the session banner when gaps exist, so the agent sees the missing entries up front and asks the user to close them via `construct claude:allow check --apply` instead of stumbling into classifier denials mid-task.

### Fixed

- **Fence-gate deadlock eliminated: engineer allowedCommands broadened, per-agent timestamps, docs auto-fix.** Three systemic anti-patterns were compounding into a hard deadlock on push: the guard-bash fence restricted engineer to `bd *` commands only (blocking `node`, `npm`), the pre-push gate required `node` to run quality checks (tests, docs verify, lint), and the shared `~/.cx/last-agent.json` timestamp was reset by any background agent dispatch, keeping the fence window open indefinitely. `specialists/role-manifests.json` now includes `node`, `npm`, `git status`, `git diff`, and `git log` in engineer's `allowedCommands`. `lib/hooks/guard-bash.mjs` reads per-agent timestamp files (`~/.cx/last-agent-{id}.json`) with fallback to the shared file, and prints the actual allowed list plus bypass hint on denial. `lib/hooks/agent-tracker.mjs` writes both shared and per-agent timestamp files. `lib/hooks/pre-push-gate.mjs` now shows full test output on failure (not just the first 3 lines), and auto-runs `docs:update` when `docs:update --check` detects drift, with a follow-up re-check before blocking. Resolves errors 1-3 from the `feat/platform-capabilities` branch. Tests verified in isolated sandbox: 2021/2021 pass, 14/14 release gates pass, all construct doctor/lint checks clean.

### Fixed

- **`pre-compact` hook no longer clobbers manually-written context sections.** `lib/hooks/pre-compact.mjs` previously rebuilt `context.md` and `context.json` from scratch on every compaction, silently wiping any sections the session author had written (active work, architecture decisions, release notes). The hook now reads the existing `context.md` before writing, extracts any section whose heading is not in the auto-generated set (`What was in progress`, `Files changed this session`, `Decisions captured`, `Pending TODOs`, `Session efficiency snapshot`, `Open issues`), and re-appends those manual sections verbatim after the freshly generated auto content. Similarly, non-empty `decisions`, `filesChanged`, and `pendingTodos` arrays in `context.json` are carried forward when the transcript scan finds nothing new, preventing manually captured state from being erased. Both the project-local and global `~/.cx/` writes use the same merge logic. Unit tests in `tests/pre-compact-context-merge.test.mjs` cover `parseSections`, `extractManualSections`, `mergeExistingJson`, and an end-to-end integration case.

### Changed

- **Specialist skill roster rebalanced; strategy, operating, and exploration categories expanded.** `business-strategist` was bound solely to `architecture/api-design` (a technical skill that belongs to `cx-architect`, which already covered it). The binding is removed and replaced with four new strategy skills: `strategy/competitive-landscape` (Porter Five Forces + JTBD applied to software products), `strategy/market-research-methods` (JTBD interview protocol, survey design pitfalls, instrumentation patterns), `strategy/pricing-positioning` (value-based pricing, Van Westendorp, conjoint analysis, positioning statement construction), and `strategy/narrative-arc` (Andy Raskin's five-move strategic narrative structure for PRDs, board updates, and fundraising). `skills/operating/` expands from one file to four: `incident-response.md` (severity tiers, war-room protocol, blameless postmortem), `oncall-rotation.md` (handoff design, runbook quality bar, escalation paths), and `change-management.md` (Type 1 vs. Type 2 decisions, rollback planning). `skills/exploration/` expands from one file to four: `dependency-graph-reading.md` (npm/Cargo/Maven lockfile interpretation, supply-chain risk signals), `unknown-codebase-onboarding.md` (first-hour/day/week checkpoints), and `tracer-bullet-method.md` (minimal end-to-end path before deep implementation). Registry bindings updated: `operations` and `orchestrator` gain the three operating skills; `explorer` and `engineer` gain the three exploration skills. New `scripts/migrate-skill-frontmatter.mjs` one-shot migration script converts HTML-comment preamble to YAML frontmatter across `skills/**/*.md` (idempotent, dry-run default, `--apply` to write).
- **CI pre-push gate wired and extended.** `lib/hooks/pre-push-gate.mjs` now runs seven additional Construct-specific jobs in parallel before any push: `evals retrieval`, `docs:verify`, `docs:update --check`, `dashboard:sync --check`, `lint:comments`, `lint:agents`, and `lint:contracts`. The `.beads/hooks/pre-push` shell script is updated to delegate to the gate module; the `CONSTRUCT_SKIP_PREPUSH` and `BEADS_SKIP_PREPUSH` escape hatches are preserved. `build:sea` (esbuild SEA bundle check) runs when `CONSTRUCT_PREPUSH_FULL=1`. Two new CLI commands: `construct ci preview` parses `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, maps each job to its underlying `npm`/`node` commands, and runs them locally (`--job=<name>` for a single job, `--list` to enumerate, `--full` to include Docker/Trivy steps); `construct ci status` calls `gh run list` for the current branch and surfaces recent CI outcomes. `construct ci status` is also consumed by the session-start hook so a session opening on a branch with red CI sees a warning.
- **Policy architecture documented; `construct policy show` CLI added; beads close-reason promoted to deterministic.** Three changes land together: (1) New `docs/concepts/policy-architecture.mdx` documents the three-layer enforcement model (JSON manifests, validation modules, hooks), cross-references every `rules/common/*.md` to its enforcement file and mode, and explains why OPA/Cedar are not adopted at Construct's 5-50-specialist scale. (2) New `specialists/policy-inventory.json` is the machine-readable source of truth for all 22 active policies (id, source, enforcement file, mode, description); `construct policy show` renders a tabular view and CI asserts every `rules/common/*.md` has an inventory entry. (3) `validateBeadsCloseReason(reason)` and `applyBeadsHygienePolicy(reason, opts)` added to `lib/contracts/validate.mjs`: a close reason must be at least 20 characters and include either a PR reference (`#N`) or a `file.ext:line` evidence pointer; enforcement mode controlled by `CONSTRUCT_BEADS_HYGIENE=warn|block`.
- **CI workflows pruned to remove redundant runner overhead.** .github/workflows/ci.yml is rewritten with three structural changes: (a) the trigger model drops push on feature branches so the same code does not fire CI twice (once on push, once on PR open); push only fires on main and PR events cover everything upstream; (b) the test matrix shrinks from 2 OS x 2 Node = 4 runners to 1 runner (Ubuntu / Node 22) on pull_request while keeping the full 2x2 matrix on main pushes - macOS + Node 20 coverage stays gated to merges, which is when cross-platform regressions actually matter; (c) the nine previously-independent lint/check jobs (lint-comments, lint-prose, lint-profiles, lint-agents, lint-contracts, lint-templates, docs, gates-audit, dashboard:sync --check) consolidate into one lint suite job that pays the npm ci setup once and runs each lint as a separate step - failure surface stays granular in the GitHub UI, only the runner-minutes shrink. lib/gates-audit.mjs extended to scan both job-level and step-level names so the consolidated structure stays auditable.
- **Pricing readouts suppressed from user-facing surfaces; underlying telemetry stays stubbed-and-ready.** Session-end notifications no longer append `~$X.XX [N turns]` cost notes (`lib/hooks/stop-notify.mjs`); the `construct cost`, `construct costs`, and `construct pricing` CLI commands are unwired from the dispatch map; the dashboard insights payload omits the cost panel and the telemetry summary's cost fields; doctor stops surfacing pricing-catalog freshness and divergence advisories. The mechanism beneath these surfaces — the session-cost ledger writes, the LiteLLM pricing catalog refresh, per-call cost computation, the cost-ledger helpers, and the telemetry trace `totalCost` ingestion — remains intact so the OpenTelemetry GenAI integration (plan Workstream J) can rewire consumer-facing surfaces without re-implementing the underlying accounting. Handler functions (`cmdPricing`, `cmdCost`, `cmdCosts`) stay in `bin/construct` and the cost catalog row in `lib/cli-commands.mjs` is the only public-facing removal.

### Fixed

- **`construct doctor` no longer warns about Cursor and VS Code when neither is installed.** The checks for "Cursor MCP config" and "VS Code settings file" previously fired a warning whenever the target files were absent, regardless of whether the tool was installed. On machines where Cursor has never run, `.cursor/` does not exist, and there is nothing to configure. On machines where VS Code is not installed, the Application Support directory does not exist. Both checks now gate on installation detection first: Cursor is considered installed when `~/.cursor/` exists; VS Code is considered installed when the platform-appropriate Application Support root exists. If the tool is not installed, the check is skipped entirely. If the tool is installed but the config file is absent, the warning fires with an actionable message. The VS Code warning message is updated to say "create settings.json or wait for `construct sync` VS Code support" since `construct sync` does not yet write VS Code settings (tracked as construct-9r6c).
- **`construct doctor` SQL warning is now mode-aware and schema-migration check no longer cascades.** When `DATABASE_URL` is configured but Postgres is unreachable (`ECONNREFUSED`), the old message "SQL backend reachable ⚠" gave no context. The new message says "SQL backend unreachable (solo mode — run `construct dev` to start Postgres)" in solo mode, or "check DATABASE_URL and that the server is running" in team/enterprise mode. The schema-migration check that runs after the SQL check now skips when SQL is already known to be offline (`sqlReachable` flag), eliminating a redundant second warning for the same offline server.
- **`cx-reviewer` prompt now requires `noIssuesFoundAt` when findings are empty.** The `reviewer.findings-or-explicit-clear` postcondition requires that a review output either have non-empty findings or explicitly list the inspected paths via `noIssuesFoundAt`. The prompt said "say so clearly" but did not name the required field, producing 114 contract violations per day where the reviewer rubber-stamped empty findings without the explicit clear statement. The prompt now names the field directly and explains that omitting it logs a contract violation.
- **`loadConstructEnv` no longer warns on empty-string shell values.** Claude for Desktop injects `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=""` (empty string) into subprocess environments so its internal auth takes priority, not as a competing credential. `lib/env-config.mjs` previously fired the shadow warning whenever `process.env[key]` differed from the config.env value, which meant every Construct invocation from inside Claude for Desktop printed a spurious warning about a non-existent conflict. The fix adds `if (!env[key]) continue;` before the conflict check: an empty-string value is not a real credential, config.env wins silently, and no user action is needed. The warning still fires when a real non-empty shell value conflicts with the stored config.env value.
- **Intake classifier no longer produces high-confidence wrong classifications via substring keyword matching.** Two demonstrable regressions exercised on `/tmp/intake-demo.mjs` (a research note routing to `security` at 1.00 confidence because `rce` matched as a substring of `enforce`; an eval finding routing to `bug` at 0.80 because filename hint was outweighed by stray body keywords) are now closed. `lib/intake/classify.mjs:countMatches` compiles per-entry keyword regex with `\b` word boundaries at module load (cached via `WeakMap`), so plural forms like `leaks` and embedded fragments like `rce`-in-`enforce` no longer trigger false positives. Filename hints that agree with an H1 family term (e.g. `research-*.md` filename + `# Research:` H1) lock the type at confidence 0.90 and short-circuit scoring; stray body keywords cannot override two independent author-intent declarations. Confidence ramp is calibrated by hit count (1=0.55, 2=0.72, 3=0.82, 4+=0.92) with an ambiguous-margin cap at 0.50 when top-2 candidates are within 0.30.
