- Document metadata extraction. `extractDocumentMetadata(filePath)` in `lib/document-extract.mjs` returns structured metadata from source documents: `title` (from frontmatter, H1, HTML `<title>`, or filename), `authors` (from `author`/`authors`/`contributor` frontmatter fields), `dates` (from frontmatter date fields or filesystem timestamps), and `links` (deduplicated URLs from document body). `lib/document-ingest.mjs` now calls `extractDocumentMetadata` during ingestion and writes source metadata (`authors`, `source_date`, `source_links_count`) into the output frontmatter. Ingested document title is now derived from source content rather than always falling back to the filename.

### Changed

- Responsive dashboard. The sidebar collapses into a slide-in drawer below `lg` (1024px) with a hamburger header strip; above that it stays the familiar 256px rail. Mission Control hero row goes 1-col → 2-col (`sm`) → 3-col (`lg`). Headings drop from `text-3xl` to `text-2xl` on narrow screens. Tables wrap in `overflow-x-auto`. A reusable `<SmallScreenNotice />` component shows an amber "Tight fit — designed for a wider screen" hint above editing-heavy pages (Models, Config, Intake) on widths below `sm` (640px) — advisory, not a block; forms still render. Read-only ops pages (Mission Control, Beads, Doctor, Providers) work cleanly at every breakpoint.
- Within-surface duplicate edit paths removed (CLI + dashboard each get a single source of truth per setting):
  - **Provider credentials** moved from Models page to Providers page. The Providers page is now the canonical edit surface — inline `CredentialsCard` (admin-gated POST `/api/providers/credentials`, hot-reloads `process.env`, audit-logged). Models page links to Providers when a key is missing. The previous stale "edit .env and restart construct" copy on Providers is gone.
  - **Alias** is edited in the sidebar wordmark only. The duplicate Alias field on the Config page is replaced with a one-line pointer ("click <strong>Construct</strong> in the top-left to rename").
  - **Deployment mode** has a single CLI setter. `construct config mode <value>` is no longer a setter — it errors out and points to `construct config set deployment.mode <value>` (the canonical path that writes to `construct.config.json`). `construct setup` no longer writes `CONSTRUCT_DEPLOYMENT_MODE` to `~/.construct/config.env` on a default install; that env var is reserved for ephemeral runtime overrides (CI/scripts via `export`). Foot-gun fixed: a user could previously `construct config set deployment.mode team` and see `solo` afterward because setup had pinned the env value masking the JSON.
- First-run "Getting started" strip on Mission Control. Lists the gates that block a productive session — "Pick a model per tier" (reads `specialists/registry.json`, shows N of 3 set) and "Configure at least one provider" (reads `/api/insights → providers`). Each gate links to the page that fixes it (`#/models`, `#/providers`). Strip auto-hides when every gate passes. No parallel onboarding flow — the strip is just a different view of the same state the rest of the page already loads.

- Model selection consolidated to a single source of truth. Previously the same tier assignment could be written in three places that quietly diverged: `specialists/registry.json` `models.<tier>`, `construct.config.json` `models.<tier>` (a separate field in `lib/config/schema.mjs` that nothing in `lib/` read), and `~/.construct/config.env` `CX_MODEL_*` env overrides. The Config page edited the orphan `construct.config.json.models` block with no runtime effect. Now: registry.json is the only persisted selection, edited by the Models page and `construct models --set / --apply`. The schema drops the `models` block, the Config page replaces that section with a one-line pointer to the Models page, and the env-override layer stays read-only at runtime for CI/ops (still wins, never edited from the dashboard). `getProviderModelCatalog` already surfaces the catalog in one place; the resolution chain is `env override → registry → null` (no implicit default).
- No shipped model defaults. `specialists/registry.json` ships with `{reasoning, standard, fast}.primary: null, fallback: []`. The hidden `BUILTIN_DEFAULTS` constant in `lib/model-router.mjs` (which silently filled in `deepseek-r1` / `qwen3-coder:free` / `llama-3.3-70b-instruct:free` on a clean install) is gone. When nothing is configured every tier resolves to `null` with source `not configured`. Surfacing: `construct doctor` fails (not warns) on missing tier config and prints the fix; `construct models` shows `(not configured)` plus a three-line instruction block; the dashboard Models page renders an amber **"No models selected"** banner with an inline **"Auto-pick free models"** button.
- Free-model router exposed in the dashboard. The existing `pollFreeModels` + `selectForTier` selector — previously only reachable via `construct models --apply` / `--poll` — is now also wired to `POST /api/models/free/apply`. The Models page's auto-pick button polls OpenRouter, picks the best free model per tier (32k context floor for reasoning, 16k for standard, 8k for fast), persists the choices to `specialists/registry.json`, and refreshes the page. Missing `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` returns a 400 with a one-line fix; an empty catalog returns a 502.

### Added

- Intake configuration — user-defined parent dirs + scan depth, exposed in both the CLI and dashboard. `lib/intake/intake-config.mjs` reads and writes `.cx/intake-config.json` with a clamped `maxDepth` (0–16) and a `parentDirs` list (absolute or rootDir-relative). `lib/embed/inbox.mjs` `scanDir` now walks each parent up to the configured depth instead of unbounded recursion, so a 50 GB Downloads folder doesn't accidentally bog down the daemon. CLI: `construct intake config` shows the current state and a 6-stop depth guide ("Only this dir" → "Unlimited (capped)"); `construct intake config set --depth=N --add-dir=PATH --remove-dir=PATH --include-project-inbox=on|off --include-docs-intake=on|off` mutates it. Dashboard: new **Intake** page under Operations renders the parent-dir list, a slider mapped to the same 6-stop guidance, and a depth-detail panel so the user can see what each step actually means before saving. `CX_INBOX_DIRS` and `CX_INTAKE_MAX_DEPTH` env vars still win over file state for ops overrides. Endpoints: `GET/POST /api/intake/config`.
- Local LLM providers (Ollama + generic OpenAI-compatible) added as first-class families in `lib/model-router.mjs` alongside `github-copilot`. Both are tagged `local: true` with `requiresEnv` (Ollama needs `OLLAMA_BASE_URL`, the generic family needs `LOCAL_LLM_BASE_URL`) and a `pricingHint` of `free · runs locally`. `getProviderModelCatalog()` surfaces those flags so the **Models** page renders a "Local providers available" callout listing the local families and their required env vars. The selector now treats local models exactly like remote ones — no separate UI, no separate config path.
- Live model pricing in the selector. `lib/model-pricing.mjs` resolves USD-per-1M-tokens pricing from three sources: the OpenRouter `GET /api/v1/models` catalog (fetched once per 5-minute window and cached to `~/.cx/model-pricing.json`), a built-in static table for direct Anthropic / OpenAI endpoints, and a zero-priced `local` source for Ollama / `local/*` ids (no network call). Endpoint: `GET /api/models/pricing?ids=a,b,c`. The Models page passes every renderable model id to the pricing fetcher and appends the resolved label (`$3.00 in · $15.00 out / 1M`, or `free · local`) to each dropdown option plus a smaller footer line under the primary selector. Cache is defensive against future-timestamped writes (clock-skew safe).

- `construct claude:allow` CLI for managing `~/.claude/settings.json` `permissions.allow` from outside the agent. Claude Code's auto-classifier denies the agent from editing its own permission allowlist — correctly, but that leaves users hand-editing JSON every time the classifier flags a new pattern. The CLI provides `list`, `add <pattern...>` (idempotent), `remove <pattern...>`, and `check [--apply]` which scans local branch prefixes (`feat/`, `fix/`, `chore/`, `docs/`, `refactor/`, `perf/`, `cleanup/`, `test/`, `build/`, `ci/`, `style/` — never `main`/`master`/`dev`/`claude`/`agent`) against the current allowlist and proposes the missing force-push entries. Implementation in `lib/claude-allow.mjs` with 11 tests. Atomic JSON writes, preserves unrelated fields. Session-start hook reads the same module and appends a one-line "Permission posture" section to the session banner when gaps exist, so the agent sees the missing entries up front and asks the user to close them via `construct claude:allow check --apply` instead of stumbling into classifier denials mid-task.

### Fixed

- Removed a duplicate top-level `embeddings` key in `specialists/registry.json`. JSON parsers silently picked the second copy, so the bug was invisible until you tried to diff or hand-edit the file. Single block now.

- Stop-event blocks converted to advisories. `lib/hooks/policy-engine.mjs` Stop event previously refused to let a session end while CI was red on the current branch or while any beads issue was `in_progress`, with `process.exit(2)` and two sticky env-var bypasses (`CONSTRUCT_STOP_OK_RED_CI=1`, `CONSTRUCT_STOP_OK_OPEN_BD=1`). Both states are normal across sessions — red CI is what next session fixes; an in-progress bead is literally what beads tracks. Blocking with an env-var ack reproduced the antipattern this same PR removed from `config-protection.mjs`: friction the first time, zero protection thereafter once the var lands in `~/.zshenv`. Both checks now print the same diagnostic and exit 0; the env-var bypasses are removed. Drive-mode block stays (explicit opt-in, not a default state).
- Hook gating hardening — three related cleanups so the policy layer stops fighting itself. (1) **Bootstrap matcher.** `platforms/claude/settings.template.json` `pre:policy-engine` matcher previously registered `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit|TodoWrite|Bash`, so the hook never saw `Read|Grep|Glob|LS|NotebookRead` and the read counter could not increment. The earlier session-start auto-bootstrap silenced the symptom for normal opens, but resumed sessions or hook-ordering edge cases left the read-counter escape hatch structurally dead. Matcher now lists every tool the hook tracks; reads count regardless of session-start's bootstrap-write firing. (2) **Bash gate removed.** `BENIGN_BASH` allowlist deleted from `lib/hooks/policy-engine.mjs`; `Bash` dropped from the bootstrap matcher. Dangerous-Bash policy was already owned by `guard-bash.mjs` (rm/force-push/DDL blocklist) — double-gating with a positive allowlist created friction (every safe command — `grep`, `find`, `rg`, `git cat-file` — had to be hand-added) and put the bootstrap gate at the wrong layer (grounding is about modifying code, not running shell). (3) **Meta-edit env-var removed.** `lib/hooks/config-protection.mjs` no longer blocks edits to hooks / `specialists/registry.json` / `claude/settings.template.json` / `install.sh` behind `CX_ALLOW_META_EDIT=1`. The pattern was a sticky env-var toggle — friction the first time, zero protection thereafter once set in `~/.zshenv` and forgotten. Replaced with a `meta.edit` role event (advisory only, audited but unblocked). Real layers for self-modification accidents: CLAUDE.md guidance, Claude Code's intent-aware auto-classifier, and the post-hoc event log. Code-quality config protection (eslint/prettier/tsconfig/biome/stylelint) still blocks — those rules are a contract; weakening them is almost always a bug fix in disguise. Net effect: one matcher line, ~30 fewer lines of hook code across two files, no env-var artifact to install or remember.
- Hook follow-ups from the same audit. `lib/hooks/persona-validator.mjs` and `lib/hooks/probe-before-read.js` removed — orphans not registered in `~/.claude/settings.json` for the install path. `lib/hooks/block-no-verify.mjs` vendored (was `npx block-no-verify@1.1.2` on every Bash call, paying an npx cold-start tax + a third-party supply-chain hop for ~30 lines of logic); the template now invokes the local file. `platforms/claude/settings.template.json` `pre:mcp-health-check` matcher narrowed from `*` to `mcp__.*` — the hook already early-exited on non-MCP tools so the change is a pure perf win (skip the Node spawn entirely instead of spawn-and-exit).
- Hook system audit + repairs. `lib/hooks/audit-trail.mjs` had a malformed double comment block (closing `*/` followed by stray `*`-prefixed lines outside any block) that made the file a Node syntax error — the mutation audit-trail hook has been silently failing to load since the bug was introduced. Merged the two halves into a single well-formed JSDoc block. `lib/hooks/policy-engine.mjs` `BENIGN_BASH` allowlist widened to cover read-only diagnostics the agent needs during recovery flows (`grep`, `rg`, `find`, `fd`, `jq`, `sort`, `uniq`, `cut`, `tr`, `awk`, `du`, `df`, `ps`, `lsof`, `stat`, `npm test`, `sed -n`, more `git` subcommands) — previously the gate blocked the agent from running `npm test` to debug a test failure surfaced by the push gate, making the recovery loop unworkable. `lib/hooks/session-start.mjs` now writes `{done: true}` for the active `session_id` into `~/.cx/bootstrap-state.json` at session open. The PreToolUse matcher in `claude/settings.template.json` only registers `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit|TodoWrite|Bash`, so the documented "3+ exploratory reads" escape hatch was structurally dead (Read tools never reach the hook, the counter never increments). Session-start already emits branch + recent commits + prior observations — that is grounding — so marking the session bootstrapped at session open replaces ceremony that the hook could not actually demand. Test suite updated to match the priority flip from the earlier `feat(pricing): live catalog wins over static fallback` commit: assertions now expect `openrouter` / `litellm` / `static-fallback` as the live source labels.

### Added

- Signal-driven proactive triggers in `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`. `requestSignals(request, context)` computes a stable signals object — `intent`, `workCategory`, `riskFlags`, `ambiguityScore` (0–1 over hedge words like "maybe / not sure / figure out"), `hasSuccessMetric`, `hasNamedConstraints`, `blastRadius` ('narrow' | 'medium' | 'wide' derived from migration/backfill/feature-flag/rollout language), `authOrPayments`, `visualDeliverable`, `namedUsers` — so specialists don't re-derive the same surface signals from raw text. `proactiveTriggers(signals)` returns `[{specialist, reason}]` for specialists that should engage pre-dispatch: `cx-product-manager` on high-ambiguity deep work, `cx-designer` on visual or UI risk, `cx-security` on auth/payments/PII with non-narrow blast radius, `cx-devil-advocate` on architecture without a stated success metric, `cx-sre` on wide blast radius. `routeRequest` unions triggers with the existing keyword paths and emits `dispatchSummary` (per-specialist line with the reason) plus `dispatchReasons` so the routing decision is visible before work starts. `CONSTRUCT_VERBOSE=1` traces each proactive engagement on stderr.
- Five binary contract postconditions on `specialists/contracts.json` paired with the existing `lib/specialists/postconditions.mjs` rules. `engineer-to-reviewer` output requires `verdict` in the enum {APPROVED, APPROVED_WITH_WARNINGS, BLOCKED} and at least one of `findings` or `noIssuesFoundAt` — blocks silent rubber-stamp reviews. `reviewer-to-security` output requires both `threatModelUpdatedAt` and `contractStart` so the postcondition `threatModelUpdatedAt >= contractStart` can fire — blocks post-hoc threat models. `any-to-debugger` (new contract) requires `rootCauseConfirmedVia` in {reproduction, trace, test} — blocks symptom-only fixes. `any-to-designer` (new contract) requires `accessibilityCheckRan=true` — forces pre-ship a11y instead of post-hoc. `any-to-docs-keeper` output adds `crossDocCoherenceCheckRan` + `coherenceDiff` — blocks stale-doc PRs that claim "docs updated" without a real diff. `validatePacket` in `lib/specialist-contracts.mjs` now understands `mustContainOneOf` (OR groups; missing reports joined keys like `findings|noIssuesFoundAt`) and `mustMatchEnum` (mismatch reports `<key>!=<allowed>`), so contracts can express these binary rules at the shape layer in addition to the programmatic checks in `postconditions.mjs`.
- Real performance review generator. `lib/performance/generate.mjs` exports `generatePerformanceReview({windowDays, env, now})` that reads `~/.cx/session-cost.jsonl` (per-call cost ledger) + Langfuse generation observations for the trailing 7-day window and computes per-agent stats: invocations, avgScore (1 − failureRate placeholder until Langfuse scores are wired), failureRate, p50 / p95 latency, totalCostUsd, trend ('improving' / 'stable' / 'declining' / 'unknown' compared against the prior 7-day window), status ('healthy' / 'needs_attention' / 'failing' classified by failureRate + avgScore thresholds), models seen, and a reasoning string. `writeReview` saves to `~/.cx/performance-reviews/review-<iso>.json`. New `construct review run` CLI invokes the generator; legacy Langfuse-based script preserved as `construct review legacy`. Stop hook (`lib/hooks/stop-notify.mjs`) auto-invokes the generator at session end, throttled to once per 24h via a `.last-run` stamp file to prevent review-spam.
- Doctor probe for broken Claude Stop hook installation. `claude/settings.json` template wires `policy-engine.mjs`, `stop-typecheck.mjs`, `stop-notify.mjs`, and `readme-age-check.mjs` at `$HOME/.construct/lib/hooks/*`. If `~/.construct/lib/hooks/` doesn't exist or referenced files are missing, every Stop hook silently fails — including the one that writes session cost data to `~/.cx/session-cost.jsonl`. Symptom: the dashboard stops updating after the install breaks. Doctor now lists this as a hard failure with the exact fix command: `ln -sfn <install>/lib ~/.construct/lib`.

### Fixed

- Performance page was rendering 10 test fixtures as if they were real session reviews. Every file matched `test-*-mock.json` — seeds left over from tests; the production review generator was never wired. `GET /api/performance/reviews` now filters those out and returns `{reviews: [], total: 0, mockFiltered: 10, generatorImplemented: false}`. The page shows an honest empty state: "The end-of-session review generator is not yet wired. The schema + UI are in place — once `construct review run` or the session-stop hook produces real data, it will land here. Filtered out N mock fixture files." No more fake data parading as real.

### Changed

- Billing-mode auto-detection so the default doesn't mislead. Previously the default was `metered` which displayed theoretical dollar values to subscription users who hadn't paid any of it. `resolveBillingMode` now returns `{mode, source}`: explicit env / config values still win, but otherwise the mode is inferred from whether `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set (presence → metered, absence → subscription — covers the dominant Claude Code install path). The dashboard tags inferred values with a `(auto)` pip on the corresponding card and shows an amber "⚠ Billing mode auto-detected. Confirm in Config →" banner so the user always knows the value was guessed and can correct it. Verified end-to-end: with no env / no config, the page renders subscription labels with the auto banner; setting `costs.billingMode` in `construct.config.json` removes the banner and locks the labeling.

### Added

- Billing-mode awareness — `construct.config.json.costs.billingMode` accepts `metered | subscription | mixed` (default `metered`). Surfaces through `/api/insights` → `cost.billingMode` so the dashboard can label numbers honestly. **Subscription mode** changes two cards: the **Today's spend** hero card becomes "Today's usage (metered-equiv)" with an inline `i subscription` pip and the explanatory caveat "metered-equiv only · real bill is your monthly plan" (no progress bar against a cap — that doesn't apply on flat-rate plans). The **Cache savings** card becomes **"Token cache efficiency"** showing `N% efficient` (share of input tokens served from cache instead of consuming fresh-input quota), with a footnote that the metered-equivalent dollar savings are still computed but don't represent money on a flat-rate plan. `CONSTRUCT_BILLING_MODE` env var wins over the JSON value. The Config editor exposes the choice as a dropdown ("metered (API key, pay-per-token)" / "subscription (Claude Pro / Max / Team)" / "mixed (both)") with help text explaining each mode and a note that the daily cap "has no meaning under pure subscription billing."

### Changed

- Cache-savings honesty + tooltips on technical terms. The cache-savings card previously rendered a confidently-precise dollar value computed against a single model's price; now it renders as `≈ $N` (with the `≈` prefix), splits the actual 7d cost out from the hypothetical no-cache cost, and surfaces every assumption: token-weighted avg input price across the models that actually ran in the window (returned as `modelsInWindow` from `summarizeCostLedger`), the math (`cacheReadTokens × avgInputPrice × 0.9 − cacheCreationTokens × avgInputPrice × 0.25`), and an "approximation" explainer string. Hover the "weighted across N models · approx" footer for the full math + per-model breakdown. Added a reusable `<Hint>` component (cursor:help + dotted underline + `title` attribute + aria-label) wrapped around every technical term on Mission Control: **System health** ("count of running local services responding to health probes"), **Today's spend** ("USD spent on LLM provider calls today, from the cost ledger"), **cache hit** ("share of input tokens served from prompt cache instead of paid full-price input"), **Active work** (P0–P3 priority tiers explained on the chip hover), **Cost trend (7d)**, **Token mix** (cache-read / cache-write / input / output / reasoning all defined), **Latency** (p50 / p95 / p99 percentiles defined), **TTFT** ("time-to-first-token: lag until first character streams"), **Throughput** ("tokens-per-second of streamed response"), **Error rate** (red / amber thresholds), **Retrieval recall@5** (the IR metric + MRR defined). 23 tooltips on Mission Control alone.

### Fixed

- Dark / light mode coverage on legacy pages. `dashboard/src/App.css` had `.card { @apply bg-white border-gray-200 }` which was overriding the themed `.card` from `index.css` — every legacy page (Beads / Doctor / Workflow / Approvals / Snapshots / Knowledge / Models / MCP / Providers / Plugins / Artifacts / Infrastructure) was forcibly white in dark mode. Rewrote `App.css` to route through CSS vars (`var(--surface)`, `var(--text)`, `var(--text-dim)`) and added a dark-mode shim in `index.css` that re-points the hardcoded Tailwind palette to theme-aware tokens: `bg-white` → `var(--surface)`, `bg-gray-{50,100,200}` → `--legacy-gray-{50,100,200}` (translucent overlays so the surface shows through), `text-gray-{500..900}` → `--legacy-gray-{500..900}` (translucent whites with rising opacity), `border-gray-*` → `var(--border)`. Badge colors (bg-red-100 / text-red-800 / bg-blue-100 / bg-green-100 / bg-violet-100 / bg-amber-50 etc.) get translucent backgrounds + lighter foregrounds so they stay legible on dark surfaces. Both `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` and `[data-theme="dark"]` are covered so the dashboard's explicit theme toggle works alongside OS-level preference.

### Added

- Observability deepening — round 5. **Cache savings card** translates the 99%-cache-hit telemetry into a real dollar value: `summarizeCostLedger` computes `cacheSavings = {netSavedUsd, grossSavedUsd, writePremiumUsd, wouldHaveCostUsd, savingsRatio, pricedAgainstModel}` by priceing the cache-read tokens at the 0.9× saving over uncached input and subtracting the 0.25× cache-write premium, against the dominant model's pricing entry. Caught a real $469.69 (57%) saving on the live ledger. **Token mix card** renders a stacked horizontal bar of the 7-day usage split (input · cache-read · cache-write · output · reasoning) with proportional segments + a chip legend showing absolute counts and percentages — surfaces the 99%-cache-read pattern visually rather than burying it as a number. **Retrieval recall@5 sparkline**: `construct evals retrieval` now appends each run to `~/.cx/evals/retrieval-history.jsonl`; the recall card renders an SVG polyline of the last up-to-30 runs as a cyan trend line so regressions are visible at a glance. **Per-agent latency table** added next to the per-model table when generation observations carry agent attribution (extracted from `metadata.agent` or the `llm.<agent>` name convention emitted by the opencode plugin) — shows generations, avg latency, errors per `cx-*` agent, so the operator can see whether a specific persona is slow without correlating manually.
- Broader LLM observability — round 4. Mission Control gains a five-card **Observability (last 7d)** section that goes well beyond cost: **Latency** card shows p50 / p95 / p99 of total generation latency, **TTFT** card shows p50 / p95 of `completionStartTime - startTime` (time-to-first-token) with a `sampleSize` of generations that reported it, **Throughput** card shows tok/sec p50 / p95 derived from `completionTokens / (endTime - completionStartTime)`, **Error rate** card shows percentage of generation observations with `level: 'error'` colored red over 5% / amber over 1%, **Retrieval recall@5** card shows recall + MRR + p95 retrieval latency from the last `construct evals retrieval` run. New `summarizeLangfuseLatency(env, {days})` aggregator fetches `/api/public/observations?type=GENERATION` (with a `?fromStartTime=…` first attempt and a parameterless fallback for older Langfuse builds that 400 the param), computes quantiles via `quantile()` helper, and returns a per-model breakdown (`{model, traces, avgMs, errors}`) that the dashboard renders as a "Latency by model" table when generations exist. `summarizeRetrievalEval()` reads `~/.cx/evals/retrieval-latest.json` and surfaces recall, MRR, latency, empty-rate, fallback-rate. `construct evals retrieval` now writes the result to that cache path on every run so the dashboard card reflects the most-recent eval without re-running on each refresh. Each card has graceful empty / unreachable / misconfigured states with concrete next-action CTAs (e.g. "Run `construct evals retrieval`", "No TTFT data — model didn't report completionStartTime"). Observability has the same single-call refresh model as the rest of the page: one `/api/insights` request, every section comes back.
- Dashboard restructure round 3 — Mission Control layout reshape + model-provider single source of truth + Langfuse cost trend + vector card. Mission Control reshaped into three readable rows: **Hero** (System health + Today's spend + Active work as oversized numbers with caps + priority chips), **Trend** (7-day cost bar chart from new Langfuse daily aggregator + compact Services strip on the right), **Secondary** (.cx/ disk + Handoffs + Intake + Vector index as smaller cards), then a Providers row and Authentication footer. Bars are aurora-gradient when error-free, switch to status-degraded when a day has errors, dim when trace count is zero. New `summarizeModelProviders(env)` in `lib/server/insights.mjs` enumerates Anthropic / OpenAI / OpenRouter / Ollama / Groq / Mistral / Gemini with `{id, displayName, envKey, docsUrl, configured, state}` so the **Mission Control providers row** and the **/providers page** read from the same array — no drift. Providers page now shows the connection table with status pips + env-var names + "Get key ↗" docs links + a 4-step add-a-provider guide. New `summarizeVectorStore(env)` reports `{mode, bytes, records, model, updatedAt, path}` from the local vector index; Mission Control Vector card shows record count + file size, or the typed empty state with a `construct ingest` CTA. New `summarizeLangfuseCostTrend({days: 7})` buckets traces by day from `/api/public/traces?fromTimestamp=…` and feeds the bar chart with `{day, costUsd, traces, errors}`. Whole render pulls from a single `/api/insights` call so refresh is one round-trip.
- Dashboard restructure round 2 — closes the gaps raised after the Phase 10 review. **Sidebar grouped** into five named sections (Overview / Authoring / Operations / Models & integrations / System) with small uppercase headings between groups, so related pages live next to each other instead of in declaration order. **All 28 cx-* agent prompts editable** — `specialists/prompts/<n>.md` is now a first-class override category (`category: 'agents'`) reachable from the new "Agent prompts" page; previously only `personas/construct.md` showed up. **Inline alias rename** on the sidebar wordmark — click the gradient name, edit in-place, Enter saves via `PUT /api/project-config`, Escape cancels; the page no longer relies on the buried Config-page alias field. **Performance page** reads `~/.cx/performance-reviews/*.json` (auto-generated by the session-optimize hook) and renders per-agent stats (invocations, avg score, fail rate, trend, status) in a table with an inline Feedback affordance per row that writes to `.cx/feedback/<ts>-<agent>.json` and appends `performance.feedback.submitted` to the audit log — the operator-feedback channel that seeds proposed prompt updates for the construct persona to review. New `GET /api/performance/reviews` + `POST /api/performance/feedback` endpoints back the page. Langfuse insight card "Top models by cost" no longer dumps everything into an "unknown" bucket — model extraction now also reads `output.model`, `output.costModel`, `input.metadata.model`, and the first observation's model field, dropping the bucket entirely when the trace truly has no model attribution (a Langfuse data-shape issue, not a rendering decision).
- Handoff hygiene rails — closes the gap raised post-Phase-10. Handoff files (`.cx/handoffs/<date>-<slug>.md`) accumulated indefinitely with no retention, no prune branch, no doctor surface, and no dashboard visibility — even though the budget primitive listed `handoffs` as a soft-warn category. Schema now defines `resources.disk.handoffsMaxDays` (default 30) and `handoffsMaxItems` (default 50). `lib/resources/budget.mjs::planPrune` gains a handoffs branch that adds prune actions for files past either cap. New `lib/handoffs/inventory.mjs::summarizeHandoffs(projectRoot, env)` returns `{total, bytes, oldestAgeDays, pastRetentionCount, resolvedCount, resolvedFiles, recent}` — `resolvedCount` cross-references each handoff's bead-id mentions against `bd list --status closed` so the operator can see which files reference only-closed work (safe to prune). New `construct handoffs status` CLI prints the inventory + warnings. `construct doctor` surfaces handoff hygiene as a probe with three states: empty (✓), under retention (✓ with count + oldest age), past retention (warn with `construct prune` hint). New Handoffs insight card in Mission Control sits between Intake and Langfuse, showing count + oldest age + the same three-state breakdown with the retention cap inline. Pruning honors the same hard-reject / soft-warn classification (handoffs are soft-warn — never refuse a write, only warn).

### Added

- Accessibility + polish — Phases 9 + 10 (construct-0qa + construct-25k). Command palette (⌘K / Ctrl+K) — single consistent navigation primitive for neurodivergent-forward keyboard flow: filterable list of every nav item, arrow-keys to move, Enter to navigate, Esc to close, aria-modal + role="listbox" with `aria-selected` on the active option. Mouse hover + click also work. Theme toggle in sidebar with `auto | light | dark` choices persisted to `localStorage.cx-theme` — auto follows `prefers-color-scheme`, explicit modes set `data-theme` on `<html>`. Audit log page reads `.cx/audit.jsonl` (newest first, capped at 200 entries by default) with a `GET /api/audit?limit=N` endpoint behind it — every config / persona / skill / rules / restore write produces an entry. Sidebar footer now shows the keyboard hint, the theme switcher, and the docs link in a single zone so all global controls live in one predictable place. Three-channel status (color + icon + text) is already in place across every state pip via the earlier .pip primitive; `:focus-visible` cyan ring + skip-to-main link + `prefers-reduced-motion` honored were established in Phase 0 and confirmed in this pass.
- Persona / Skill / Rules editors — Phases 4 + 5 (construct-kqs + construct-0bz). New shared `OverrideEditor` component (`dashboard/src/pages/Editor.tsx`) renders a left-rail list + right-pane markdown textarea wired to the override resolver: any save routes through `applyEdit(projectRoot, category, name, content)`, prior content auto-snapshots to `.cx/backups/<category>/<name>.<iso>.<ext>`, and the resolved active file at next read is the override. Three pages wired from the same component: Personas (1 shipped), Skills (131 shipped), Rules. Each page lists every shipped + custom item, marks `edited` pip on items with a live override, filter input narrows the list, and per-file Backups section shows every snapshot with a one-click Restore button that re-applies the backup content through the resolver. Save is gated on dirty state; Discard reverts to the last-loaded original. New REST endpoints: `GET /api/overrides/<category>` returns `{category, items: [{name, hasOverride, source, custom}]}`, `GET /api/overrides/<category>/<name>` returns `{content, source, path}`, `GET /api/overrides/<category>/<name>?action=backups` lists the snapshot stack, `PUT /api/overrides/<category>/<name>` accepts `{content}`, `POST /api/overrides/<category>/<name>?action=restore` accepts `{backupFilename}`. Every successful write appends `{ts, action: "override.<category>.<name>.write", backupPath, bytes}` to `.cx/audit.jsonl` so the edit trail is durable. Custom personas / skills live at `.cx/<category>/custom/<name>.md` (gitignored) and surface in the list with the `custom` flag — first save of a new name creates the file there.
- Dashboard Config editor — Phase 3 (construct-hmd). The existing Config page is replaced with a structured form editor for `construct.config.json` covering four sections: **General** (alias text input, deployment-mode dropdown, MCP-broker dropdown, auto-embed checkbox, telemetry checkbox), **Model tier overrides** (per-tier text inputs for reasoning / standard / fast / embeddings with the corresponding `CX_MODEL_*` env var labeled), **Resource limits** (.cx/ total cap MB, trace retention days, backup retention days), and **Cost ceilings** (daily total cap USD, per-persona default USD, enforce on/off). Each field carries a help line describing its effect. Save button is disabled until the form is dirty; a Pending changes section shows a per-line diff (`-` / `+`) preview before save. Discard reverts to original. New backend routes: `GET /api/project-config` returns `{path, source, config, errors}` from `loadProjectConfig`; `PUT/PATCH /api/project-config` validates against the v1 schema, writes to `construct.config.json` at the project root, and appends a `{ts, action: 'project-config.write', path, keys}` entry to `.cx/audit.jsonl` so every config change is traceable. Validation errors return 400 with the schema-error list (e.g. "deployment.mode: must be one of solo/team/enterprise").
- Cost ceilings (Phase 7b — construct-79z). The existing env-driven `lib/cost-ledger.mjs::checkBudget` now reads `construct.config.json.costs.budgets.{<personaId>|default|total}.dailyUsd` and `construct.config.json.costs.enforce` as the canonical source, with env vars (`CONSTRUCT_BUDGET_<PERSONA>`, `CONSTRUCT_BUDGET_TOTAL`, `CONSTRUCT_BUDGET_ENFORCE`) as the back-compat override layer — env wins when set. New `enforcementActive()` export resolves the same precedence for any caller that needs the bare boolean. `construct costs status` prints today's total spend vs cap, enforcement mode, per-persona breakdown with usage %. `construct costs check <persona>` returns the typed checkBudget result and exits 2 when not allowed. `/api/insights` `cost` block now ships `budget: {todayUsd, capUsd, usageRatio, enforce, byPersona}`. Dashboard Cost card now shows today's spend vs cap with an aurora < 80% / amber 80–100% / red > 100% progress bar and an `enforce on/off` indicator — symmetric with the .cx/ disk usage card. 9 unit tests pin env > config > default precedence for personaBudget / totalBudget / enforcementActive across all five branches.
- Resource budgets — Phase 6 (construct-dsr). `construct.config.json.resources` schema accepts `disk` block with per-category caps (`tracesMaxDays`, `intakeArchiveMaxItems/Days`, `taskGraphsMaxItems/Days`, `workerLogsMaxMb/Days`, `sessionsMaxItems`, `backupsMaxDays`, `totalCxMaxMb`) and a `process` block with RSS ceilings. Defaults: `.cx/` total 2GB, traces 30d, intake archive 500 items / 90d, task graphs 200/90d, worker logs 100MB/14d, backups 60d (matches the override resolver retention). New `lib/resources/budget.mjs` exports `measureUsage(projectRoot, env)` (walks the .cx/ tree per category, returns bytes + file counts + cap), `reserveOrReject(projectRoot, category, sizeBytes, env)` (called before each write — hard-rejects on `traces` / `worker-logs` because observability is replaceable, soft-warns on load-bearing R&D state like `intake-archive` / `task-graphs` / `backups` / `sessions` / `handoffs`), `planPrune` (pure planner returning typed actions: age cap, item cap, size cap), `executePrune` (applies the actions, returns bytesFreed). New `construct prune [--dry-run]` walks .cx/ and applies / previews the prune plan with a per-category summary. New `construct resources status` prints per-category usage + enforcement classification + total .cx/ usage vs cap. `construct doctor` now reports `.cx/ NMB / capMB (N%)` with three states: ✓ ≤80%, ⚠ 80–100%, ✗ over (fix-hint points at `construct prune`). Dashboard insights panel gains a `.cx/ disk` card with a live usage bar that gradients aurora < 80% / amber 80–100% / red over. 15 unit tests pin the soft-warn/hard-reject split, the per-category prune actions (age cap, item cap, total cap), the planner-vs-executor split, and that unknown categories don't enforce.
- Dashboard insights panel (Phase 8 partial — construct-5vd). Mission Control now surfaces live data from every Construct backend in a single section with typed states + graceful degradation. Four insight cards (Beads / Cost-24h / Intake / Langfuse) each carry a status pip (`ok | empty | misconfigured | unreachable`) and a guidance message when degraded. Beads card shows total + open/closed/in_progress counts + P0..P3 breakdown. Cost card shows 24h spend + 7d spend + cache hit rate read from the `~/.cx/session-cost.jsonl` ledger via `summarizeCostData`. Intake card shows pending/processed/skipped queue depth from `.cx/intake/`. Langfuse card shows trace count + total cost + observation count + an Open Langfuse link. A "Top models by cost" table renders when Langfuse has traces — model name, trace count, USD cost descending. New `lib/server/insights.mjs` aggregates the four sources behind `GET /api/insights`; every section caught in its own try/catch so one unavailable source doesn't break the page. `GET /api/alias` returns the resolved alias for the wordmark fetcher introduced in the alias commit.

### Fixed

- Dashboard `/api/beads` returned zero issues when the server ran in a git worktree — bd was looking at the worktree's empty `.beads/` instead of the canonical DB in the main repo. The endpoint now resolves to the main worktree via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` and runs `bd list` there, so all 50 issues (open + closed) render in the Beads page regardless of which worktree the user is in.
- Mission Control "No services reported" rendered even when the status API was returning healthy services — the page was reading `data.services` while the API ships them under `data.system.services`. Fixed the path; Dashboard / Langfuse / Memory (cm) / OpenCode cards now render with their live status pips and Open buttons.

### Added

- Alias system — Phase 2 (construct-9hy). `lib/config/alias.mjs` exports `resolveAlias({cwd, env, homeDir})` with the precedence `CONSTRUCT_ALIAS` env > `~/.construct/config.json.aliasOverride` (user-level) > `construct.config.json.alias` (project-level) > "Construct" default. `applyAliasToTemplate(text, alias)` replaces `{{alias}}` tokens in persona prompts and CLI banners with the resolved name. Dashboard sidebar wordmark now fetches from the new `GET /api/alias` endpoint and renders the resolved name in the aurora gradient — set `alias` in `construct.config.json` (or `CONSTRUCT_ALIAS=Atlas`) and the dashboard wordmark reflects it on reload. 10 unit tests pin the four-level precedence, whitespace handling, the `{{alias}}` template substitution, and the empty-value default fallback.
- Override resolver — single helper for the {original → override → backup} pattern across every editable Construct primitive (Phase 1, construct-5px). `lib/overrides/resolver.mjs` exports `resolveOverride(projectRoot, category, name)` (returns `{path, source: 'override'|'original'|'missing', overrideExists, originalPath}`), `readResolved` (read the active content regardless of source), `applyEdit(projectRoot, category, name, newContent)` (writes the override to `.cx/<category>/<name>.<ext>` AND snapshots prior content to `.cx/backups/<category>/<name>.<iso>.<ext>` — no-op when content is identical), `listBackups`, `restoreFromBackup`, `pruneBackups({maxDays=60})`, `describeOverrides` (per-category summary). First call to `applyEdit` auto-populates `.cx/.gitignore` with `backups/` so backups are never accidentally committed. Supported categories: `personas`, `skills`, `rules`, `contracts` (singleFile: `specialists/contracts.json`), `role-manifests` (singleFile: `specialists/role-manifests.json`). Override semantics is full file replacement — decided 2026-05-14, no section-merge. Backup retention is 60 days (decided same date). 16 unit tests pin resolution precedence (override > original > missing), first-edit vs subsequent-edit backup behavior, the `.cx/.gitignore` auto-write, restore round-trip, the 60-day prune cap, and the unknown-category guard. `construct overrides list|backups|prune|resolve` CLI exposes the surface for ops + scripting.
- Override resolver primitives unblock the persona editor (Phase 4) and skill editor (Phase 5) since both consume the same {edit, backup, restore} contract.

### Changed

- Pricing catalog priority flipped — **LiteLLM (live, daily-refreshed) now wins over the static fallback table** for paid models. Previously the static table was authoritative, which meant published Anthropic / OpenAI price changes never reached Construct until a maintainer hand-edited `lib/telemetry/langfuse-model-sync.mjs`. New priority: LiteLLM > OpenRouter > static. Static stays as the offline / pre-warm safety net. GitHub Copilot remains pinned at $0 per-token (per-request billing). `buildPricingCatalog` was reordered so static entries are seeded first, then OpenRouter, then LiteLLM — last write wins. The `source` field is renamed `static-fallback` so call-site logs and doctor output make the priority explicit. Behavior note: with this flip live, paid Opus 4.5/4.7 sessions are now priced from LiteLLM (currently $5/$25 per M) instead of the static $15/$75 — if your operator-of-record disagrees with LiteLLM's number, `construct pricing check` surfaces every >25% divergence and the static table remains the override point until Phase 7b ships a config-level model price override.

### Added

- `construct pricing` CLI surface for the live catalog. `construct pricing status` reports cache freshness (file present? ageHours? stale per 24h TTL? source: litellm | static-only) plus the resolved per-tier price for `CX_MODEL_{REASONING,STANDARD,FAST}` with its source. `construct pricing refresh [--force]` re-fetches LiteLLM bypassing the 24h cache when `--force` is supplied, returning the divergence list against the static fallback. `construct pricing check` exits 2 when any model's LiteLLM price diverges >25% from the static entry, with each line spelling out the absolute prices and the delta percentage. `lib/telemetry/langfuse-model-sync.mjs` exports `checkPricingDivergence(litellmModels)` for programmatic use; called by both the CLI and the doctor probe so anywhere the catalog is loaded, the same guard is applied. The 25% threshold was chosen to allow for normal provider drift (5-15% is common) without tripping on legitimate price changes — anything bigger is worth a human look.
- `construct doctor` now surfaces pricing divergence as an advisory check ("Pricing divergence on N model(s) — run `construct pricing check`") alongside the existing catalog-freshness check. Both are degradation signals, not hard failures: a stale or divergent catalog still produces usable cost numbers from the fallback.

### Fixed

- Cost-tracking underbilling on Anthropic sessions: opencode's runtime plugin was extracting cache 5m/1h tokens only from the legacy `cache_creation_5m_input_tokens` paths, never from the new `info.tokens.cache.{write_5m, write_1h}` shape OpenCode actually emits. Result: every Sonnet/Opus session with cache-creation tokens was being billed short by the cache-write tier (typically 1.25x–2x the input rate × the cache-write token count). Extended `sanitizeUsage` in `lib/opencode-runtime-plugin.mjs` to read both shapes. Caught by the new 3-way reconciliation test.

### Added

- Cost-tracking audit + hardening — Phase 7a (construct-6ae). Critical-path gate before any cost-ceiling work (Phase 7b). New `tests/cost-reconciliation.test.mjs` pins the 3-way invariant that any usage payload produces an identical cost across (A) `buildRuntimeTracePayload` metadata, (B) the cost the opencode plugin attaches to `ingest.generation`, and (C) a direct `estimateUsageCost()` call — the canonical value the ledger and dashboard read from. Static-table fixture (Sonnet 4.6) so the test is hermetic — no network, no catalog-refresh dependency. The reconciliation test caught the cache-token underbilling bug listed in Fixed. Additional invariants pinned: every cache tier and the reasoning bucket are billed (no token dropped, no double-count); Ollama + unknown models return `costUsd: 0` with `costSource: "unavailable"`; reasoning tokens price identically to the same count of output tokens (output rate, never input rate); cache reads bill strictly less than uncached input. `lib/telemetry/langfuse-model-sync.mjs` exports `describePricingCatalogFreshness()` reading the `~/.cx/pricing-cache.json` mtime and reporting `{present, ageMs, ageHours, stale, source, message}` — `construct doctor` surfaces this as an advisory ("Pricing catalog fresh (Nh old)" or the stale-message guidance). Cache 5m/1h + cache-creation-total + reasoning tokens now surface in Langfuse `generation` metadata from both telemetry paths (`opencode-runtime-plugin.mjs` and `opencode-telemetry.mjs`) so a Langfuse-side cost reconciliation can be done with the full breakdown, not just the read-cache count.
- Dashboard design system v2 (aurora) — Phase 0 visual foundation. `dashboard/src/index.css` carries solid white/black bases driven by `--ink-50..900` + `--bg / --surface / --border / --text` CSS vars; auto-switches via `prefers-color-scheme` and supports an explicit `[data-theme="light|dark"]` override. Aurora palette (`--aurora-mint #00ffaa → --aurora-cyan #00d4ff → --aurora-violet #b76eff → --aurora-pink #ff66c4`) renders the wordmark gradient (`.text-aurora`), card hover borders, button surfaces, and a soft radial wash fixed behind content (static, no animation). Component primitives: `.card` (aurora-gradient border on hover), `.btn` / `.btn-primary`, `.pip` + `.pip-{healthy,degraded,down}` (three-channel status — color + before-dot + text), `.hairline-aurora`. `prefers-reduced-motion` zeros animations + transitions; `:focus-visible` shows a 2px cyan ring offset 2px. `tailwind.config.js` exposes semantic utilities (`bg-bg`, `text-text-muted`, `border-border`, `border-aurora-cyan`) so components don't hard-code hex values. `App.tsx` rewrites the sidebar with the aurora wordmark, a left-cyan-border active-link affordance (replaces the saturated indigo block), a skip-to-main-content link for keyboard users, and a documentation footer link. `Resources.tsx` rewrites as Mission Control: single-screen system snapshot polling `/api/status` every 5s, typed empty / error / no-data states each carrying a concrete next-action CTA (no bare errors), three-channel status pips, services / features / auth sections in a predictable order. Built bundle synced to `lib/server/static/`.
- `construct.config.json` project-level config primitive (Phase 0 of the dashboard rebuild epic — construct-1ju/pca). New module `lib/config/project-config.mjs` exposes `findProjectConfigPath` (walks up to git root), `loadProjectConfig` (parse + validate + secret-interpolate), `writeProjectConfig`, `initProjectConfig`, `getConfigValue` / `setConfigValue` (dotted-path accessors), and `resolveSetting({config, jsonPath, env, envKey, defaultValue})` encoding the env > config > default precedence. `lib/config/schema.mjs` defines the v1 schema with hand-rolled validation (no AJV / zod — Construct's startup runs before npm install in some bootstrap paths). Secret-interpolation rule: any string value matching `^\$[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*$` resolves from `process.env` at load time, so the JSON config carries pointers (`"$OPENAI_API_KEY"`) instead of secret literals. v1 covers `alias`, `deployment {mode, mcpBroker, projectName, tenantId}`, `providers`, `models {reasoning, standard, fast, embeddings}`, `autoEmbed`, `telemetry {enabled, langfuse}` — additive `resources` + `costs` blocks accepted for forward-compat with Phases 6 and 7b. `construct config show|get|set|init|validate` CLI subcommands operate on the file; the existing `construct config mode` keeps writing to `~/.construct/config.env` for back-compat. `getDeploymentMode(env, {cwd})` now reads through the new primitive — env still wins, then `construct.config.json.deployment.mode`, then the default. `construct doctor` reports config validity status. 25 unit tests pin schema validation (required fields, enums, version mismatch), secret interpolation (env-pointer resolution, missing-var → null, recursive into arrays + nested objects), path resolution (walk up to git root, sub-directory invocation), the env-over-config-over-default precedence, deep-merge over defaults so users can keep their config minimal, and refusal to write an invalid config.
- Cross-platform session-start parity. `lib/intake/session-prelude.mjs` exports `buildIntakePrelude({cwd, env})`, `buildBrokerStatusLine({env})`, and `buildSessionPrelude({cwd, env})` — pure builders that render the R&D-intake count + triage block and the MCP-broker status line. Claude's SessionStart hook (`lib/hooks/session-start.mjs`) now consumes the shared helpers instead of carrying the inline intake-queue read, and the OpenCode plugin (`lib/opencode-runtime-plugin.mjs`) exports `emitSessionPrelude(event, {client, env, cwd})` which fires once per session id on `session.created` and surfaces the prelude via `client.app.log({level: "info"})`. Both platforms now show: "Pending R&D intake (N)" with up to three triage lines + the close-via-`construct intake done` instruction, and "MCP broker: on/off · deployment mode: solo/team/enterprise" with the ApprovalRequired reminder when active. Solves the cross-platform gap where the R&D-loop's most user-visible status was claude-only. 12 unit tests across two files pin the builder shapes (empty-queue branch, multi-packet count, broker on/off matrix, override precedence) and the opencode integration path (once-per-session de-dup, no-op for non-session.created events, log-channel routing).
- CI paths filter: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains a `changes` detector job using `dorny/paths-filter@v3` that computes per-target outputs (`code`, `retrieval`, `postgres`, `deps`, `agents`, `docs`, `templates`, `workflow`). Each downstream job (test matrix, retrieval evals, dep audit, postgres-integration, docs drift, lint-comments, lint-agents, lint-templates, gates-audit) gates on `needs: changes` + an `if:` matching its own scope. Doc-only PRs (`docs/**`, `README.md`, etc.) no longer trigger the unit-test matrix, retrieval evals, or postgres-integration; persona/*.md or `agents/**` edits skip postgres-integration and the retrieval evals; workflow edits force the full matrix so changes here can't bypass the gates they configure. The secret-scan job runs unconditionally on every push and PR — security gating must not depend on path scope. Mirrors the pattern already in `.github/workflows/pages.yml`. Closes construct-3nb (F7 from the 2026-05-13 UX audit).
- Local Langfuse zero-touch sign-in: `lib/server/langfuse-login.mjs` ships a magic-link bridge that fetches the CSRF token from the locally-running Langfuse, then returns an auto-submit HTML form POSTing the seeded `admin@construct.local` / `construct-admin` credentials to Langfuse's NextAuth callback. The browser lands on `${LANGFUSE_LOCAL_BASEURL}/` already authenticated — no manual signup, no password copy/paste. `LANGFUSE_LOGIN_DEFAULTS` reads from the existing `LANGFUSE_LOCAL_*` constants in `lib/services/langfuse.mjs` so the seeded compose creds and the bridge stay in sync. 502 with a clear "run `construct up` first" message when Langfuse is unreachable. 6 unit tests pin the HTML escaping (per hidden field), the 200/502 status branching, and the constants-alignment.
- Three small Docker/Langfuse fixes batched together: `construct doctor` now probes `cm` availability and Docker daemon reachability (both as advisories — neither is a hard failure since Construct degrades gracefully without them, but doctor previously reported all-green even when both were missing, contradicting `construct up`); `construct up` on macOS now auto-launches Docker Desktop when the CLI is present but the daemon is down and polls `docker info` up to 60s for it to come up (opt-out via `CONSTRUCT_DISABLE_DOCKER_AUTOSTART=1`, no-op on Linux/Windows); and `construct up` no longer prints `Langfuse: undefined` when `LANGFUSE_BASEURL` points at a remote instance — the `configured` service record now carries an explicit `note` with the remote URL.
- Beads hygiene automation closes the gap between the contract in `rules/common/beads-hygiene.md` and runtime enforcement. Two new library modules under `lib/beads/`: (1) `auto-close.mjs` parses commit ranges for `closes / fixes / resolves construct-xxx` references (case-insensitive, plural / singular, multi-id de-duped), then invokes `bd close <id>` for each unique match with an `Auto-closed on merge <sha>: <subject>` reason — runnable standalone via `--range` for backfill, and invoked from `.beads/hooks/post-merge` on every merge so the tracker matches reality without manual intervention; (2) `drift.mjs` detects three classes of tracker drift via injectable runners: stale-open (open beads untouched past 14 days, advisory), stuck-in-progress (in_progress beads untouched past 3 days, hard signal that the claiming session didn't follow through), and merge-drift (open beads whose title shares 3+ significant tokens with a recent commit subject, i.e. the work shipped but the bead didn't close). Both modules expose pure functions for the dispatcher to consume and CLI entrypoints for the hook layer. New `construct beads drift [--json] [--stale-open-days=N] [--stuck-in-progress-days=N] [--merge-lookback=N]` reports drift and exits 2 when stuck-in-progress or merge-drift exists. New `construct beads drift-report` writes the report to `.cx/handoffs/beads-drift-<date>.md` for the weekly snapshot. `construct doctor` now surfaces hygiene drift as its own check with a fix-hint pointing at `construct beads drift`. 19 new tests cover the parse-grammar variants, the auto-close driver, and the three drift detectors.
- Worker plane Docker image + team-mode compose topology. New `Dockerfile.worker` builds a minimal Alpine + Node runtime that ships `lib/`, `agents/`, `db/`, `rules/`, `scripts/`, and `bin/`, runs as an unprivileged `construct` user, and ENTRYPOINTs to a long-running worker loop. The loop (`lib/worker/entrypoint.mjs`) claims pending intake items from `PostgresIntakeQueue` via `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, runs each through `runJob` with `allowedPaths: [/work]` so the bind-mounted workspace is the only writable surface, attaches evidence to the originating task graph node when the packet names `graphId` + `nodeId`, surfaces failures as `blockedPacket(taskId, reason, attempted)` markings in the queue, and exits cleanly when the queue stays empty for `--idle-timeout-seconds` (default 300s) so a scheduler can scale to zero. `docker-compose.team.yml` wires Postgres + a worker pool (replicas via `CONSTRUCT_WORKER_REPLICAS`) sharing `DATABASE_URL` and `LANGFUSE_*` env. Six unit tests verify the loop: idle-timeout exit, passed/failed/timed-out job paths, multi-claim ordering, `stopAfter` cap, and the sql-or-injected-queue precondition. Closes the construct-9dx follow-up filed during PR 8.
- Distribution: project-local launcher now falls through to Docker when no Node/binary runtime resolves. `.construct/run.mjs` resolution order is now dev path → `node_modules/@geraldmaron/construct` → `npx` → globally-installed `construct` → cached binary in `.construct/cache/bin/` → **Docker** (`ghcr.io/geraldmaron/construct:<pinned-version>` with the project bind-mounted at `/work`) → typed failure. The Docker branch confirms the daemon is reachable (not just that the CLI is installed) and disables itself when `CONSTRUCT_DISABLE_DOCKER=1` so CI runners can fail fast. The no-runtime failure message now names `docker pull ghcr.io/geraldmaron/construct` alongside the bootstrap shims as install options. Closes the cloner-without-global-install story end-to-end: peers who clone a project that pins Construct can run hooks via the project-local launcher regardless of language ecosystem.
- `worker_run` MCP tool exposes `runJob` with task-graph evidence linkage. Wraps `lib/worker/run.mjs:runJob` — bounded execution with hard timeout, restricted env, and path-policy denial (default `allowedPaths` is `[cwd]`). When `graphId` + `nodeId` are supplied, an evidence record is built via `evidenceFromJobResult` and appended to the named task graph node so it can transition to `done`. Emits `worker.started` / `worker.completed` / `evidence.recorded` trace events all correlated by `traceId`. Errors are returned in the `error` field (e.g. path-policy denial) rather than thrown. Schema registered in `lib/mcp/server.mjs`. Closes the wiring gap for `runJob` — agents can now produce evidence-bound work without dropping out of MCP. With this commit the Tier 1 dispatch-hot-path wiring epic (`construct-8yo`) and all four sub-beads close out: orchestration_policy now surfaces per-specialist context packets + auto-generates task graphs from intake; broker_check + worker_run expose the broker and worker primitives at the MCP layer.
- `orchestration_policy` MCP tool now auto-generates a task graph when an `intakeId` is supplied. The tool reads the pending intake packet, calls `generateTaskGraphFromTriage` (one node per persona in the recommendedChain), persists the graph to `.cx/task-graphs/<id>.json`, and emits a `task_graph.created` trace event whose `traceId` is either the caller-supplied id or a freshly minted one. The graph is returned in `taskGraph` alongside the existing route. Unknown intake ids return `intakeError` rather than throwing so the call stays cheap. Closes the wiring gap for `generateTaskGraphFromTriage` — an agent dispatching on an intake packet no longer needs a separate manual `construct graph from-intake` step.
- `broker_check` MCP tool exposes `policyDecision` for pre-action queries. Agents call this BEFORE attempting a high-risk action so the response (`allowed` / `approvalRequired` / `reason` / `source`) can be surfaced in the agent's voice rather than triggering a denial after the fact. Solo mode short-circuits to `brokerActive: false` with `allowed: true` so the call costs no policy work when the broker is inactive; team / enterprise mode reads `specialists/role-manifests.json` via the existing `policyDecision` path. Every call (active or inactive) emits a `tool.called` trace event for audit-trail parity. `CONSTRUCT_MCP_BROKER=on` engages the broker in solo mode for testing. This closes the wiring gap for `Broker.invoke` — agents can now consult the policy gate at handoff time rather than relying on the broker wrapping every tool dispatch.
- `orchestration_policy` MCP tool now returns per-specialist context packets. When the caller supplies a `candidates` artifact list (and optionally `triage` and `budget`), the tool calls `buildContextPacket(role, candidates)` for every specialist in the dispatch plan and returns `contextPackets[specialist]` keyed by the `cx-*` name. Each packet is the same shape the context router emits: a role-filtered `relatedArtifacts` list with attached reasons and an `omitted` list naming why each candidate was dropped. The `cx-` prefix is stripped before the `ROLE_POLICIES` lookup. Back-compatible — the field is absent when no `candidates` are supplied. This closes the runtime-wiring gap for the context router: an agent calling `orchestration_policy` now receives the deterministic per-role bundle instead of having to assemble it manually for every handoff.
- Hard binary postconditions on five soft-norm-only producers, turning documented anti-patterns into typed runtime gates. `lib/specialists/postconditions.mjs` carries the rules: `cx-reviewer` must surface at least one finding or an explicit `noIssuesFoundAt: [paths]` / `noIssuesFoundStatement` (no rubber stamps); `cx-security` must have `threatModelUpdatedAt >= contractStart` (no retrofitted threat models); `cx-debugger` must declare `rootCauseConfirmedVia ∈ {reproduction, trace, test}` (no symptom-only fixes); `cx-docs-keeper` must have `crossDocCoherenceCheckRan === true` *and* a non-empty named `coherenceDiff` (no stale-doc PRs); `cx-designer` must have `accessibilityCheckRan === true` (no post-hoc a11y). `enforcePacket(contractId, packet, 'output')` evaluates these rules after the shape check and throws `ContractViolationError` with `verdict: 'BLOCKED_CONTRACT'` on failure — distinguishable from regular `CONTRACT_VIOLATION` so the dispatcher branches on the typed verdict rather than parsing the message. The chain-hashed `~/.cx/contract-violations.jsonl` log gets a `verdict` + `postconditionFailures` field per BLOCKED_CONTRACT record. The `agent_contract` MCP tool now attaches `binaryPostconditions: [{id, description}]` to every returned contract so specialists see the hard checks they're being held to at handoff time, not after the fact.
- Second documentation pass covering the operations and reference surfaces that the first sweep didn't touch: `docs/concepts/knowledge-layout.md` adds an R&D-loop layer to the `.cx/` directory taxonomy (`.cx/inbox/`, `.cx/intake/`, `.cx/task-graphs/`, `.cx/traces/`) and points the inbox section at the triage queue; `docs/concepts/prompt-surfaces.md` describes the triage → context-router stage of dispatch; `docs/reference/hooks.md` updates the `session-start` row to mention the `Pending R&D intake (N)` surface; `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` lists the R&D-loop module surface (classify / queue / graph / context router / broker / worker plane); `docs/cookbook/observability-and-cost.md` documents how R&D-loop events flow to Langfuse and the `.cx/traces/<date>.jsonl` always-on path; `docs/cookbook/index.mdx` updates the inbox + observability entries; `docs/operations/troubleshooting.md` adds a "Schema migration drift" section with the `construct storage repair-migrations --yes` fix; `docs/start/what-next.mdx` adds "pick a deployment mode" and "intake and triage" to the read-once table.
- Documentation sweep aligning every guidance surface with the algorithmic-infrastructure work: README adds a "Signals to R&D" section + concept links; `docs/start/install.mdx` notes solo as default and points at the deployment-model page; `docs/start/first-task.mdx` walks through dropping a file in `.cx/inbox/` → `construct intake show` → `construct graph from-intake`; `docs/concepts/architecture.md` adds an R&D loop mermaid + key invariants for evidence and broker; `docs/concepts/agents-and-personas.mdx` describes the triage + context-router stages of the chain; `docs/concepts/gates-and-enforcement.md` adds a Layer 4 brokered-tool-call gate; `docs/concepts/beads-and-state.md` slots `.cx/intake/`, `.cx/task-graphs/`, and `.cx/traces/` into the durable-state taxonomy; `docs/reference/cli/services.md` documents `construct config`; `docs/reference/cli/work.md` documents `construct intake` and `construct graph`; `docs/reference/config.md` enumerates the deployment-mode, intake-queue, and MCP-broker env vars; `docs/cookbook/use-the-inbox.md` links the inbox to the triage queue; `templates/docs/construct_guide.md` lists the new CLI commands; `skills/operating/orchestration-reference.md` reconciles `.cx/workflow.json` and `.cx/task-graphs/` as siblings. The construct persona prompt gained an "R&D intake surface" section explaining how to read pending packets, generate task graphs, attach evidence, and honor broker `ApprovalRequired` decisions — staying inside the 900-word budget by tightening adjacent sections.
- MCP broker + policy gateway: `lib/policy/engine.mjs` exports `policyDecision({role, project, tool, action, risk})` returning `{allowed, reason, approvalRequired, source}`. Decision precedence is explicit deny > approvalRequired > risk-tier > default. Role permissions read from `specialists/role-manifests.json` (`fence.deniedActions`, `fence.approvalRequired` with `path:**` / `name:**` glob suffix support). High-risk actions need approval for non-autonomous roles; `sre`, `security`, and `release-manager` carry the autonomous exception. `lib/mcp/broker.mjs` wraps tool invocations through `Broker.invoke({role, tool, action, risk, traceId, execute})` — every call emits a `tool.called` trace event tagged with the decision, denied calls throw the typed `PolicyDenied`, approval-required calls throw `ApprovalRequired`, and per-(role, tool) rate limiting throws `RateLimited` after the budget. `isBrokered(env)` defaults off in solo mode, on in team / enterprise mode, with a `CONSTRUCT_MCP_BROKER` env override. Tests pin every branch plus a real-manifest read to catch role-manifest schema drift.
- Retrieval hardening primitives: `lib/storage/fusion.mjs` exports `fuseScores({vector, lexical, metadata, recency}, weights)` for weighted-linear hybrid scoring (default weights vector 0.45, lexical 0.35, metadata 0.15, recency 0.05), `reciprocalRankFusion(rankedLists, {k})` for rank-based fusion robust to mismatched score scales between BM25 and cosine backends, and `recencyScore(timestamp, {halfLifeDays})` for exponential freshness decay. `lib/evals/retrieval-bench.mjs` adds `runRetrievalBench({fixtures, search, thresholds})` with structured metrics (recall@5, precision@5, MRR, p50 / p95 latency, empty-rate, fallback-rate, mustNotInclude assertions) and threshold-driven regression detection so CI can fail on retrieval drift. `formatBenchSummary` renders the result for terminal output. Tests pin the math, the threshold gates, and the rank-based fusion's robustness to mismatched score scales.
- `construct storage repair-migrations --yes` heals drifted `construct_schema_migrations` rows in long-lived dev databases. `runMigrations` accepts `repair: true`; drifted files are re-applied (their `CREATE … IF NOT EXISTS` clauses make this a no-op against the live schema) and the recorded SHA is updated. Destructive drift (`DROP` / `TRUNCATE` / `ALTER … DROP` / `DELETE`) is refused via the new `isIdempotentMigration` safety check — the only path forward for those is a new migration file with a higher sequence number. `construct storage migrations` is the no-side-effects companion that just reports drift. `construct doctor`'s Schema-migrations check now names the fix command when drift is repairable and the "write a new migration" path otherwise. Live-verified against a dev DB with simulated drift on the shipped `001_init.sql` (repaired cleanly) and the shipped `002_pgvector.sql` (correctly refused — it carries a conditional `ALTER … DROP COLUMN` inside a `DO` block).
- **R&D trace events now flow to Langfuse.** `lib/worker/trace.mjs` `emitTraceEvent` previously wrote only to `.cx/traces/<date>.jsonl`. It now also pushes batched ingest payloads to Langfuse via the existing `lib/telemetry/langfuse-ingest.mjs` client whenever `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` + `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` are configured and `CONSTRUCT_TRACE_BACKEND` is unset or `langfuse`. First sighting of a `traceId` emits a `trace-create`; subsequent events on the same trace emit `event-create` observations attached to it. Failures are swallowed — observability never breaks the caller. The local JSONL log remains the always-on path so `CONSTRUCT_TRACE_BACKEND=none` (or absent Langfuse keys) is a graceful no-op. Verified end-to-end against a local Langfuse instance: six R&D-loop events on a single traceId surfaced as a single trace with six observations in the Langfuse UI.
- Worker plane primitives: `lib/worker/run.mjs` (`runJob` executes a bounded command with hard timeout, path-policy denial, restricted env support, and writes stdout/stderr artifacts under `.cx/runtime/worker/<jobId>.{stdout,stderr}.log`), `lib/worker/trace.mjs` (append-only JSONL trace event log under `.cx/traces/<YYYY-MM-DD>.jsonl` with a typed event vocabulary covering intake.received → worker.completed → evidence.recorded, plus traceId/spanId helpers), `lib/worker/evidence.mjs` (`evidenceFromJobResult` derives the canonical record from a job result; `recordEvidence` appends to a task graph node and emits an evidence.recorded trace event; `blockedPacket` / `needsInputPacket` enforce the typed BLOCKED + NEEDS_MAIN_INPUT contracts so workers cannot fail silently). Tests pin the timeout, path-policy denial, trace-id linkage, and the structured contracts. Docker image for cross-machine isolation is a follow-up.
- Task graph as a first-class object. `lib/task-graph/schema.mjs` defines node + edge shapes and validation. `lib/task-graph/generate.mjs` derives a graph from an R&D triage packet — one node per persona in `recommendedChain`, depends_on + handoff_to edges chaining them in order, node `type` selected from `rdStage` (with owner overrides for `qa→verification`, `reviewer→review`, `evaluator→evaluation`, `debugger→diagnosis`, etc.), acceptance criteria seeded per stage, verification requirements attached when the stage warrants. `lib/task-graph/store.mjs` persists graphs to `.cx/task-graphs/<graph-id>.json` (solo mode). New CLI: `construct graph list|show|from-intake|status` to inspect, generate, and update graphs from the command line. Tests cover the bug, experiment, architecture, and eval-finding workflows; the store; node-status updates with evidence; and schema validation including dangling-dependency detection.
- Intake queue IDs gained millisecond precision plus a per-process counter so two enqueues fired in the same millisecond cannot collide. Previously the timestamp truncated at one-second resolution, which raced under load and produced duplicate-primary-key errors in `construct_intake_items` for tight ingest loops.
- Role-aware context router in `lib/context-router.mjs`. `buildContextPacket({request, triage, role, project, budget, candidates, constraints, priorObservations, verificationRequirements})` is deterministic — same input always produces the same packet. `ROLE_POLICIES` maps each persona to a prioritized list of artifact kinds (e.g. product-manager prioritizes user-signal → prd → research-brief; engineer prioritizes target-file → test → interface-contract; sre prioritizes incident → runbook → slo). Each kept artifact carries a `reason` explaining why it was selected; each omitted candidate carries the cause (`avoid list`, `maxArtifacts cap`, `token budget`). Token budget honored via an approximate-tokens count over title + summary + path. Unknown roles fall back to a default policy so the router never silently produces an empty packet. Test coverage cross-checks `ROLE_POLICIES` against the persona names produced by `classifyRdIntake` so adding a new triage class without a router policy is caught.
- `PostgresIntakeQueue` adapter in `lib/intake/postgres-queue.mjs` for team and enterprise deployment modes. Backed by the new `construct_intake_items` table (schema migration `db/schema/003_intake.sql`) with composite indexes on `(status, created_at)`, `(primary_owner, status, created_at)`, and a GIN index on `payload`. Triage fields are flattened into typed columns for efficient filtering; the full packet lives in `payload jsonb`. Adds an atomic `claim({ claimedBy })` method that uses `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` so concurrent workers across processes cannot grab the same pending item. `createIntakeQueue` resolves the postgres backend in team / enterprise mode, pulling the SQL client from `createSqlClient(env)` and scoping queries by `CONSTRUCT_PROJECT_NAME` / `CONSTRUCT_TENANT_ID`. CI's `postgres-integration` job runs the new `tests/intake-postgres-queue.test.mjs` (gated on `DATABASE_URL`) including the concurrent-claim race test.
- `IntakeQueue` interface + `FilesystemIntakeQueue` adapter in `lib/intake/filesystem-queue.mjs`. The six contract methods are `enqueue`, `listPending`, `count`, `read`, `markProcessed`, `markSkipped`, `reopen` — same shape regardless of backend. `createIntakeQueue(rootDir, env)` in `lib/intake/queue.mjs` resolves the backend: `CONSTRUCT_INTAKE_QUEUE_BACKEND=filesystem|postgres` override wins, otherwise solo mode → filesystem and team / enterprise → postgres. The postgres adapter is the next step; until it lands, team / enterprise calls throw a "not yet implemented" error rather than silently falling back. All callers (intake CLI, session-start hook, intake/prepare.mjs) updated to use the factory.
- `construct intake` CLI for inspecting and driving the R&D intake queue. Subcommands: `list` (tabular ID / type / stage / owner / action), `show <id>` (full packet — triage block, recommended chain, related artifacts, excerpt), `done <id> [--notes=…]` (move pending → processed), `skip <id> [--reason=…]` (move pending → skipped with audit trail), `reopen <id>` (processed or skipped → pending, clears completion fields). Operates against the filesystem queue at `.cx/intake/` in solo mode; the same CLI works against the Postgres queue in team / enterprise modes once that adapter lands. New concept page: [`docs/concepts/intake-and-triage.md`](docs/concepts/intake-and-triage.md).
- R&D triage classification on every intake packet via the new `lib/intake/classify.mjs` `classifyRdIntake()` function. Maps a raw inbox signal onto the R&D loop: `intakeType` (bug, user-signal, requirement, research, experiment, eval-finding, architecture, incident, launch-asset, ops, security, legal-compliance, unknown), `rdStage` (signal, framing, hypothesis, research, artifact, design, implementation, evaluation, release, operations, unknown), `primaryOwner` (persona name from `specialists/registry.json`), `recommendedChain` (full handoff sequence), `recommendedAction`, `risk`, `requiresApproval`, `confidence`, `rationale`. Deterministic keyword classifier — no LLM call in the daemon. Tie-breaking prefers higher-stakes classes (security beats research when keywords overlap). `formatTriageLine()` renders the canonical session-start summary line.
- `construct config` command — inspect and set the deployment posture. `construct config` shows the active mode and resource topology; `construct config mode` prints the active mode; `construct config mode [solo|team|enterprise]` persists a new mode to `~/.construct/config.env`. The three modes select the backends for the intake queue, memory, telemetry, workers, and policy: solo runs everything locally with filesystem queue and optional Postgres/Docker; team promotes the queue to Postgres with row-locked claims, runs workers in Docker, brokers MCP, and centralizes telemetry; enterprise adds tenant isolation, signed MCP allowlists, isolated worker containers, and mandatory audit. Default is solo when unset. `construct status` shows the active mode and topology inline at the top of its output. New module `lib/deployment-mode.mjs` is the single source for mode validation and topology resolution.
- New docs concept page: [`docs/concepts/deployment-model.md`](docs/concepts/deployment-model.md) explains the three modes, the capability matrix, and degraded-mode guarantees. The previous concept page covering offline operation has been folded into this one.

### Changed

- Folder renamed: `.cx/review-queue/` → `.cx/intake/`. The unreleased "Proactive intake review pipeline" entry below now refers to it as the R&D intake queue. Modules moved correspondingly: `lib/review/queue.mjs` → `lib/intake/queue.mjs`, `lib/review/prepare.mjs` → `lib/intake/prepare.mjs`. Symbols: `enqueueReview` → `enqueueIntake`, `prepareReviewForIngestedFile` → `prepareIntakeForIngestedFile`. The `CONSTRUCT_DEBUG_REVIEW` env var becomes `CONSTRUCT_DEBUG_INTAKE`. Session-start surfaces the triage line per packet instead of the older lane-only display.
- Product framing: Construct is now described as a deployable AI R&D operating system that runs locally for solo users or is deployed centrally for shared team usage. README tagline, docs homepage, install description, architecture, beads-and-state, and getting-started copy reframed to match. The site nav, search corpus, and Homebrew formula description follow.
- LLM intent-verification overlay (PR #38) demoted from inline gate to fire-and-forget telemetry. `routeRequestVerified()` is now synchronous: it returns the keyword classifier's verdict immediately and schedules the LLM verifier in the background; the verifier appends a `{ specialist, flavor, keywordVerdict, llmVerdict, agreed, confidence, reason, latencyMs }` record to `~/.cx/intent-verifications.jsonl` for offline analysis. Removes one LLM round-trip per non-cached dispatch (was up to 3 s on a miss); the field-aligned pattern is description-driven self-dispatch with measurement, not inline LLM gating (Anthropic Skills, OpenAI Swarm handoffs, Cognition's June 2025 essay). Disable the background call entirely with `CONSTRUCT_INTENT_VERIFY=off`.
- Agent roster generation in `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` now prefers a new optional `when_to_use` field over `description` when present. Mirrors the Anthropic Skills progressive-disclosure pattern: name + routing-optimized trigger preloaded as the roster, body loaded only on dispatch. Existing personas without `when_to_use` fall through to `description` so this is a non-breaking addition.
- Comment-policy sweep across `lib/` — 50 pre-existing JSDoc violations (caller references, narrative voice, point-in-time / comparative-history phrasing) rewritten to current-tense descriptive form. The linter was extended in PR #43 to scan `*` lines in block comments but the sweep was deferred; this completes it. `node bin/construct lint:comments` is now clean (0 errors, 0 warnings).
- `construct setup` now auto-installs both local Postgres and local Langfuse via Docker, with interactive default-yes prompts that pattern-match Supabase CLI and Hasura's local-first install flows.
